# Fretwise

Guitar music-theory, ear-training, rhythm and microphone trainer for an aspiring jazz guitarist (Berklee-style curriculum). Single-page app, no build step for development, no framework. One vendored dependency (user-approved): Spotify Basic Pitch + TensorFlow.js in `vendor/` for guitar note detection.

- **Live site (the one page the user uses):** https://ealkan23.github.io/fretwise/ — GitHub Pages from the public repo `ealkan23/fretwise`. `.github/workflows/pages.yml` publishes `src/fretwise.html` as `index.html` with `piano/` beside it on every push to `main`, so **deploying = commit + `git push`** (watch with `gh run watch`). Mic works there; progress is in that browser's `localStorage` key `fretwise-v1`. The user works only from Claude Code: never send them to a claude.ai chat to publish.
- **Old claude.ai artifact (retired):** https://claude.ai/artifact/756Z4Kc3AywDU1mgsGazsQ — no longer updated. `initCloud` only runs when framed with `window.claude`; elsewhere it shows "Saved in this browser". Progress moves between copies with Overview → "Move your progress" (export/import JSON; per game the copy with more answers wins).
- **Offline file:** `dist/fretwise.html` (built by `build.py`, piano embedded, git-ignored). Open from disk in Chrome.
- **Ideas talked about and not built:** `IDEAS.md` (from 26 Nov 2026, at the user's request). **Whenever a feature or change is discussed and not built, add it there in the same turn**; when one is built, move it to its “Built since” section. The full description of goal R that an outside review was made from is `docs/goal-R-read-at-sight.md`.
- **Pedagogical review** that drove the roadmap: https://claude.ai/artifact/7emdzZtQ8s6Eb2FDthZEUL

## YouTube Notes for Scale Runner (27 Nov 2026, user brief: “YouTube Notes · 37 lessons” beside “Open the full notes”; the brief is kept in `.claude/ytg/brief.md`)
The user's explicit request overrides “YouTube transcripts are planning material” for this one place (that section and the memory note say so).
- **What**: Scale Runner's lesson card has a second button, “YouTube Notes · 37 lessons”, opening view `ytgame` (`vYTG`, `ytgBody`, cached in `YTG_HTML`): the notes page's look, a lesson list that jumps (`notesec`), one section per video: the title as a link to YouTube (new tab), channel, length, level, the lesson as textbook items (`noteItem`), “Unclear in the video”, “In the video” (3 to 8 timestamp links). No embedded player. The button appears only on games with entries. The highlighter works there (`hlKey()`: highlights kept under `STORE.hl['yt:'+gid]`; `hlBar` now draws the bar for both pages).
- **The 37 videos**: every video with `"game":"scales"` and no `skip` in `videos/jenslarsen/_categories.json` (29) and `videos/marbin/_categories.json` (8). None left out. Page order (`ORDER`, and `parts` = six headings drawn in the lesson list and above each part's first lesson; reordered 28 Nov 2026 at the user's question, the first order put four notes per string third and triads after sevenths): 1 how to learn a scale and where it lies (4) · 2 inside the major scale: chords, arpeggios, pentatonics (6) · 3 from exercise to line: triads, then the Barry Harris seventh-chord work, then its variations (12) · 4 beyond major: minor scales, altered, symmetric, new pentatonics (8) · 5 through tunes and progressions (4) · 6 speed and wider fingerings: groups of six, four notes per string, fusion (3). Each lesson after what it assumes; a remake straight after its original. Highlights are found by lesson number: reordering again orphans any made on the page. Levels: 10 beginner, 24 intermediate, 3 advanced.
- **Code**: block `/*YTG_BEGIN*/ … /*YTG_END*/` right after `/*YT_NOTES_END*/` (after the music core and the generators, before VIEWS). `YT_GAME[gid] = {title, intro, entries:[{vid, ch, title, len, level, p, unclear, refs}]}`, `YT_CH`. **`YTF`** = the figure helpers, every fret computed: `pos` (a Leavitt position by `scPos`, `o.sc`/`o.home`/`o.lab`), `inpos` (pitches placed where a position plays them), `chordIn`, `nps` (N notes a string from a pitch), `at` ('E3@6': a pitch on a string, the fret follows), `stf` (NF.staff with sounding octaves, wraps after four bars, `o.per`), `seq` (a line with rhythm on the score engine: beams, triplet brackets), `pmids` (the game's own `scPattern` from the lowest tonic), `oct`, `names`, `INF` (the “reconstructed” tag). The entries sit in the block one `{ }` scope per writer's file (some files keep small local helpers: G1F/G1L, NN, dg…). To remove it all: the block, `vYTG`/`ytgBody`/`hlBar`'s callers, the lessonCard button, the `ytgame` lines in `render` and the click handler.
- **How it was made**: six digest agents read every transcript whole (`videos/<channel>/digest_scales/<id>.md`, git-ignored), six other agents verified them (`<id>.verify.md`: coverage, every note and fret recomputed, reconstructions judged), six writers wrote the entries from digest + verification (scratch in `.claude/ytg/entries_*.js`), checked by `.claude/ytg/check.js`; two proof-readers read the page text (`.claude/ytg/page.txt`) against the sources: 4 errors, fixed.
- **Numbers**: about 29,600 words, 52 figures and 53 tables, 58 “reconstructed” tags, 140 unclear points. Most of what the teachers play is behind “[music]” in the captions: a line is written out only where what is said fixes it, else it is listed as unclear.
- **Decisions**: each video its own complete entry, also the two remakes (“The Most Important Scale For Jazz” and its remake, the two triad-exercise videos), with one sentence naming the other; a position or register chosen for a figure is said in its caption and tagged; “C major in the 8th position” is drawn both ways (VII and VIII, or VII and IX) where the video does not settle it; his “finger 2 at fret N” is Leavitt position N−1, and his seven positions are not claimed to equal the game's sheet; the four “Uncommon Pentatonics” shorts (Danny Markovitch, Marbin's saxophonist) are short entries with computed tables, no invented position; their middle timestamps are estimates (the captions stamp only 0:00 and 0:30); “Fretboard Challenge! 12 Licks” is music only until 6:14, so its entry is the spoken advice only.
- **Checks**: sweep block `/*YTGSW_BEGIN*/ … /*YTGSW_END*/` (each entry renders, title, channel, level, id and length match `_list.tsv` when the folder is present, 3–8 refs rising inside the video, no maj7, no lower-case numerals, no em dash, every neck titled, NF.err empty; proved by breaking a ref time and adding “maj7”: both caught); sweep `bad 0` twice; `pages_check` renders the page (all ok); `tests/ytg_shots.py` (page at 1440 and 1000, every section, and every figure on contact sheets `tests/out/ytg-scales-<w>-figs*.png`: no figure error, nothing wider than its frame, no cut title; all 105 figures and tables looked at). Two helper faults found and fixed on the way: `stf` raised the number in a chord symbol (G7 drawn G8), `seq` drew triplets without brackets.
- **Not verified**: nothing was played on a guitar; the reconstructions are reasoned from what is said, not checked against the videos' pictures; Scale Runner's levels, keys, grading, saved data and `LESSON_NOTES.scales` are untouched.

## Scale Runner on the user's seven-shape sheet (27 Nov 2026, user: a D-major run in position III started on finger 3, “are you sure these are in Leavitt's method?”; then the sheet, and “use the sheet's seven shapes… YES. Go”)
- **The sheet** (the one set aside on 12 Nov as having wrong notes; read note by note on 27 Nov, every shape is right and equals the app's fingering except one note, below): a key's shapes are named by where the tonic lies: on string 6 under finger 1 (by a first-finger stretch), 2 or 4; on string 5 under finger 1, 2 or 4; on string 4 under finger 1 (by a stretch). In Leavitt's terms types 1A, 2, 4, 4B, 1, 3, 1C. **No shape puts the tonic under finger 3** (types 1D and 4D only come from forcing all twelve keys into one position).
- **`scSheetPos(keyPc)`** = the key's seven positions (II to XIII; C: II, III, V, VII, IX, XI, XII). **`scPosList(keyPc, pos)`**: where a level asks the basic fingerings (`SC_BASIC` or nothing) the five basic types of that key; `SC_SEVEN` and `SC_ALL` → the seven; an explicit list keeps its sheet positions. `scQ` uses it for every level; only “Twelve keys in one position” (`o.cycle`) goes outside it. `scModeQ` (Modes in every position) draws from the parent key's seven. `scOctQ` (one octave from any root) keeps its mix of root strings and fingers but only in a sheet position; a root under finger 1 may be reached by the first-finger stretch (the position one fret higher). **Position VIII (type 4C in C) and X (4A) are no longer asked** (VIII had been the teacher's on 18 Nov; the user did not keep it when asked). The constants `SC_SEVEN` and the weights (`scPickPos`, `scHomeFinger`) are unchanged.
- **The one note the sheet writes differently**: in type 4B (C in position III) the 2nd (D) is played on string 3 at fret 7 by the fourth-finger stretch, not on string 2 at fret 3. `scPos(keyPc, P, pcs, sheet)` does that only with `sheet = true` (Scale Runner's calls and its notes); Reading and Sight Reading in position III keep D under the first finger on string 2.
- Texts: the descriptions of levels 1, 2, 9, 12, 13, 19, the level notes (seven shapes; IX and III as the first-finger shapes; XI in place of VIII), the full notes' chapter “C major in five places, then seven” (a computed table of the seven shapes in C, F and G). Item keys carry the type, so items of 4A and 4C stop coming back; their records stay.
- Sweep block `/*SHEET_BEGIN*/ … /*SHEET_END*/` (every key: seven positions, types 1 1A 1C 2 3 4 4B, no tonic under finger 3 on strings 6–4; C = II III V VII IX XI XII; the 4B note for Scale Runner and not for Reading; 40 questions of every generator but level 15 never show types 1B 1D 4A 4C 4D), proved by breaking `scPosList`; the older octave-form check allows the first-finger stretch. `tests/john_player.py scales:1 5 6 7 13 16` pass.

## Reading Rhythm: the three switching levels made one (27 Nov 2026, user: “no need for this much baby sitting… 16, 17, 18 combined, a starter for grid changes, challenging enough but not hard; 19 no change; 20 stays”; 33 → 31 levels; snapshot: tag `v-2026-11-27-before-grid-merge` (local), store copy `backups/store-before-grid-merge-2026-11-27.json`). **Supersedes the level numbers of the section below.**
- **16 Switching grids** (`cGridMix(RRG)`): each line of two bars draws how it changes: one grid held for the line (35 %), a new grid on beat 3 (40 %, the halves different), or beat by beat (25 %; there the next beat keeps its grid half the time); a line never starts on the grid the last ended on; no rests, ties or dots; start 56. **17 Rests and dots inside the grids** (old 19, unchanged: `cGrid(4,RRG4)`). **18 Everything so far, in 4/4** (old 20, unchanged). Old 21–33 → 19–31. `cGrid` is kept for level 17.
- **`REORDERS32`** (flag `r58`, after `r57`): old 16, 17, 18 → 16 (merged: their windows concatenated, counts summed by `migrateReorder`), 19 → 17, 20 → 18, 21–33 → 19–31; the frontier put back on the level it was; a merged level stays out of skip when any part of it was passed. **On the user's store** (none of the merged levels played): frontier 21 → **19 “6/8, with sixteenths”**, skip [7, 9, 10, 16, 17, 18], the level page opens on 16, everything else as before, other games byte-identical, second load a no-op (`tests/grid_migtest.py` now tests r58: made-up players on old 21, 18, 20 and 30 land on 19, 16, 18 and 28). A player whose frontier was inside the merged levels may see 16 as passed by its own merged record.
- **Goal R**: stage 3 `rhyread 13–21`, stage 4 `22–23`, stage 5 `24`, appendix `25–31`; `GOAL_WHY` keys `rhyread:22`, `rhyread:24`. Required levels in R: 107 → 105.
- Sweep (block RBB): the new order by name, 31 levels, goal ranges; level 16 over 400 bars: no rest, tie or dot, bars fill 4/4, no figure crosses a beat, all three ways of changing occur, a line never starts on the last line's grid; level 17 holds its grid for a line; `REORDERS32`. Proved by breaking the generator (only held lines: caught). Levels 16–19 started headless (`tests/out/grid-L*.png`).

## The reading build: real sight reading (26 Nov 2026, brief `.claude/reading-brief.md`; snapshot: tag `v-2026-11-26-before-reading-build` (local), store copy `backups/store-before-reading-build-2026-11-26.json`)
**Re-read this section and the brief before each phase.** The user's answers to the brief's five choices: pass mark 90 %; scan 2 s a written bar, 4 to 30; Reading Rhythm reordered; Chart Reading a new game with Joe; four bars from Sight Reading 3 (about two in three), eight bars from 13 (about half). Phases A → D, each checked, committed, pushed and published before the next. **Nothing in this build was played on a real guitar**: synthetic takes through the live listening path stand in for it.
### Phase A: a long take is graded by its share; a scan before the count-in, done
- **The share rule** (`shareJudge(slots, extraN, o)`, `shareTxt`, just before `micGradeOne`): a Sight Reading question with **16 slots or more** carries `q.share = {min:.9, stop:4, from:16, len}` (`srShareSet`, called by `srMake` and `srPageMake`; `len` = each target's written length in clicks, ties summed, rests not counted, from `scoreData`). In `micGradeOne`, after the per-slot results: a slot is good when heard at the written pitch (the low-string octave allowance included) **and** within a quarter of a beat; the take passes when good ÷ (slots + extra notes) ≥ 90 % **and there is no stop**. **A stop** = a run of slots in a row with no good one whose written notes last **more than 4 clicks** (one bar of quarters lost passes, two bars fail; rests between them do not count). Extra notes (after the existing noise rules) each count as one bad slot. `q.tol` and `rhyStrict` no longer decide such a take (a note half a beat off is just not good). Shorter lines keep exactly the rule they had (a long line under 16 slots keeps `tol`). The result carries `r.share = {ok, good, total, pct, min, late, wrong, miss, extra, stop:{at, bar, beats}, n}`; `micFeedbackExtra` writes “47 of 52 notes right and in time (90 %)… Lost: … You stopped in bar 6…”. `perfLog` stores `e.sh` (the share, 0–1) in the log entry, archived by `LOGDB` with it. `STD.sightread.acc` unchanged.
- **Matching by time for share takes**: the note matcher (`micGradeOne`'s edit-distance alignment) ignored time; with one bar skipped in a line that repeats a figure it slid every later note onto an earlier slot (all of them “late”). For `q.share` questions only (`shareT`), a match costs up to 0.3 more the further it lies from its own beat, so among alignments of equal cost the one in time wins. Every other question is matched exactly as before.
- **The scan** (`scanSecs`, `scanStart`, `scanHTML`, before `micListen`): `q.scan` = 2 s a written bar, 4 to 30 (`srMake` unless free start; `srPageMake` always: a road map counts its written bars). `micListen` runs the scan first (`S.scanning`, `S.scanEnd`, `S.scanQ` = the question whose scan is done); at its end the count-in starts by itself; Space or the button (“▶ Count in now”) during the scan starts it at once. `nextQ` starts it **whether “Listen as soon as…” is on or off** (the level page says so under that option for Sight Reading and Chart Reading). Shown in the count-in box (`.cin.scan`, so on a page of 8 bars and more it sits fixed at the top). Nothing listens during the scan, so `rt`, the round clock (mic questions are untimed) and listening start at the count-in as before; the idle rule of Endless counts the scan as activity. Level 1 (free start) has no scan.
- Texts: the level-24 description and note, the line prompts (`SR_SHARE_TXT` appended by `srMake`/`srPageMake` when the share rule applies; the old “one note in sixteen…” only on long lines under 16 slots), the lesson card (the first rule is the scan, a rule line for the share), “How to read” (“Before the first note: the scan”, what to look at in order), “How the game listens”.
- Checks: sweep block `/*RBA_BEGIN*/ … /*RBA_END*/` (52 slots: 5 wrong pass, 6 fail; 5 a beat late pass, 6 fail; 4 wrong + 1 extra pass, + 2 extra fail; 100 slots: 8 quarters unplayed = a stop in bar 11 though 92 % holds, 4 unplayed pass, 5 wrong in a row fail; 8 eighths (4 beats) are not a stop; 15 slots: one wrong note fails; the feedback numbers; scan times; Scale Runner, Along the Neck and Tunes by Ear carry no share or scan) and in the `SIGHTREAD` block (every generator: a question of 16 slots or more has the share rule, `al` wrong notes spread out pass and one more fails; the scan time per written bars; no scan on free start). Each check proved by breaking the code once (pass mark −3 %, no stop rule, no time in the matching, extra notes not counted). `tests/scan_check.py` (headless, muted): the scan lasts 4.4–4.5 s (2 bars), 8.4 s (4), 30.3–30.5 s (16, 32) from its start to the count-in, auto on and off (the rest is the microphone opening); it begins 0.2 s after the question; Space after 1 s starts the count-in at 1.6 s; level 1 has none. `tests/sr_player.py` / `tests/john_player.py` wait for the scan and skip it with `micListen()`, and print the share. `tests/sr_player.py 3 25 27 28`: level 3 (9 notes, strict rule) passes; 8 bars (50 notes, 100 %), 16 bars (63 notes, 100 %) and the road map (42 notes, 100 %) pass; the road map read straight through fails (50 %, a stop).
- **Not verified on a guitar**: the share and the stop on a real take; whether the matcher's time preference ever picks a worse alignment on a real, uneven take.

### Phase B: the goal's required levels, done
- **Reading Rhythm reordered** (`REORDERS30`, flag `r56`, its block right after `r54`'s in `G()`): 18 Cut time (old 19) · 19 Everything so far (20) · 20 Broken triplets (21) · 21 Everything (25) · 22 Road map, played (26) · 23 12/8 (18) · 24 Sixteenth-note triplets (22) · 25 5/4 and 3/8 (23) · 26 Changing meters (24); 1–17 and 27–29 unchanged. The level list and `LEVEL_NOTES.rhyread` were reordered in the source; the note of “Everything” rewritten (it said “The same with…”, meaning the level before). “Everything so far” and “Everything” keep their content: no 12/8, sixteenth-note triplet, 5/4, 3/8 or changing-meter bar, and “Everything” has no broken triplets although they now come before it (the brief: leave it as it is). No other text named these levels by number.
- **The frontier rule of r56** (a reshuffle, not an insert, so `migrateReorder`'s “highest contiguous open level” would be wrong: a player on old 18 would land on 17, which he had passed): every level under the old frontier keeps its standing under its new number, the new frontier is the first level, in the new order, that was not under the old one; old `skip` levels stay open, not passed; reached levels that now stand above the frontier stay open (`g.unl`) and **count as passed by their own record** (`lvPassed`), as in every earlier reorder. `opts.bpmL`, `altN`, `unl0` remapped first, then `migrateReorder` moves the data.
- **On the user's store**: Reading Rhythm 5,393 answers / 4,544 clean, 28,962,314 ms, 300 log entries, 1,227 history points, 80 rounds, 15 days before and after, every per-level count, recent window, log entry, history point, round and tempo on its new number; frontier **17 “6/8, with sixteenths” before and after**, skip [7, 9, 10, 16] unchanged, nothing above 17 passed or open; every other game byte-identical, no top-level key changed, second load a no-op. Made-up players (`tests/read_migtest.py`): old 18 → 18 “Cut time” with 23 (12/8) open; old 21 with old 18 in skip → 20 “Broken triplets”; old 25 with 26 open → 21 “Everything”, 22 open, 23–26 passed above it; old 26 with old 22 in skip → 22 “Road map, played”, 24 open not passed, the others passed. In each: every level that was passed still passed, every open level still open.
- **Goal R**: stage 2 loses `rhydict 1–4`; stage 3 `rhyread 13–19`; stage 4 `rhyread 20–21` (text: “the last figures tapped: broken triplets, then everything at once”); stage 5 `rhyread 22`; appendix `rhydict 1–16` and `rhyread 23–29` (“12/8, sixteenth-note triplets, 5/4 and 3/8, changing meters, and written accents…”). `GOAL_WHY.R`: `rhyread:13` rewritten, `rhyread:21` → `rhyread:20` (new text), `rhyread:26` → `rhyread:22`, `rhydict:1` removed from R (B and D keep theirs). Goals B, C and D name `rhyread` 1–12 only: untouched. **Levels required by goal R: 101 → 93**; by some goal: 367 → 363.
- Sweep block `/*RBB_BEGIN*/ … /*RBB_END*/` (the new order by name, `REORDERS30` a permutation, goal R's rhythm steps and appendix, no Rhythm Dictation required by R, B C D under 13, levels 19 and 21 never ask a moved meter). Sweep `bad 0` twice, `pages_check` all ok.

### Phase C: longer lines early, done (no level added, removed or renumbered)
- **`pickGen(gs)`** (before `pickQ`, used by it in both places): a level's generator is drawn with even odds unless the generators carry a weight `gen.w`. Only Sight Reading 3–23 have weights.
- **`srLen(twos, fours, sh)`** (with `srAt`, `srTieN`, `SR_L3`, `SR_L13`, before `SR_SYNC`): a level's existing two-bar generators are kept as they are (item keys from Reading included), its existing four-bar ones too, and for every two-bar setting a four-bar (and from 13 an eight-bar) version is made with the same settings: `bars`, `long`, ties scaled 1 → 2 → 3, an even line of quarters filling the bars (`len` = bars × the bar). Weights: **levels 3–12 a third two bars, two thirds four; 13–23 a sixth two bars, a third four, a half eight** (sweep over 600 draws a level: 0.55–0.78 four bars, resp. 0.40–0.60 eight and 0.24–0.43 four). Keys of long lines carry `4b-` / `8b-` (`sr-…`), so no two-bar key is touched. **Level 21**: `srShiftQ` takes `o.bars` (a longer walk, 3,000 tries; key `sr-sh-<from>-<to>-<N>b-…`) and the level has the three shift pairs at 2, 4 and 8 bars with the same weights; one shift in every line.
- **Decision**: in 13–23 the brief keeps a two-bar introduction only where the level brings a new figure (15, 17, 18, 21, 22, 23); here every level from 13 keeps its existing two-bar generators at a sixth of the draws, so their items (some with history from Reading) still come back. Levels 1, 2, 24 and 25 unchanged in content; 24 and 25 say in their description that the mix is what is new.
- **The count-in box and the scan sit fixed at the top for every question with a scan** (`html.srpage` when `q.scan`; it was pages of eight bars only): on a two- or four-bar line the box was under the neck, out of sight. Eight-bar lines of sixteenths (level 17) take two systems and end at 651 px of 820 (`tests/sr_len_shots.py`: one four- and one eight-bar question of 3, 6, 10, 13, 17, 18, 22, screenshots `tests/out/srlen-*.png`, none scrolls).
- Texts: the descriptions of 3–23 (“Two lines in three are four bars long…” / “Half the lines are eight bars…”), their tempo reasons (“take the tempo you can hold to the last bar”), a line in each level note (3–6 also: a four-bar line under 16 notes keeps the strict rule), 24 and 25 rewritten, the lesson card (“read a bar ahead… come back in on beat 1”), “What sight reading is” (why the lines are long early), “How to read” (two items); a share line's prompt drops “Keep going through a wrong note”. **Level 29's tempo reason** no longer says the eighths stop swinging under 80: the page is long, start where you can hold it (100); over 120 the microphone stops separating swung eighths.
- Checks: sweep `bad 0` twice (the `SIGHTREAD` block runs every new generator 40 times: `scoreCheck`, the page read back, the position, no pitch twice, perfect passes, the share rule's wrong notes; new block `/*RBC_BEGIN*/ … /*RBC_END*/`: proportions, no A B A B A, a natural only where the level has one, keys name their bars, share follows the slots, no take over 90 s; proved by breaking the weights and the key's bar mark), `pages_check` all ok, `tests/sr_start.py` (now on the newest store copy) all 29 levels without a page error, `tests/sr_player.py 3:4 13:8 17:8 18:8` (new: `level:bars` forces a length and adds a bad take, every fifth note a whole step off): perfect takes pass (18, 34, 68 and 31 notes at 100 %), bad takes fail (strict rule on 15 notes; 78 %, 70 %, 79 %).
- **Not verified on a guitar**: whether two in three four-bar lines from level 3 is the right size of step; eight bars of sixteenths at 50 on a real guitar.

### Phase D: Chart Reading (`chartread`), a new game of chord charts, done (43 games)
- **The game**: cat `voicings` (Joe), `mic`, guitar only, in `GAMES` right after Guide-Tone Voice Leading; `COURSE` 201, `BADGE` life/core, `STYLE` jazz, `SHORTN`, `STD` {acc .9, ab 35} (Comping's timing), `GSTD`, `LESSONS`, `LOBBY_NOTES`, `LEVEL_NOTES` (ten: full notes for 1, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 10), `LESSON_NOTES` (nine chapters: what a chord chart is · slashes, diamonds and written rhythms · reading the symbol, choosing the grip · a bar ahead, coming back in · the Charleston and the push · the blues and eight bars · the road map with chords · how the game listens · common faults; every chart drawn by the game's drawer, every table computed); not in `PLAY_GAMES`/`HAT_GAMES`. Its questions carry `strumSeq` themselves, so the stored “Strum chords” option cannot turn them into arpeggios. The per-level tempo row and `tempoAdvice` serve it as they serve Sight Reading (`tp` per level, 50–76, start 60–66; `o.lv` stamped after `GBY`).
- **Code**: block `/*CC_BEGIN*/ … /*CC_END*/` just before `const GAMES`. Material `CCM` (II–V–I, turnarounds, two-chord bars, backdoor, subV7, the minor cadence and turnaround, the cycle of the key, the minor cycle), `CC_BLUES` (basic, quick change, both with a turnaround, minor), `E8` spans (`ccPickE8`), `CC_ROAD` (three sixteen-bar charts in the `CHARTS` form, a repeat with 1st and 2nd endings, played order by `chartOrder`). Figures `CCF` (bar-relative events, `c` = chord 0/1 or `'n'` = the next bar's chord pushed on the “and” of 4; a push is never written at a line's end), `CC_RHY` (which figures each kind of level draws), `ccChart` (spelling by `e7Sym`/`anChordOf`, chord symbols over the strum where the chord begins, the one-bar repeat sign `mr1` where a bar repeats the one before), `ccDraw` (four bars a line on `rhySVG`), `ccGrips` (shells near each other, drop-2 where there is no shell), `ccQ` (strums in the played order, `strumAltSeq` = `chordVoicings`, `q.scan`, `q.cc`), `ccLv` (generator 1 in C F G B♭ / A D E G minor, generator 2 in all twelve by `rootPick`). Item keys `cc-<kind>-<progression and window>-<figure codes>`, no key name.
- **Ladder**: 1 One chord a bar (whole-note diamonds) · 2 Two chords a bar · 3 Four to the bar (slashes) · 4 The Charleston · 5 Pushes (“and” of 4 tied over, “and” of 2) · 6 Rests and hits · 7 The minor cadence and the blues (twelve bars of slashes) · 8 Eight bars of a standard's changes · 9 Eight bars with a written rhythm and the one-bar repeat sign · 10 A chart with a road map (16 written bars, 22–23 played).
- **`rhySVG` options** (no existing caller changes): `slash` (slanted heads, hollow diamonds for halves and wholes), `noStem`, `chords` (symbols above; the drawing moves down 18 units), `barOk` (shaded bars), an event's `mk` colours its head (`.cc-ok/late/bad/miss`).
- **Grading**: under 8 strums `strumGrade`'s old rule (in order, one wrong strum fails, three in four on time). **From 8 strums `q.share`**: each heard strum goes to the written strum nearest in time (within half a beat; strums are a beat or more apart), good = the right chord within `STRUM_TOL`, then `shareJudge` (90 %, no stop of more than four beats; extra strums count). 16 strums: one wrong chord passes, two fail.
- **How close two strums can be** (`tests/strum_gap.py`, synthetic rolled strums through the live path): with strings 12 to 60 ms apart, strums a beat, ¾ and ½ beat apart at 60 and 76 all pass (down to 395 ms); a quarter beat at 60 (250 ms) fails (`CHO.dist` 300 ms). **A slow roll (100 ms between strings) is not heard as a strum at any spacing**: the onset is spread too thin for the jump test. The figures keep every strum at least a beat apart and the tempo at 76 at most; the prompts and notes say “strum, do not roll”. The slow-roll limit applies to every strummed level, not only this game.
- **Two faults found on the way, both older than this build and both fixed** (they affect every strummed take, and the second any take):
  - `chordStrums` placed the model's frames at 256 samples each. Basic Pitch keeps 142 frames of each 36,164-sample window step, so its frames run about 0.5 % fast; the library's own note times correct for it, `chordStrums` did not. The loudness it read drifted behind the onsets (4 frames at 4 s, 13 at 24 s) and after about 20 s the jump test dropped real strums: **every strummed take longer than about 20 s lost its last strums** (Repertoire's comped blues chorus among them). Now `bpFrameSec(f)` (the library's formula) places the frames; strum offsets came down from 60–90 ms to about 30 ms on the synthetic takes. Checked: `tests/chord_live.py` ALL PASS; the user's fix-list chords #91, #93, #94, #95 graded straight from their recordings (`tests/chord_direct.py`, new) all right.
  - For about one take length in twenty (26.9 s, 52.9 s, 79.0 s… at 22,050 Hz) tensorflow.js builds a WebGL shader that does not compile (“'getT1': no matching overloaded function”): the analysis threw and the take read **“Nothing heard”**, strummed or not. `bpEval` (used by `bpRaw` and `bpAnalyse`) retries such a take padded with silence to a whole number of window steps; every failing length tested passes padded. A take that analyses today is analysed exactly as before (`tests/live_takes.py bp`: 3 errors, as before).
- **Goals**: R gains the element `chart` (“Chord charts”), stage 4 `chartread 1–5`, stage 5 `chartread 6–10`, `GOAL_WHY` `chartread:1` and `chartread:6`, the end text “…and comp a chord chart at sight in time”; D's appendix `chartread 1–10`. **Levels required by goal R: 101 before the build → 93 after phase B → 103 now.**
- **Checks**: sweep `bad 0` twice; block `/*RCC_BEGIN*/ … /*RCC_END*/` (ten levels, registries, goals; 40 charts a level: bars filled with a push's spill, no push across a line, every chord parses and has a grip, the grid = the written strums a beat apart, grips line up, no key name in the item key, the perfect take passes, under 8 strums one wrong chord fails, from 8 the allowance passes and one more fails, every strum half a beat late fails, a road map played straight through fails; 12 items or more a level in 300 draws; Shells has no share rule), each check proved by breaking the code (no share rule, a push across a line, the key in the item key, no time-matching). `pages_check` all ok. `tests/john_player.py chartread:L` and `chartread:L:first` (new `:first` mode: the first grip strummed for every chord) for all ten levels: every right take passes (the blues at 96 %, the road map at 96 %), every first-grip take fails. `tests/cc_shots.py` (new): every level started on the store copy without a page error, the chart during the scan and the answer page with marks and grips (`tests/out/cc-L*-q.png`, `-a.png`), looked at.
- **Decisions**: a chord symbol stands over the strum where the chord begins (over the push when pushed); half-note diamonds have no stems on the slash levels; level 7 draws the minor cadence a third of the time and the blues two thirds; level 10's rhythm is diamonds, the Charleston or rests and hits; the clef and time signature are on every line (as `rhySVG` draws them); the extra strums the headless audio pipe sometimes produces (14–17 dB under the real strums, mid-ring) are not filtered, so an occasional synthetic run fails on them.
- **Not verified**: a real strum on a real guitar in time (rolled chords, a room, the low roots under C3, which are not checked); whether ten levels are the right size of step. The first real test is the user's.

## Tap matching in the continuous rhythm games: one missed note no longer shifts the taps after it (26 Nov 2026, user: Reading Rhythm 16 “Everything so far, in 4/4” at 60 bpm, 33 %, ±70 ms, “a big bug with the playing”)
`grooveMatch` looked for the nearest note to a tap among the recent notes but **stopped at the first note already answered** (`if(o.hit!==null||o.t>t+tol)break`); since the recent notes always start with answered ones, the nearest-note search never ran and a tap went to the first unanswered note within reach (the fallback loop). The window is a quarter of a beat (250 ms at 60), a whole sixteenth: after one missed sixteenth, a tap that came slightly early for the next note was credited to the missed one, a sixteenth late, and every tap after it the same (offsets +230 ms, bars broken one after another). Now an answered note is skipped and the search goes on. Affects every continuous game (Pulse & Feel, Reading Rhythm, Jazz Rhythm, Comping, Two Hands, Aksak), taps and strums alike; it showed most in dense bars. Sweep block `/*GMSW_BEGIN*/` (sixteenths at 60, one skipped, taps 20 ms early: the old code gave −20 ×5 then +230 ×10; proved by running it on the old code). A simulated player through the live page on Reading Rhythm 16 (headless, muted): misses only the notes it skipped, no offset over 60 ms; without skips the clean run grows a bar every four seconds. Stored blocks played before the fix are left as they are.

## Rhythm Dictation (`rhydict`): the dictation levels became a game of their own (25 Nov 2026, brief `.claude/dictation-brief.md`; snapshot: tag `v-2026-11-25-before-dictation` (local), store copy `backups/store-before-dictation-2026-11-25.json`)
The same operation as the Sight Reading move (phase 3), smaller. **Rhythm Theory & Dictation is now Rhythm Theory (8 levels)**, and its four dictation levels with their answers are in **Rhythm Dictation** (`rhydict`, cat `rhythm`, Bongi, screen only, placed right after Rhythm Theory in `GAMES`; `COURSE` 201, `BADGE` life/core, `STD` accuracy 0.9 with no speed measure (dictation questions carry `audio`, so `fluTarget` gave them none before either; not in `FLU_GAMES`), `SHORTN`, `LESSONS`, `LOBBY_NOTES`, `LESSON_NOTES`, `LEVEL_NOTES`; no `STYLE`). Code: block `/*RD_BEGIN*/ … /*RD_END*/` (after `tapReadQ`, where `rhyDictQ` was: `rhyDictQ` is gone, no game used it any more), `moveDictation` beside `moveReading`; sweep block `/*RDSW_BEGIN*/ … /*RDSW_END*/` at the end of `tests/sweep.js`.
### The ladder (16 levels, Reading Rhythm's order of figures; one bar of 4/4 unless said)
1 Whole, half, quarter · 2 Eighth notes · 3 Eighth rests and off-beats · 4 Ties and dots · 5 3/4 · 6 Syncopation I · 7 Syncopation II: ties into the beat · 8 Eighth-note triplets · 9 Quarter-note triplets · 10 Sixteenths · 11 Dotted eighth and sixteenth · 12 Sixteenth rests · 13 6/8 · 14 Swing · 15 Two bars · 16 Two bars: everything (4/4, 3/4, 6/8, swing, broken triplets `trn tnr tnrn`). The vocabularies are `RD_V[L]` (the brief's table); each level: generator 1 = the new figure with the simplest company (`o.must` = a bar holds at least one of the new cells), generator 2 = the whole vocabulary; level 16 has five (broken triplets, everything in 4/4, 3/4, 6/8, swing).
- `rdQ(o)`: `cells`, `M` (4, 3; **6 = 6/8 counted in eighths**), `bars`, `swing`, `max` (tempo cap: 80 for sixteenths, which equals what old Dictation III played at, `rbpm()` being 80; 60 for the 6/8 dotted quarter), `must`, `what`, `hint`. **Item keys are `rhyDictQ`'s** (`rd-<meter>-<onsets>`), so a moved answer keeps its item; 6/8 keys are `rd-6-…`. The question carries `rdCells` and `rdBpm` (for the sweep).
- **6/8** (`RD68`: `d3 e3 qe eq re2 qr r3 dh`, in eighths; `rd68Bars`, `rd68Name`): drawn by `rhySVG8` with groups [3,3], the click on the two dotted quarters. The writer takes `q.wu` = 2 (`rwAdd` multiplies every value: an eighth is 1, a dotted quarter 3, a dotted half 6); `rhyText(ev, wu)`, `givenText` and the writer's line (“eighths left”) read it; `q.rso` = the drawing options. `rhySVG8` now keeps a dotted half on beat 1 of 6/8 as one dotted note (it split it into two tied dotted quarters); no other caller draws a [3,3] bar.
- **Swing is heard swung now** (`rdSw`): the old Swing dictation played its notes straight (only the onsets in the key were swung). 3/4 needed no change (writer, drawing, click and grading already took `M`).
### The move (`moveDictation`, flags `g.mig.rd1` on Rhythm Theory and `STORE.rd1`; run at the end of `G('rhytheory')`, and `G('rhydict')` calls `G('rhytheory')` first when it has no `rd1`)
- `RD_KEEP` = {1:1, 2:2, 3:3, 5:4 Count it II, 7:5 Swing vocabulary, 10:6 Repeats and endings, 11:7 The road map, 12:8 Accents, dynamics, articulation} · `RD_MOVE` = {4 Dictation I → 4 Ties and dots, 6 Dictation II → 8 Eighth-note triplets, 8 Swing dictation → 14 Swing, 9 Dictation III → 10 Sixteenths}.
- Moved as `moveReading` does: items by `it.lv`, the log `pl` by level, rounds and their practice time, history points, `hist ht hs hm`, `lc`, totals `n c ms pn pc time`, each day's share where the moved answers are dated (log, rounds). `bpmL`/`altN` options renumbered, round length copied. Kept in Rhythm Theory: mix-ups `cf`, per-key `rk`. **LOGDB is not touched**: its old entries stay under `rhytheory` with the old level numbers. The new game's `mig` gets the flags of the older one-off migrations (`rv1` among them: without it `playsAfter` would have removed every moved record after 15 Nov).
- **Frontiers**: Rhythm Theory = the first kept level not passed (the `moveReading` rule; `skip` and the honest open list `unl0` renumbered). Rhythm Dictation = the level after the highest moved level that was passed (below the frontier and not in `skip`, or by its own record), the levels under it not passed in `skip`; a moved level that was opened (`unl`, or `unl0` while “all levels open” is on) stays open; **a moved level that was reached but not passed opens nothing** (the brief).
- **On the user's store**: Rhythm Theory 159 answers / 141 clean before; after, Rhythm Theory 144 / 140 + Rhythm Dictation 15 / 1. Items 87 = 76 + 11; answer time 1,163,696 ms = 838,701 + 324,995; rounds 8 = 8 + 0, history points 8 = 8 + 0, log 0; per-level counts 159 = 144 + 15 (the 15 on new level 4). Frontiers: Rhythm Theory old 4 (Dictation I) → **new 4 “Count it II”**, `last` 3; Rhythm Dictation **1**, nothing in `skip`, nothing open; the 15 answers sit on level 4 as history. Every other game byte-identical, the only new top-level key `rd1`, second load a no-op (`tests/dict_migtest.py`). **Decision**: the 15 moved answers have no dated record (no log entry, no round at level 4: review answers inside level-3 rounds), so their share of `g.days` and the practice time stay with Rhythm Theory. Made-up players: old Dictation I and II passed, frontier old 7, old 9 open → Rhythm Theory 5, Rhythm Dictation 9 with `skip` 1 2 3 5 6 7 and 10 open, all totals and days add up; “all levels open” on → both games keep their honest lists (`unl0` [] and [8]).
- Highlights in Rhythm Theory's notes: none stored (`STORE.hl.rhytheory` absent), so moving a chapter moved none.
### Goals (sweep goal checks pass; 367 levels required by some goal)
- B stage 2 `rhytheory 1–3` + `rhydict 1–4`; stage 3 `rhytheory 4` + `rhydict 5–8`; appendix `rhydict 9–16`. D stage 2 `rhytheory 1–4` + `rhydict 1–4`; stage 3 `rhytheory 5–7` + `rhydict 5–14`; appendix `rhydict 15–16`. R stage 1 `rhytheory 1–3` (as before); stage 2 `rhytheory 4` + `rhydict 1–4`; appendix `rhytheory 5–8`, `rhydict 5–16`. `GOAL_WHY`: B `rhytheory:1`, `rhydict:1`, `rhytheory:4`, `rhydict:5`; D `rhytheory:1`, `rhydict:1`, `rhytheory:5`, `rhydict:5`; R `rhytheory:4`, `rhydict:1`.
- **Decision**: a goal that required old Dictation II (B) or Swing dictation (D) now requires its new level, and the rule “a game's steps run from level 1 without a gap” makes it require the levels under it too: B requires `rhydict 5–8`, **D requires `rhydict 5–14`** (ten dictation levels in stage 3, where it had three). The brief's “no further required levels” and the no-gap rule could not both hold.
### Texts and notes
- Rhythm Theory: name, `desc`, lesson card, lobby note, the four dictation level notes gone, the chapter “Rhythmic dictation: the method” and two dictation rows of “The usual mistakes” moved to Rhythm Dictation. Reading Rhythm's `desc`, Reading Rhythm's and Sight Reading's pointers to “Rhythm Theory & Dictation” corrected.
- `LEVEL_NOTES.rhydict`: levels 1–14 full notes, 15–16 “a” notes. `LESSON_NOTES.rhydict`, eleven chapters: the method (moved) · hearing values against the click · eighths and off-beats · ties and dots by ear · 3/4 and 6/8 by ear · syncopation · triplets of every size · sixteenths and their groupings · swing · two bars · common faults. Figures by `rhySVG`/`rhySVG8`; the figure tables are computed from the cells (`slots`: where a sound starts, which slots are held or silent); points to Reading Rhythm for notation and to Hear the Degree 10–12 for melodic dictation.

### Review (25 Nov 2026, the same evening; user: "double check everything makes sense and is working")
Reread the brief and the code, reran every check, sampled every generator's bars with the timing of the click and each note, rendered the notation. The ladder, the move (store copy and made-up players), the goals and the writer were right. Changed:
- **A count-in**: `rdQ` played the bar from the first click, with no bar of clicks before it (the old `rhyDictQ` did the same), so a bar that opens with a rest gave no tempo to count from. One bar of clicks now counts in (two dotted-quarter clicks in 6/8), as Hear the Degree's melodic dictation does; the prompt, the lobby note and the notes say so.
- **A half rest on beat 2 of 4/4** (levels 1, 15, 16 through the cell `hr`): print writes two quarter rests there. `rdQ` draws again until no half rest stands off beats 1 and 3 (the build had dropped `hr` from 3/4 for the same reason). Level 1 still gives 14 to 15 different bars in 300 draws.
- Sweep: both checked (a half rest off 1 and 3; one bar of clicks before the first note can sound), each proved by breaking the code once.
- Left as they are: the 6/8 staff numbers its eighths 1 2 3 1 2 3 under the bar while the prompts and counts say 1 & a 2 & a (`rhySVG8`, as in Reading Rhythm); `tests/dict_start.py` starts each level once per generator but does not force which generator asks (every generator is sampled by the sweep).

## The same key again and again (25 Nov 2026, user: Intervals & Keys 1 asked D♭ seven times in a row)
`rootPick` (twelve-key coverage) took the root this game had seen least, with a noise of 1.5 answers: a root more than two answers behind the rest was drawn **every time** until it caught up. The count `g.rk` is per game, and levels with other root lists raise the other roots (on the user's store D♭ stood about ten behind when level 1 was opened again). Now a lagging root is preferred only a little (its deficit counts as 1 at most, noise 3) and **the roots of the last two questions are not drawn again** while the list has others. Simulated on a copy of the user's store with D♭ fifteen behind: 12 of 60 questions, never twice running. It affects every game that draws its key with `rootPick`.

## OSJ: levels from a guitar workbook the user studied (24 Nov 2026, the OSJ brief in `.claude/`, tiers 1 and 2; built by another session, reviewed here and published on 25 Nov 2026)
Fourteen levels **appended** and one generator pair added to an older level; no insert, no renumbering, no migration, no existing item key changed, no saved data touched. **Nothing was played on a guitar or sung; the strummed levels were never tried with a real strum.** Code: the block `/*OSJ_BEGIN*/ … /*OSJ_END*/` (just before `const GAMES`) for generators, `/*OSJN_BEGIN*/ … /*OSJN_END*/` (just before `/*YT_NOTES_BEGIN*/`) for level notes and notes items; the sweep block `/*SPRSW_BEGIN*/ … /*SPRSW_END*/` at the end of `tests/sweep.js` (no template literals, no backslashes; written from `/tmp/osj/sw.js` by `/tmp/osj/putsw.py`).
### The tag
- `OSJ` (right after `GAMES`) = `{arp:[16,17,18,19], drop2:[19,20,21], triads:[15], scales:[19], modes:[13], sing:[17], tgrips:[11]}`; `lvNum(gid,L)` → `'16-OSJ'`, `lvNumH` (the rung, the tag small), `lvRange`. Used wherever a level number is printed: play header, rungs (level page and goal page), side panel and Start button, Details (a heading line for tagged levels), results and unlock banners, coach notes, “Next:” bars, groove screens, tree boxes, tooltips, appendix chips, stats level table, tempo rows, the masters' lines (`gameFacts` gives `lvTxt`) and the chat prompt. Display only: `g.lvl`, keys, `GOALS`, `LEVEL_NOTES`, level names are plain.
- Notes items: `osj:true` on a chapter or an item; `noteItem` draws `<span class="osj">(OSJ)</span>` (also in the chapter list and heading); two new item forms `{txt}` (paragraph) and `{li}` (bullets); `noteText` leaves the tag out. Arpeggio Runner's `ig` prints `lvNum`; the notes sweep reads `16-OSJ “…”` and checks the tag. Arpeggio Runner 11's new generators carry “(OSJ)” at the end of their `.qsub` (`osjSub`).
### Tier 1
- **Arpeggio Runner 16 “Connect across keys”**: `CON.xm7a` (Maj7 a major 3rd down three times), `xm7b` (four Maj7/m7 chords, roots a 3rd apart, never all in one key: `osjInOneKey`), `xii` (II–V–I in three keys a major 3rd apart), `…8` eighths; spellings `osjSp`/`osjKey` (fewest accidentals). **`spec.holdOk`** (false everywhere): in `soloGrade` it would let a note held over the change pass; the connect rule is unchanged. Chapter “Connecting across keys” after chapter 20 (computed line, table of shared notes and half steps).
- **Drop-2 19 “Four inversions up one set, in time”** `d2InvQ`, `d2InvPath` (`osjD2` = genDrop2's rule over frets 0–17; `osjUp` = every voice to the next chord tone above on its string), keys `d2inv-<q>-<set>-<root>`; exact grips, seven strums. Chapter “From one inversion to the next” after chapter 1.
- **Drop-2 20 “Common tone on top”** `d2CtQ`/`d2CtPath`: four Maj7/m7 chords a 3rd apart that share one pitch class; every accepted grip (any set, `strumAltSeq`) has that pitch on top; keys `d2ct-<root>-<qualities and moves>`. Chapter “The common tone on top” after “II–V–I: the nearest grip”, and in that chapter a paragraph and grips IMaj7 → I6.
- **Arpeggio Runner 17 “More four-note arpeggios”**: `arpPosQ` with `OSJ_Q4`; **`CH['maj7♯5']`** added (name in `QNAME_X`; the notes' `XQ` now takes it from `CH`); `TONE_LAB` and `BN_Q` entries added.
- **Arpeggio Runner 18 “One arpeggio over another chord”** `ARPOV`, `arpOvMake`, `arpOvQ` (band opt-in as the game's other band levels), keys `arpov-<row>-<key>`; chapter 26 lost “No game drills these as arpeggios yet” and gained a paragraph pointing to Chord Spelling's look-alikes chapter.
- **Triad Shapes 15 “The triads of a key up one string set”** `tskQ`, `tskPath` (start frets 1–5, all frets 1–17), `TSK_ANY` (computed pairs that fit), keys `tsk-<set>-<inv>-<key>-<up|down>`; chapter 9 gets a paragraph, figure and pointer.
### Tier 2
- **Scale Runner 19 “Triad shapes and cells”**: kinds `t3135 t1531 t5135 t1353 c1231 inv1 inv2` in `SC_ST`/`SC_GDEF`/`SC_PAT`, `SC_PX` explanations; **`scPattern` and `scQ` take the span as the largest step** (the sweep proves every older kind gives the same output as before).
- **Arpeggio Runner 11**: figure `sur` (`encFig`; `encTone` returns `ap2`), two generators at the end of the level (`enc-chr-sur-<key>`, `-d`).
- **Arpeggio Runner 19 “Ninth arpeggios”**: `arpPosQ` with `OSJ_Q9`; `arpPosQ` takes `o.keys`.
- **Modes 13 “Inside the half-whole scale”** `hwTriQ` (`mdhw-tri-<I key>`), `hwDimQ` (`mdhw-dim-<I key>-<M|m>`).
- **Sing It 17 “Sing it, then play it: an arpeggio or a scale”** `singArpScaleQ` (`spa-<q>-<root>`, `sps-<mode>-<root>`), and **`q.parts.share`** (set only here): `micGradeParts` also tries cuts where the join note counts for both parts.
- **Drop-2 21 “Chord scales in minor”** `d2csQ`, `d2csPath`, `d2csChords`, keys `d2cs-<hm|mm>-<set>-<key>`.
- **Tension Grips 11 “One grip, four places”** `tg4Q`, `tg4Path`, keys `tg4-<root>-<set>`.
- **Notes only (2.8)**: Chord Spelling look-alikes (with every note / only without the root, computed), Chord–Scale pentatonics (minor-6, inside Lydian ♭7 and altered, each checked), harmonic-minor modes as chords, the half-whole table in “Symmetric scales”, Harmony & Analysis 16 (diatonic passing chords; triads over a falling bass on a computed four-note melody), Triad Shapes 9 (triads of harmonic and melodic minor).
- Goals: appendices only (A `arp 16–19`, `scales 18–19`, `modes 5–13`; B `sing 16–17`; C `triads 11–15`, `drop2 19–21`, `tgrips 8–11`; D `drop2 19–21`).
### Decisions (the brief did not settle them)
- `xm7b` rejects a chain whose four chords fit one major key (the level is about leaving the key).
- The jump-to-root check on 72-note lines breaks two changes, because `soloGrade` already lets one broken rule through from 48 slots.
- “A strum half a beat late fails” is proved with the whole take late: the strum grader's three-in-four timing rule lets one late strum in seven pass, and it was not changed.
- Drop-2 20: the shared pitch stays on top of all four chords (all four share it), and the first chord also accepts any drop-2 grip with that top note.
- Arpeggio Runner 18: C°7 over A♭7 is written C E♭ G♭ B𝄫 (one double flat allowed).
- Modes 13: the triads are spelled as triads on roots with the fewest accidentals (G7: G, B♭, D♭, E), not by `mdSpell` (which writes A♯ and C♯ as ♯9 and ♯11); the °7 line is five eighths from the “and” of 2 landing on the root of I (a half step above its top note); keys name the I key.
- Arpeggio Runner 11: the surround is two generators (plain, displaced), so the level draws it in two of six.
- Sing It 17: `parts.share` (the arpeggio 1 3 5 7 5 3 1 ends on the pitch the played part starts on).
- Chord–Scale's harmonic-minor table marks modes 1, 2, 5, 7 as in use, the rest “rarely as a scale of its own”.
- Highlights: chapters were inserted in Arpeggio Runner and Drop-2 notes; the stored highlights (backup of 24 Nov) have none in those games.
- Older sweep checks adapted: ladder counts subtract `OSJ` (Scale Runner, Modes, Sing It, Triad Shapes, Arpeggio Runner, `REORDERS27`), the chord table has 32 entries.
### Checks, and what was not verified
- Sweep `bad 0` twice after each tier, `pages_check` all ok, `build.py`, `tests/arp_notes_shots.py` clean, every new level started headless and muted (`john_start.py`, `ear_start.py` for Sing It 17), the simulated player (`john_player.py`) PASS on Arpeggio Runner 11 (surround), 16, 17, 18, 19, Scale Runner 19, Modes 13, Sing It 17; each key sweep check proved by breaking the code once.
- **Not verified**: any of it on a real guitar or voice; the strummed levels (Drop-2 19–21, Triad Shapes 15, Tension Grips 11) have never heard a real strum (notes under C3 on the low set are not checked by the chord detector); the texts are unread by the teacher.

### Review before publishing (25 Nov 2026)
Sweep and `pages_check` rerun, sampled questions of every new generator read, and **the strummed levels run through the live listening path**: `tests/john_player.py` now strums (a question with `strumSeq`: every chord rolled at its grid point, ringing to the next). Drop-2 19, 20, 21, Triad Shapes 15 and Tension Grips 11 (both sets) pass with the right take and fail when the first grip is strummed every time. The music was right everywhere. Changed:
- **Scale Runner 19, the inversions**: `inv1` = [0,2,5] and `inv2` = [0,3,5] (the brief's [2,4,7] and [4,7,9] were the same shapes two and four scale steps higher, so the run did not start on the lowest note of the position as its own line says and never reached the bottom four notes). The sentences for 5 1 3 5 and 1 5 3 1 said the wrong direction.
- **Arpeggio Runner 16**: the first chord of a chain is spelled like the others (`osjSp`): a line no longer shows A♭m7 and G♯m7 as two chords.
- **Drop-2 19**: the prompt's “then the 3rd, the 2nd and the first again” read as inversion names; it says “the third grip, the second, the first”.
- Still not verified: a real strum on a real guitar, and a real voice in Sing It 17. The synthetic strum is cleaner than a guitar in a room.

## Overview: time per day by master, game and level (25 Nov 2026, user: “break this down into which master's games … also game level breakdown in the same chart”)
`timeChart` (Overview) is a stacked bar a day: one colour per master (`TM_COL`, the user's choice: John blue, Bongi yellow, Jessica red, Alfred grey, Joe purple; stack order bottom → top `TM_ORDER` Bongi, Jessica, John, Alfred, Joe so that red, yellow and grey are not neighbours), a thin gap between the games inside a master's part. Hover a slice: game and its levels with minutes; click a day (`view.tday`, action `tday`): a table of master, game, time, levels. A game's day time is `g.days[day].dur`; it is divided between levels in proportion to the day's history points (`tmLevels`, `g.rl` `dur` and `L`). Legend: each master's 14-day total. Checked on the live progress: the slices add up to the old total (1,796 min), every slice has its levels. Colours checked with the dataviz validator on the panel (#1e1f22): adjacent pairs clear the colour-blind and normal-vision floors; the grey has no chroma by the user's choice, so names are always given (legend, hover, table).

## Endless: every game runs as long as you play (25 Nov 2026, user: “a continuous mode with games where it continues as long as you play”)
- **Round games**: Round length has a fourth button, **Endless** (`g.round=0`; before, only tap games had it, as “Flow · endless”, and no tap game has round levels any more). `start` sets `S.round=Infinity`, `S.endless`, `S.lastAct`. A right answer moves on by itself and a wrong one waits for Space or Next, exactly as in a round (the user's choice). The keys already asked (`S.used`) are forgotten every 24 questions. HUD “N · Endless”, button “End session”.
- **Idle end**: `idleCheck` (every 5 s, `IDLE_T`, `IDLE_MS` = 2 min): nothing answered for two minutes ends the session (`S.idleEnd`, said on the results page). Activity = an answer that is not a time-out, any key or pointer press in the play view, or the mic listening / a sound playing.
- **Passing the level mid-session**: a note under the HUD says the next level is open; the session stays on its level (user's choice).
- **Continuous rhythm sessions**: no 5-minute stop. `GRV.max` 300 → 7200 s (a safety ceiling only), `GRV.idle` 120: two minutes without a tap or strum (`GR.lastAct`, set in `grooveMatch`) end the session (`sum.idle`, said in the summary). The setup text says so. (Older notes above that give `GRV.max` as 180 or 300 s are superseded.)
- Checked headless on a copy of the store: the button in screen and guitar games, 31 answers in one endless session, auto-advance after a right answer, a wrong one waits with Next, the unlock note, the idle end, a 6-question round still ends at 6; a Pulse session alive past 6 minutes and ended by the idle rule.

## Arpeggio Runner's full notes rewritten (24 Nov 2026, brief `.claude/arp-notes-brief.md`; built in the working tree, not committed)
The user could not follow the old notes (organised round the levels, dense, terms before their meaning, figures that made him count). `LESSON_NOTES.arp` is now a text on **arpeggios for guitarists** that says at the end of every chapter which levels practise it: 31 chapters, about 12,300 words of prose (tables not counted; about 1,000 of them are the “In the games” lines), 44 figures (67 neck diagrams, the rest staves and rhythms), 28 tables. No level, generator, key, grading rule or saved datum changed.
- **Outline** (the brief's eighteen parts, some split to stay under 600 words): 1 What you need first (the homes of the things assumed, how to read a formula, how to read the figures) · 2 What an arpeggio is, and what it is for · 3 The five seventh chords as one shape · 4 The other four-note chords (6, m6, m(Maj7), Maj7♯5 and the augmented triad, 7sus4) · 5 The hands · 6 One octave from the root (strings 6, 5, 4, 3) · 7 From the 3rd, 5th, 7th · 8 The whole position · 9 The diminished 7th and the augmented triad · 10 Over the whole neck: five areas · 11 Leaving the position (two notes a string, one string) · 12 In time · 13 Patterns · 14 A major key · 15 A minor key · 16 II–V · 17 II–V–I in one position · 18 The minor cadence · 19 Connecting (triads) · 20 Connecting sevenths, eighths · 21 Outlines · 22 Approach note, neighbour tone, the turn · 23 The turn through II–V–I · 24 The enclosure, chromatic figures, the beat · 25 The bebop dominant run · 26 One arpeggio over another chord · 27 9, 11, 13 · 28 A blues worked through · 29 Eight bars of a tune worked through · 30 In the games (every level of Arpeggio Runner with its chapters; the levels of other games) · 31 A routine, common faults.
- **The rules of this text** (keep them when editing it): the plain idea first, one worked example told in words, then the figure, then the reference table, then a tip · a term from another home gets a one-clause gloss at its first use and the home is named by the game's display name · **every chapter ends with an “In the games” line** built by `ig([[gameId, level…]…], extra)`, which reads the level names from `GAMES` · outside those lines and the chapter “In the games” the text never says “level”, “the game asks” or “the microphone” · **Maj7**, never maj7 · a pointer to another chapter is written by its title through `see(key)` (titles live in `T`), so renaming or reordering a chapter keeps every pointer true · no teaching hidden in a caption.
- **The figures**: every neck diagram has its own title (`neckT`), every dot its degree, the root gold. **Nothing is typed**: `XQ` (the chord formulas, `CH` plus Maj7♯5 and Maj13♯11), `sy` / `form` / `spn` (symbol, formula, spelling), `shape(root, quality, R0, layout)` (a fixed fingering given as [string, chord tone, octave]: the fret follows from the pitch), `posDots` / `posMap` / `posRun` (a position), `lineDots`, `stf` (a staff from sounding pitches and their spelling, written an octave higher), and the game's own builders for its own lines (`arpJoin`, `fitWindow`, `nearLine`, `nearTones`, `conRows`, `conExample`, `olLine`, `encTone`, `encFig`, `encTriLine`, `alPairSpots`; the turn through the cadence repeats `encCadQ`'s choice of octaves in `cadTurn`). **Comparison rows** (`cmpFig`, layout `LAY.cmp6`) keep every chord tone on one string so one dot moves one fret between neighbours; the item carries its dots in `cmp`, which the sweep checks. They are for comparing, and the text says so; the playing shapes (♭3 on the root's string) follow in chapter 6.
- **A fault found while the text is built** (a missing level, a chord that does not fit, a claim that the computation contradicts) goes to `LESSON_NOTES.arp.err` through `bad()`; the sweep fails on any. (Load-time entries in `NF.err` alone would be wiped before anything reads them.)
- **The worked forms**: `formOf(name, bars)` reads a stored form through `formQual`; `lineOf` writes a quarter-note line with `nearLine`, taking the nearer note at every change; `guideLine` one guide tone a bar. Chapter 28 uses `Basic blues in F`, chapter 29 `Autumn Leaves (first 8, G minor)`; each chapter carries `forms:[{name, syms}]` for the sweep. Chords only, no melody.
- **What changed in `NF`** (small, other games' figures draw as before): `neckSVG` takes `o.title` (a title above the diagram; `NF.scale` and `NF.where` pass it on; CSS `.nn-title`) · `NF.staff` takes `^text` in a token (`D4^Dm7=R`): a chord symbol above the staff from that note on (CSS `.ns-up`). Table heads are set in capitals by the page: in these notes a chord symbol or numeral in a head is wrapped so it keeps its case (Dm7 read DM7).
- **Also changed**: `Maj7` for maj7 in the lesson card's first rule and in the descriptions of levels 1 and 4 (display text only; the prompts and item keys still come from `sym()` and are untouched). No pointer to an old chapter existed in the lesson card, the level notes or other games' notes; the three references from Improvise and Call & Response (“the shapes are in the notes of Arpeggio Runner”, “the enclosure as a figure of its own…”, “its notes explain how each is built”) are still true.
- **Sweep**: block “ARPEGGIO RUNNER'S FULL NOTES” at the end of `tests/sweep.js`: every “In the games” line and the two tables of chapter 30 name only levels that exist, by their real names; every chapter ends with such a line; every neck diagram has a title; no maj7; each comparison row differs from its neighbour by one dot moved one fret on one string; the blues and the tune use the chords of their stored form and no other chord is named in those chapters; no talk of the game outside the lines; pointers name chapters that exist; `err` empty. Each check was proved by breaking it once (a wrong level name, a missing title, a second dot moved, maj7, D7 in the blues, “the game asks… level”).
- **Checks**: sweep `bad 0` twice, `pages_check` `all ok`, `build.py`. `tests/arp_notes_shots.py` (new) renders the notes page headless and muted at 1440 and 1000 px, full page and chapter by chapter (`tests/out/arp-notes-*.png`), and reports figure errors, figures wider than their frame, rows that wrapped, titles or chord symbols cut off or overlapping: none. All 31 chapters were read as text and the figures looked at as rendered. A proof-read by script (`/tmp/arpn/proof.js`, not in the repo) read all 67 diagrams back from their SVG: 510 dots, each label equal to the interval its string and fret make with the root in the title, gold only on roots, every label a member of the titled chord.
- **Decisions the brief did not settle**: 31 chapters, not 18 (the 600-word limit) · about 12,300 words, over the brief's 9,000–11,000 · **the brief's “m7 arpeggio from the 5th of a Maj7 for a 6/9 sound” is not true as written** (Gm7 over CMaj7 has B♭ and F); the table gives what the computation shows: a m7 from the 6th of a major chord is the 6 chord, a m7 from the 5th of a **dominant** is the suspended sound, a Maj7 from the 5th of a Maj7 is the ♯11 · the minor-key chapter is in A minor (the same seven arpeggios as C major), the minor cadence in C minor beside the major one · the worked tune is Autumn Leaves, the blues the basic three-chord form · economy picking is shown in a computed table for one shape, with the carried stroke marked · the Bert Ligon sentence is kept (“teaches lines of this kind as outlines”).
- **Not verified**: nothing here was played. The teacher has not read the text; the choices of emphasis (what each chord “sounds like”, which notes are left out of big chords, the five areas of the neck) are mine. A highlight the user made in the old notes stays stored but is no longer drawn (it is found by its words). The chapter count of the text and the brief's eighteen parts differ: the report to the user lists the mapping.

## The review of John's games: the fixes (22 Nov 2026, brief `.claude/john-audit-brief.md`, reviews `.claude/audit/<gameId>.md`; snapshot: tag `v-2026-11-22-before-john-audit` (local), store copy `backups/store-before-john-audit-2026-11-22.json`; built in the working tree, reviewed, then committed and published on 23 Nov 2026 at the user's word)
**Re-read this section and the brief before each phase.** Rules: build exactly the brief's list (recommendations of the review files that are not in it are not built); no saved data dropped; every musical fact computed and proved in `tests/sweep.js` (block “THE REVIEW OF JOHN'S GAMES” at its end, written from `/tmp/john/sw*.js` by `/tmp/john/ins.py`, which doubles the backslashes); after each phase sweep `bad 0` twice, `pages_check` `all ok`, `build.py`, every new or changed level started headless and muted on the store copy (`tests/john_start.py game:levels…`, screenshots `tests/out/jn-*.png`), ten questions of every new generator read as text. **Nothing here is played on a real guitar.**
### Phase 1, done
- **1.0 texts** (all computed where code writes them): Fretboard: level 1 note (every fret 0–5), `fbOct` explanation from the tuning (`octTwo`, `octRuleTxt`: strings 6 and 5 → 2 frets up, 4 and 3 → 3) and a hint that names a real answer spot (`octMoveTxt`), `landmark` without “count 0”, level 9 note and chapter: the octave is checked, the string is the player's discipline · Intervals on the Neck: compound intervals by name (`nivSimpleName`: an octave plus a perfect 4th), the hint from the real spots (`spotMoveTxt`, “the same string, N frets up”), the chapter-6 table keeps cells up to five frets (♭13 three strings up = +5), the G–B exception in level 5's note · Along the Neck 8: from the ♭3 to the 7 · Reading: level 4 note (0–1–3 on strings 6, 1, 2; 0–2–3 on 5, 4), the dyad explanation states its own fret distance plus both string cases, Roman numerals in `sightQ` prompts, landmark hints (`staffLandmark`, `STAFF_MARKS`) in place of the mnemonics, an open string named as such · Sight Reading: `desc` lists the figures read with pitches, the level-9 natural (up or as an upper neighbour), chapter 2's list of values · Scale Runner: level 7 note (1 3 5 7 up, 5 3 1 down, tonic on beat 3 of bar 2; the drift only from the octave on a beat: 1 6 4 2), `scModeQ('one')` says the fingering changes, Maj7 via `BN_Q` · Modes: the reference scale of a family says “the note to bring out”, the others their difference from it (`mdDiff`); chapter 11's click facts computed (`mdClickSemis`); spelling note by note (`mdSpellNotes`: only a double accidental is respelled; E half-whole G♯ A♯ C♯); the parent on the scale's side (`mdParent(name,rp,rk)`: F♯ melodic minor); level 1 renamed “Dorian, Mixolydian, Ionian: the scales of IIm7, V7 and IMaj7” · Arpeggio Runner: level 13 note (the new IIm7 stands on the old V7's root; keys least-seen first), m6 row in chapter 1, **“Mixolydian ♭9 ♭13” everywhere** (also in YT_NOTES), the 2 1 7 1 figure is called **the turn** (the first step towards the enclosure; the enclosure puts the target last) in desc, prompts, labels, level notes, lesson card and chapter; level names kept · Blues Boxes: the mix explanation, “shuffle” dropped · Improvise: `soloHint(S0)` by spec (`soloEvery`, `soloWalk` = the walk-up line, proved to pass every spec that shows it in every key and tune; a 3rd already in the bar is taken in another octave), lesson card, chapter 1 table to 15, chapter 8 per level, chapter 10 tip, the quick-change form named (`soloBluesTxt`) · Phrase and Space: its form named (`phraseBluesTxt`, no quick change) · Bends: the finger sentence by kind, “rate and width”, level 7 note.
- **1.1 Phrase and Space 7–10** (block “PHRASES OVER THE CHANGES” after `phraseGrade`; `PHC`, `PHC_PAIRS`, `phcCad`, `phcPos`, `phcChordAt`, `phcLine`, `phraseChQ`, `phraseChGrade`, dispatched by `q.phc` in `micGrade`). Chords by `soloForm`, band as `soloQ`, ≤80 bpm, `listenTo:28` (new generic field read by `micListen`: the take runs into bar 8). The example steps down each chord's own seven-note scale (`SOLO_WALK` where it is accepted) from beat 2 to the landing guide tone. Keys: 7–8 `AN_KEYS`, 9 the brief's pairs, 10 `WHOLE`. `GOALS.A` stage 5 `['phrase',7,10]`, `GOAL_WHY` `phrase:7`, four level notes, desc, lesson card line, chapter “Space over the changes”, the chapter-1 table to 10. Decision: “a note within 0.35 beat before a bar line belongs to the next bar” is applied at bar lines only, not at mid-bar chord changes.
- **1.2 Sight Reading and Reading**: `srPosFing` (positioned windows use `scPos` of the key, stretches included; windows from fret 0 unchanged), `srSpot` (the fingering's place, stretch marked `fb-st` with “(a stretch)” in the explanation; in a window from fret 0 the open string first), `srMove`/`SR_LEAP` (`leap:true`), `srSeesaw` (A B A B A rejected in every `srLineQ`/`srShiftQ` line), `srTwoLeaps` (after an accidental is placed). `leap:true` on levels 7, 12, 13, 16, 19, 20, 24, 25; four-bar lines added to 7, 13, 16, 20; levels 24 and 25 mix 3/4, swing, a natural, 6/8 (`cap:56`) and in 25 sixteenths, cut time, quarter-note triplets (`cap:56`; `srMake` plays at min(level tempo, cap) and prints the tempo). **Shift lines**: with stretches the two positions share every pitch but the outer ones, so `srShiftQ` now starts the walk within reach of the first note the first position cannot hold (else 60 % of the questions failed to generate); the shift therefore falls near the top or bottom of the range. Reading 7–10: `sightPosQ(win)` asks a stretch note one question in six (`sfs-<s>-<f>`, answer one fret outside the window; VII none); `fb-ok` is matched as a class word in the play and answer code. Notes: level notes, “Choosing the position” (computed figure of C in position V, leaps, the mixed levels), Reading's positions chapter. **Decision**: the sweep's “no A B A B A” covers the generated lines (`srLineQ`, `srShiftQ`), not the tune pages, forms and road map (pages, not lines; a real tune may contain it); the “60 % with a leap” rule is measured per level (all its generators together), the even-quarter generator alone reaches about 55 %.
- **1.3 Scale Runner (18 levels)**: `SC_ST`/`SC_GDEF` (kinds `g3`, `g4`, `4`), `scQ` asks whole-position patterns through every group (`scPattern(n,kind,dir,0,n)`; other callers unchanged); new 9 “Groups of three and four”, 12 “Fourths”; 11's alternating and eighth-note generators and 2's bottom-to-top run in C, F, G; 13 adds triplets and eighths, 14 eighths. Chapter “Groups, thirds, fourths, triads, seventh chords”.
- **1.4 Arpeggio Runner (15 levels)**: new 4 “The whole position” (`arpPosQ`, keys `arw-<sym>-<fret>-<div>`), level 3 with m7♭5/dim7, the II–V join V7 down from its 3rd (`arpJoin`; key unchanged), displaced turns and enclosures (`encDisp`: grid from 3.5 or 2.5, the cadence's closing root on the next beat 1, key `-d`, counts in the prompt).
- **1.5 migration** `REORDERS27` (flag `r53`, after `r52`, same code). `tests/john_migtest.py` (uses `/tmp/john/oldnames.json` for the old level names). **On the user's store**: Scale Runner 1,005 answers / 668 clean before and after, frontier 9 → 10 (“Thirds, in C” both times), open “Thirds: C, F and G” (10 → 11), open-not-passed [1, 5, 6, 7, 8] → [1, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9] (9 = the new Groups level), the 3 answers of old 10 on 11; Arpeggio Runner 4 answers, level 1, unchanged; every other game byte-identical; no top-level key changed; second load a no-op. Made-up players below, on and above each insert behave (e.g. scales 11 with 12 open → 13 with 14 open, 9 and 12 in skip; arp 4 with 5 open → 5 with 6 open, 4 in skip; tempos renumbered).
- `GOALS.A`: scales 1–4, 5–8, 9–14, 15–17, more 18; arp 1–2, 3–4, 5–8, 9–14, 15; goal R's more `scales 3–15`; `GOAL_WHY` keys `arp:5`, `arp:9`, `arp:15`, `scales:15` (texts mention the new levels).
### Phase 2, done (no migration)
- **2.1 Improvise**: `SOLO.chr.needChrom` (a take with no outside note that moves a half step into the next note fails: “no approach note was played”); `soloLine` puts one on beat 4 of the IIm7 bar, a half step below the 3rd of V7. `soloChromOk`: the chromatic rule also accepts an enclosure (D♭ B C, A♭ F♯ G). **Long takes** (`soloGrade`): from 48 slots `floor(slots/48)` rule faults are let through and named (`brokeN`, `faultMax`); a flat bar and a missing approach note still fail. **This loosens “a broken rule always fails” for the 48- and 96-note choruses and the whole forms; the user has not confirmed it.** The old sweep checks that one repeated note fails a long take were changed to “one more than the allowance fails”. Decision: level 5's hint is the walk-up plus “just before the second chord, play the note a half step below its 3rd” (`soloHint`, `soloWalk` with `needChrom`): the plain walk-up cannot pass a level that requires an approach note, and the brief says a spec where it fails must not show it.
- **2.2 Modes**: `modeSoloGrade` adds three rules on the heard notes (two changes of direction, `modeTurns`; one move of 3+ half steps; a chord tone on beat 1 of bar 2); `modeSoloLine` builds the example (random search under the rules, no A B A B, 7+ different pitches). Chapter figure redrawn (G on beat 1 of bar 2). **New level 12 “The dominant resolves”** (`MDR`, `MDR_KEYS`, `modeResLine`, `modeResQ`, keys `mdr-<id>-<key>`): eight generators, each scale in C, then each in eight keys. Chapter “The scale has to land” (computed table), level note, `GOALS.A` more `modes 5–12`. Altered and half-whole lines are spelled by degree (G altered: C♯ B A♯ A♭…, the ♯11 resolving to C).
- **2.3 Blues Boxes (10 levels)**: `boxQ({form:'root'})` (key `-rt`) on levels 2 and 3; **new 9 “Over I and IV”** (`bxIVLine`, `boxIVQ`, `bx-iv-<root>`) and **10 “The three chords inside the box”** (`bxChLine`, `boxChQ`, `bx-ct-<I|IV|V>-<root>`), helpers `bxIn`, `bxSpot`, `bxBox1`, `bxWalk`. **The user's decisions (asked during the build)**: level 9 cannot fit A and B inside box 1 as written, so the climb turns and comes back where the next note would leave the box (A: A B C♯ E F♯ A B A); the descent follows the same rule; the window chosen is the box-1 copy with no skipped scale note and the fewest turns (box 1 only where all its frets are ≤ 16). Level 10 is seven notes, R 3 5 ♭7 5 3 R (up to the ♭7 and back, not the octave); the sweep checks the top note is the ♭7. Chapter “Major and minor over the blues” gains both exercises (computed tables); level notes; `GOALS.A` more `boxes 2–10`.
- Checks: sweep `bad 0` twice, `pages_check` all ok, `build.py`, `tests/john_start.py improv:5,9,13,15 modes:7,11,12 boxes:2,3,9,10` without a page error, samples read. **Not played on a real guitar.**
### Phase 3, done (no migration; the build is complete)
- **3.1 Fretboard 16 “Every root in a position”** (`fbPosQ`, keys `fbp-<first fret>-<pc>`): every place of a note in a shaded five-fret window, first fret 1–8; in guitar input the distinct pitches low to high (`toPlay`'s plain multi path). Levels 7 and 8 lost the whole-neck naming generator (data untouched); level 8's finder draws sharps and flats only (`fbLoc('acc')`, keys unchanged). Level notes, desc, `GOALS.A` more `fretboard 16`.
- **3.2 Intervals on the Neck 6 “Name the interval”** (`nivNameQ`, `NIN_LAB`, keys `nin-<half steps>-<lower string>-<upper string>`): screen input, an ordered row with short labels; the second generator adds the tensions with the root on strings 6–4. Level note, a paragraph in “From intervals to chords”, `GOALS.A` more `neckiv 4–6`.
- **3.3 Bends**: `BEND_DEG` gains `b34` (♭3 → 4, whole) and `b33` (♭3 → 3, half); `bendDegQ` names both notes from the degrees (`BEND_SP`), not at the box's first fret; level 8's second generator has `b34`, level 9's `b33`; each level gains a cue-free generator (`bendDegQ(ids,{cue:false})`, keys `-n`): only the chord is heard, voiced so that neither the start nor the target pitch sounds. `BEND`, `bendAnalyse` and the pass bands unchanged.
- Checks: sweep `bad 0` twice, `pages_check` all ok, `build.py`, `tests/john_start.py fretboard:7,8,16 neckiv:6 bends:8,9` without a page error, samples read. **Not played on a real guitar.**
### Review of this build (23 Nov 2026, user: “fix everything broken… make it whole… then commit to the real page”)
Read: ten or more sampled questions of every new or changed generator, the new graders (`phraseChGrade`, `soloGrade` with `soloChromOk`, `modeSoloGrade`), the level notes of the twelve new levels, the migration on the store copy (clean: 1,005 Scale Runner answers kept, frontier “Thirds, in C”, second load a no-op), the brief's list of wrong texts (all gone). The music was right everywhere. Changed:
- **Improvise 5** did not say its new rule: the prompt now lists “at least one approach note”.
- **Chord symbols of the improvising chords** (`soloChord`, so Improvise and Phrase and Space 7–10): `Maj7`, not `maj7`.
- **Fretboard 16**: the hint said “find one on string 6 or 5 inside the shaded frets” also when neither string holds the note there; it is built from the answer now.
- **Reading 7–10, stretch notes**: the explanation named two places and described only one; each place now says which finger reaches, and which one the position's fingering takes.
- `tests/john_migtest.py` reads the old level names from `tests/john_oldnames.json` (it read a file in `/tmp`).
- **`tests/john_player.py game:level[:generator]`** (new, after `build.py`): the question's own example rendered as a plucked take and fed through the live listening path (Basic Pitch, headless, muted), for any guitar level.
- Kept as built, on purpose: the fault allowance on long improvised takes (told to the user in the brief's hand-over, not objected to); shift lines falling near the top or bottom of the range (that is where a position runs out); example lines of the phrase levels as a plain scale descent to the landing note (“one way to do it”).
### Hand-over notes (22–23 Nov 2026)
- Not built (the brief's “not in this build” list): level removals or merges, keys in a row in one position, a 3–5–7–9 arpeggio level, Blues Boxes sequences and shuffle, vibrato evenness, dyads on one stem, written triads in Reading, rhythm-slot levels and the jazz blues in Improvise, any other change to Along the Neck or the goals.
