## The review of the ear games: build 1 (23 Nov 2026, brief `.claude/ear-build-1.md`; reviews in `.claude/audit/<gameId>.md`; built in the working tree, not committed)
“Make the existing levels honest”: no level added, removed or renumbered, no item key changed, no saved data touched. **Nothing was sung or played**: everything below is checked by the sweep, by reading sampled questions and notes as text, and by starting the changed levels headless and muted.
- **The bass is a line** (`bassLine(pcs)`, used by `playChords` and `playOuter`; done before this build): each root goes to the next by the shorter way. Texts that said where the bass goes were re-read against it and corrected (II–V–I rises a 4th twice; Im – ♭VI – ♭III – ♭VII falls a 3rd and two 4ths; a cycle of 4ths turns back at the edges). `playChords` takes `ch.up` (the upper notes as given).
- **Levels that gave the answer away**: Hear the Interval 8 (`earInterval(set,mode,{quiet:1})`: one neutral prompt for rising, falling and together) · Hear the Mode 4 (“a vamp”), 5 and 6 (`earBorrowQ`: five chords, I – three chords – I, the borrowed II played as II°, “1st” gone, the note row always ♭3 ♭6 ♭7, `q.also` = a second true note that is accepted; the item key still carries the old token `IIm7♭5`) · Hear the Progression 13 (one wording, the length not named; `FORM_LAB` = “the turnaround IMaj7 – VI7 – IIm7 – V7, played twice”), 17 (`EP_DEV[id].seq`: every phrase five chords, a plain cadence plays one chord twice; passing 7th and line cliché voiced with the moving voice on top by `epUp`), 19 (`harmQ(pool,{blind:1})`: no degrees printed, a cadence first).
- **Texts**: `IVREF` is gone. `IV_MOVE` (each interval as a move between degrees; `ivMove`) writes the explanations, the lesson card and the notes' table · `DEG_RES`/`degPull(d,minor)`: every degree its own sentence · `singDegHint` · Hear the Chord: sus chords and extensions have their own explanation and hint; `EARQ` names `m7♭5 (half-diminished)` and `m(Maj7) (minor-major 7th)` · Hear the Mode: “Lydian ♭7” (also called Lydian dominant, said once), altered = ♭9 ♯9 ♭5 ♭13 · the shown name “the turnaround with tritone substitutes” (`progName`; the `PROGS` key with “Dameron” is a statistics id and stays) · chord names write ° (`progSyms`).
- **Bass & Top**: `topTens(ch,next,minor)` = tensions by the chord's place in the key (IIm7, VIm7: 9, 11 · IIIm7: 11 · IMaj7: 9, 13 · IVMaj7: 9, ♯11, 13 · a dominant to a major chord: 9, 13 · to a minor chord a 5th below: ♭9, only there · ♭II7 and ♭VII7: 9, ♯11, 13). Root on top of a Maj7 = played as a 6 chord (`ch.as6`, said in the explanation). Answer rows are fixed (`topRow(names)`; the degree tokens always hold ♭2 ♭3 ♭6 ♭7; no “6” in level 5). `ctName`. Level 7's target is the bass as played.
- **The voice may use its method** (`micGradeLine`, `LS.last`): a voice question (`q.voice`, set on every mic question of `VOICE_GAMES`) with one note asked is answered by **the last note held**; the take ends 1.5 s after a held note or on Space; silence alone ends it after 5 s. With several notes: exactly one extra note at the front that is the tonic (`q.tonicPc`) is not counted.
- **Any octave, as a whole** (`q.contour`, `micGradeC`, `contourWhy`, `lineSlots`): graded as exact pitches against the target moved by 0, ±1, ±2, ±3 octaves, the best counts; the feedback names the first move that went the wrong way (`r.shapeWhy`). Set by `lineFlags` in a pass over `GAMES` (`CONTOUR_GAMES` = tunes, cr, sing, sightsing, outer, eardeg, eariv): every octave-free question with a line of two or more notes; not single notes, chords, or “the same pitch twice”. `micListen` counts exact slots for these questions; `tuneTimeSlots` no longer merges a note with its octave.
- **Other fixes**: Sing the Page guarantees each level's subject (`o.must`: level 5 the 7 below or the tonic above, 6 a skip, 8 one of ♭6 ♭7 raised 7); level 9 is named “With written rhythm (pitches checked)” · Tunes by Ear: God Rest Ye Merry, Gentlemen split after the 1 (keys unchanged), Scarborough Fair and Drunken Sailor are `md:'Dorian'` in explanations, level 10 is “All the stored phrases” · Call & Response 7 always has a chromatic note; a ♯6 that would need a double sharp keeps the plain name and its label (D, ♭7).
- **Decisions the brief did not settle** (told to the user):
  - Sing It 3 (“a 5th above”) has one target note, so the octave of a lone answer cannot be judged. The check that exists: **if the given note is sung first, the answer has to lie above it** (`q.from`).
  - Sing It 10–11 (arpeggios): the line would span up to an 11th, so **each chord may start in either octave** (`q.targets` = every combination); inside a chord the notes go up from the root.
  - **Silence**: the brief says a voice take ended after 2.5 s. That was true of one-note takes only (now 5 s); a line already had 7 s and keeps it. The texts say seven seconds.
  - The low-string allowance (a target at or below B♭2 heard an octave up) applies only to the line in the octave it sounds in: in a moved octave it let a leap turned over pass.
  - Hear the Mode 6: ♯4 left the row (never right, as “1st” in level 5). Hear the Progression 17: the line cliché ends with V7 to make five chords. Level 19 plays a cadence before the melody (with nothing printed the key has to be heard).
  - Level names changed with their content: Hear the Progression 13 “Blues forms & the turnaround”, 14 “Spot the changed chord”; Tunes by Ear 10; Sing the Page 9.
  - Sing It 8: both lines are shown as degrees of the key (guide tones 4 4 3 / 1 7 7); the bass target is the bass as played (up a 4th twice).
- **Where the brief did not match the code**: one bass line cannot lie in C2–C4 (four roots falling 14 half steps from D♭: it starts on C♯4; the sweep allows exactly that) · “a span of 14 or less has no step over a 5th” holds for the line taken by the shorter way; a longer line that is folded back (the turnaround with tritone substitutes in F and F♯) has one larger step.
- **Not done**: Ionian's colour note (7) and the fourth answer of Hear the Chord 8 (the reviews call them defensible) · the 3rd of VI7 on top is still written ♭2 (it is ♯1; `DEG_ORDER` has no ♯1) · pickups and meter in Tunes by Ear 7–10, partial credit in dictation, the cents tolerance, strict rhythm in Call & Response: later builds.
- **Sweep** (block “THE REVIEW OF THE EAR GAMES: BUILD 1” at the end of `tests/sweep.js`): the bass rules in twelve keys; every item of the list above that can be tested (prompts and rows that do not change with the answer, five chords and the voiced lines, degree moves against half steps, tensions in the key, the 6 chord, the guaranteed subjects, labels); the voice (tonic then the note passes, a wrong last note fails, a leading tonic passes, a leading wrong note fails); the shape (a phrase an octave up or down passes, a leap turned over fails with the move named, Happy Birthday's third phrase without its octave leap fails, a 5th above answered a 4th below fails, single notes unchanged, no contour outside the seven games).
- **Not tested with a voice or a guitar: all of it.** The listening rule for one sung note (`LS.last`) has not heard a real take; the built-in detector's octave slips on a voice now cost a line where they used to be forgiven.

## The review of the ear games: build 2 (23 Nov 2026, brief `.claude/ear-build-2.md`; built in the working tree, not committed)
“The missing joins”: ten levels, **all appended** (Sing It 12 → 15, Hear the Progression 19 → 24, Bass & Top 8 → 10). No insert, no renumbering, no migration, no existing item key changed. **Nothing was sung or played on a real guitar**: sweep, sampled questions and notes read as text, every new level started headless and muted (`tests/ear_start.py`, which picks the input each level is asked in; `tests/john_start.py` forces guitar input on every play game and so cannot start a screen level of Hear the Progression or Bass & Top), and the synthetic player (`tests/john_player.py`) on every guitar or voice level: all PASS.
The code is the block “THE REVIEW OF THE EAR GAMES: BUILD 2” (just before “FROM THE TWO HARMONY BOOKS' CONTENTS”); the sweep block of the same name is at the end of `tests/sweep.js` (written from `/tmp/ear2/sw.js` by a script that doubles the backslashes).
### Sing It 13–15
- **13 “Hear it, sing it back”** `singEchoQ(3|4|5)`: `echoLine(len, minor, noJoin)` makes the phrase with `ssLine` (steps, skips inside the tonic triad, never a note twice, ends on 1, 3 or 5, inside one octave; in minor the note under the tonic is the raised 7 and the line never steps between it and the ♭6), `ssNotes` names it. Heard after the cadence, degrees hidden, graded with `contour`, a leading tonic allowed. Keys `se-<M|m>-<degrees>` (a degree under the tonic carries `,`, the tonic above `'`): the key holds no key name.
- **14 “Sing it, then play it: a phrase”** `singPartsQ(3|4)` and **15 “A tune phrase: sing it, then play it”** `singTuneQ()` (pool `ST_POOL`: 37 phrases of 19 tunes). Both go through `partsQ`: the target is the phrase twice and the question carries **`q.parts = {n}`**. `micGrade` sends it to **`micGradeParts`**: the heard notes (a pitch struck twice in a row counted once) are cut in two at every possible place, the first half is graded as the sung line (`micGradeLine`, so one leading tonic is allowed), the second as the played line (`micGradeC`), each in any octave with its shape; the best cut counts, a tie goes to the later cut (one phrase alone is the sung part). `r.partWhy` says which part was right and which was not, `r.partOk = [sung, played]`. Keys `sp-<M|m>-<degrees>`, `st-<tune id>-<phrase index>`. The answer screen shows one place on the neck (`fretPhrase`).
- Level 9 (`singPlayQ`, `repeats`, one note) is unchanged; the phrase levels do not use `repeats`: the listener counts exact pitches (the `contour` path), and the join is always a new note.
### Hear the Progression 20–24
- **20 “Seventh chords of the key”**: `earOneQ(DIA_TRI, false, hint)` (keys `ep1-<numeral>`) and `earFreeQ(DIA_TRI, false, 4, 1)` (the new fourth argument: a cadence first, so the four chords may begin anywhere; keys `epf-…`).
- **21 “The cycle in sevenths”**: `earCycleQ(minor, 4, 1)` over `CYC7_MAJ` / `CYC7_MIN` (keys `epc7-…`), a cadence first.
- **22 “Secondary dominants: where is it going?”** `earSecQ('to'|'write')`, `secPhrase()` (`SD_T`): one secondary dominant, its target straight after it, two other diatonic sevenths (not VIIm7♭5), neighbours never on one root, ending on IMaj7 or V7. Keys `esd-<II…VI>` and `esdw-<numerals>`. Written by its root with `bnum` (VI7 for V7/II), spelled with `anChordOf`; `outsideOf(k, chord)` names the notes outside the key for the explanation and the notes' table.
- **23 “Major or minor II–V–I”** `ear251Q('which'|'write')`, `C251`, `C251_REL`: keys `e251-<M|m>`, `e251w-<M|m>` (which cadence comes first).
- **24 “Eight bars by ear”** `earEightQ()`, material **`E8`** (computed when the script loads; `E8_OUT` lists what was left out and the first chord that could not be named), `e8Kind`, `e8Make`, `e8Row`, `playBars` (a click on beat 1 of every bar, each chord struck where it begins, the bass a `bassLine`). Keys `e8-<form id>-<first bar>` (the id is the form's name in lower case with hyphens, `e8Slug`).
- **The ten spans that qualify**: the first eight bars of the basic, quick-change, jazz and minor blues (`BLUES_FORMS`); Rhythm changes bridge 1–8; Autumn Leaves 1–8 and 17–24; Fly Me to the Moon 1–8; Blue Bossa 1–8; Take the A Train 17–24 (the bridge). Left out, with the chord that decides it: the jazz blues of `FORMS` and the Bird blues (Vm7, the IIm7 of V7/IV; ♯IV°7), rhythm changes A (slash chords), All The Things You Are (a major seventh chord on III, ♭III°7 and the modulations), A Train's A section (II7♯11), Satin Doll (♭VIm7, Vm7), Blue Bossa 9–16 (the II–V–I of ♭II), Autumn Leaves 25–32 (IV7, ♭VIIm7). **Every qualifying span has one chord a bar**: the “two tokens for a bar with two chords” of the brief never occurs today. The code handles it, and the prompts are worded from the data (`E8_TWO`, `E8_BARS`).
- `TOK_ORDER` gained the seventh chords of the minor key and the secondary dominants (the order of the older tokens is unchanged); `epSym` writes the quality as the numerals do (Maj7).
### Bass & Top 9–10
- **9 “The bass of eight bars”** `outerEightQ(false)`: the same spans and sound, the bass written as degrees (row `OB8_ROW`, fixed), key `ob8-<form id>-<first bar>`.
- **10 “Play the bass of eight bars”** `outerEightQ(true)` (guitar): one note where each chord begins, a root that stays is not played again, `metro` = the player's tempo held to 60–72, `contour` and time. Key `ob8p-<form id>-<first bar>`.
### Goals, notes
- `GOALS.B` stage 5: `['sing',13,15]`, `['outer',9,10]` (`GOAL_WHY` `sing:13`, `outer:9`); appendix `earprog 17–24`. Ten `LEVEL_NOTES`, three `desc` lines, a rule line on each lesson card, the level-page note of Sing It, and chapters: Sing It “From the ear to the voice to the hand”; Hear the Progression “Seventh chords of the key”, “The cycle in sevenths”, “Secondary dominants by ear”, “Major or minor II–V–I”, “Eight bars by ear”; Bass & Top “The bass of a whole section” (all tables computed).
### Decisions the brief did not settle (told to the user)
- **13**: three generators as the brief says (three, four, five notes); each draws a minor phrase one time in three, so “major, then minor” is a mix inside each generator, not a second set.
- **15**: phrases of three to six slots (the brief: at most six), and, as in 14, never one that begins and ends on the same note name.
- **23, “which one”: no cadence is played before it** (the brief says every question follows a cadence; a cadence would give the answer). The tonic is named for both. **23, written: both orders are written in the numerals of the major key** (IIm7 – V7 – IMaj7 and VIIm7♭5 – III7(♭9) – VIm), after a cadence in that key. Written each in its own key, the named key alone would have given the answer.
- **20 and 21** play a cadence first (the brief's “all questions are heard after a cadence”), so the four free chords need not start on I.
- **24**: the forms are played in a key drawn like the other dictations (`EP_KEYS`), not in the key they are stored in, and the form is named only in the explanation. The strict reading of “a chord the app already names (♭VII7, IVm, subV7)” was taken: exactly those three, plus I7 and IV7 inside a twelve-bar blues (the brief names the blues as material). Related IIm7 chords and ♯IV°7 are not on that list, which is what leaves the jazz blues of `FORMS` out. Minor keys: the chords of the key only (V7 and Vm7 both). Spans are bars 1–8, 9–16, 17–24, 25–32; spans with the same numerals are kept once. Bar length 2 s.
- **Bass & Top 10**: the line is set above B♭2 (`BPO.lowOct`): lower, the low-string allowance of the grader would let a leap turned over pass. The prompt says strings 5 and 4 are the safer place. The eight bars are heard at the same speed as in level 9 and played at 60–72.
- **The two-part take** uses the built-in pitch detector for both parts, as level 9 does (the voice games do not load Basic Pitch).
- `tests/ear_start.py` is new (a copy of `john_start.py` that chooses the input per level).
### Could not do, or not done
- Nothing in the list was left out. Not verified: a real voice followed by a real guitar in one take (where the detector puts the join, breath noise between the parts), and the balance of click and piano in the eight bars. Level 24 has 72 seconds on the clock; a replay stops the clock while it plays, and the third replay counts as a hint (the level note's “one hearing for the bass, one for the qualities, one to check” is the first hearing and two replays).

## The review of the ear games: build 3 (23 Nov 2026, brief `.claude/ear-build-3.md`; built in the working tree, not committed)
“Chords as a guitarist plays them, real lines, real meters”: nine levels **appended** (Hear the Chord 9 → 11, Call & Response 12 → 13, Tunes by Ear 10 → 11, Hear the Degree 11 → 13, Hear the Interval 11 → 12, Hear the Mode 8 → 10) and six existing levels rebuilt in place. No insert, no renumbering, no migration; no existing item key scheme changed. **Nothing was sung or played on a real guitar**: sweep, sampled questions and notes read as text, every new or changed level started headless and muted (`tests/ear_start.py`), and the synthetic player (`tests/john_player.py`, now reading `q.cinN`) on the guitar levels of Call & Response and Tunes by Ear: all PASS.
The code is the block between `/*E3_BEGIN*/` and `/*E3_END*/` (“THE REVIEW OF THE EAR GAMES: BUILD 3”, just before “FROM THE TWO HARMONY BOOKS' CONTENTS”); the sweep block of the same name is between `/*E3SW_BEGIN*/` and `/*E3SW_END*/` at the end of `tests/sweep.js` (no template literals in it; backslashes doubled).
### Hear the Chord
- **Level 8 is context only** (`earD2Q` left it; it stays in 7). `earCtxQ` was rewritten over **`ECTX`**: four patterns, each in two versions that differ in one chord (IIm7 – V7 – IMaj7 / I6 · I – VI7 / VIm7 – IIm7 – V7 · IIm7♭5 – V7♭9 – Im / IIm7 – V7 – Im · V7 / V7sus4 – IMaj7). One version is heard, the roots are named as numerals, and the answer comes from a fixed row of eight (`ECTX_ROW`, short labels `chLab`). Keys `ectx-<id>`: `plain major II–V–I` keeps its id; the three other old ids (minor II–V–I with Im7, the blues I, the Maj7 on V) are no longer asked and stay as history. `ectxMake(vi, key)` gives the chords and the computed difference (“A7 has C♯ where Am7 has C”); `ctxSemis`/`ctxNotes` know 7sus4 (the Tension Grips' formula).
- **10 “Your own grips: shells and drop-2”** `earGripQ('shell'|'d2')` (three generators: shell, drop-2, drop-2), keys `ecg-<shell|d2>-<quality>`, `q.ecg`. Grips from `shellGrip` (root on 6 or 5) and `genDrop2` (sets 1–4 and 2–5, any inversion), piano in guitar register, the grip shown as a chord box after the answer.
- **11 “Dominant colours”** `earDomQ(names)`, row `ECD` = 7, 9, 13, 7sus4, 7♭9, 7♯9, 7♯5, 7♯11; three generators (the four natural ones · the plain 7 with the four altered ones · all eight), keys `ecd-<name>`, `q.ecd`. `ecdGrips`: `tgGrips` for every colour that has a Tension Grip; the plain 7 has none and is laid out from its formula as those grips are (root, ♭7, 3rd, 5th, the root on string 6 or 5). `ECD_COL` = the colour note of each, `ECD_WHY` the sentence.
### Call & Response
- **Level 8** (`crChordPhrase`, `crChordIdx`): swing eighths over one chord; every note on a beat is a chord tone; a note between two beats is a scale note reached by step and left by step, or another chord tone; first and last are chord tones; no pitch twice in a row. **Level 9** (`crLongQ`, `crLongLine`, `crLadder`, `crArp`): eight quarter notes, every note a chord tone of its bar moving to a neighbouring chord tone; the join is a half or whole step into a chord tone of V7; the 7th of IIm7 falls a half step to the 3rd of V7 in about three questions in four; nothing under C3. Level numbers, lengths, tempos and key schemes (`cr7-…`, `crl-…`) unchanged.
- **13 “The jazz line book”** `crJazzQ('ol'|'olm'|'enc')`: the question of `outlineQ` or `encCadQ` is taken as made and turned into a heard-once question (target hidden, the chords played under the line: two beats a chord for the outlines, a bar a chord for the turn line). Eight items, the same in every key: `crj-ol-<shape>`, `crj-olm-<shape>` (three shapes each), `crj-enc-p`, `crj-enc-d`. A key in which the line would go under C3 is drawn again. `q.crj`.
- **`rhyStrict`** on `crBlues` (levels 6, 10, 11), level 8, level 9 and level 13.
- **The lowest notes**: `crBlues` plays a lick an octave higher when it would go under C3 (`fitWindow(mids, pmax)` now takes the highest first fret; the figure falls back to `alSVG` above fret 12), else the key is dropped for that lick; in practice six licks move, all in E, and they fit at or below fret 7; no key is dropped for the low notes. `crPhrase` level 5 moves a phrase up under C3 (was under E2).
### Tunes by Ear
- **Levels 7–10 in the tune's own meter** (`tuneMeter(t, i)`, `tuneLen`): from `SR_TUNE[id]` the bar (4, 3, or 2 dotted-quarter beats for 6/8), the phrase's place in the bar from the stored pickup and the lengths of the phrases before it, `pk` (how much lies before its first bar line), `off` (the rest that fills the counted bar in front), `cn` (the count-in: whole bars, at least four clicks: 4, or 6 in 3/4). The grid starts at `off`; `q.cinN`, `q.clickBar` (0 = no accent: Swing Low, Scarborough Fair, God Rest Ye have no entry), `q.tune = {…, id, i, bar, pk, off, cn}`. The heard version has the same clicks and accents.
- **The count-in is general now**: `micListen` reads `q.cinN` (default 4) and `q.clickBar` (accent every that many clicks; undefined = every fourth, as before), `mTick(r, i, acc)`, `cinRun`, `cinHTML(n, q)` (boxes numbered by the bar: 1 2 3 1 2 3). The strummed branch still counts four.
- **11 “No key given”** `tunePhraseQ({nokey:true})`: a random key of `MKEYLIST`, no cadence, no key in the prompt, `octaveFree` always (so `contour`), not in time; the explanation opens with the key. Keys `tu-<id>-<i>k`, `q.nokey`.
### Hear the Degree
- **Dictation lines** (`dictLine`, `dictNotes`, `earDictQ(len | [lens], degs, {chrom})`): `ssLine` lines (steps, skips inside the tonic triad, never a note twice, ending on 1, 3 or 5; two notes may start anywhere, longer lines start on 1, 3 or 5). Level 9: exactly one chromatic note, set in front of the scale note it leans on (`DICT_CHR`: ♯4 before 5, ♭7 before 6, ♭3 before 2), generators `[3,4]` notes and 5 notes. Keys `edc-<degrees>` as before. `q.dict`.
- **12 “Minor-key dictation”**: `earMinDictQ(3|4)` (`echoLine(len, true)`, tokens `EDM_TOK`, at least one of ♭3 ♭6 ♭7 or the raised 7, keys `edcm-<degrees>`), then `melDictQ({minor:1, lo:-1, hi:5, …})` (keys `mdm-…`): tokens `MDM_TOK` = 1 2 ♭3 4 5 ♭6 7, **`q.mel.keys`** maps the digit keys (3 → ♭3, 6 → ♭6), `rwPitch` refuses a token the bar does not have, and the answer staff is **`mdmStaff`** = `scoreSVG` with each note spelled as its degree (C minor: B♮).
- **13 “All twelve”**: `earDegree(DEG_ORDER)`.
### Hear the Interval 12, Hear the Mode
- **Intervals in a key** `earKeyIvQ(write)`: a cadence, two notes of the major scale, rising, falling or together; generator 1 names the interval from the row of twelve (key `eik-<form>-<lower>-<upper>`), generator 2 writes both degrees in the order heard, the lower first when together (key `eikw-…`). `keyIvPairs(semi)` for the notes' table.
- **`MODE_VAMPS2`** (computed): after the home chord of the first vamp, diatonic triads of the mode (no diminished one, no root the first vamp uses) tried in the order II, VII, IV, V, III, VI: the first chord, else the first two, that hold the colour note and leave no other mode possible. Dorian Im7 – IIm · Phrygian Im – ♭VIIm · Lydian IMaj7 – VIIm · Mixolydian I7 – Vm · Aeolian Im – IVm – Vm · Ionian IMaj7 – IIm – V. `earVampQ` draws either (key `ev-<mode>` unchanged, `q.vamp`). `modePlayQ` (level 7) still uses the first.
- **9 “One root, two modes”** `earTwoModesQ('mode'|'note')`, `playTwoModes`, `MODE_FAM`, `MODE2_NEAR`, `MODE2_ROW`, `mdDegs`, `mdDiffs`: keys `em2-<first>-<second>`, `em2n-<first>-<second>`. **10 “The mode of a line”** `earModeLineQ`, `modeLine`, `playModeLine`, `mdTell` (the fewest degrees that settle the mode), `mdFits`: key `eml-<mode>`.
### Goals, notes
- `GOALS.B` stage 5: `['tunes',9,11]`, `['eardeg',12,13]` (`GOAL_WHY` `tunes:9` extended, `eardeg:12` new); appendix `eariv 7–12`, `earchord 5–11`, `earmode 1–10`, `cr 5–13`; goal A's appendix `cr 10–13`.
- Level notes for the nine new levels and rewritten ones for Hear the Chord 8, Call & Response 8 and 9, Tunes by Ear 7, Hear the Degree 9, Hear the Mode 4; `desc` of all six games; a rule line on five lesson cards; chapters: Hear the Chord “Quality in context” (rewritten), “Your own grips: shells and drop-2”, “Dominant colours” · Call & Response “The jazz line book” (and chapters 5, 8, 9 corrected, figures included) · Tunes by Ear “The meter and the pickup” inside “In time”, “No key given” · Hear the Degree “Minor-key dictation”, “All twelve” · Hear the Interval “Intervals in a key” · Hear the Mode the second vamps, “One root, two modes”, “The mode of a line”. All tables computed.
### Decisions the brief did not settle, and where it did not match the code (told to the user)
- **Shells cannot tell seven qualities apart.** A shell is root, 3rd and 7th: the shell of m7♭5 is the same three notes as that of m7, and the shell of °7 the same as that of m6. The shell generator asks five (Maj7, 7, m7, 6, m6); m7♭5 and °7 are asked as drop-2 grips only. The sweep proves the two pairs.
- **A drop-2 grip alone cannot tell 6 from m7, or m6 from m7♭5** (C6 = Am7). The root is named and sounded alone, low, before the grip.
- **Level 8 of Hear the Chord**: one version is played (not both); the row names the version by the chord that can differ. The cadence to minor differs in two chords, as the brief wrote it.
- **“Every voicing holds exactly its chord's notes”** is true of shells and drop-2 grips; a Tension Grip leaves notes out (most have no 5th), so for level 11 the sweep proves: every note belongs to the formula, the root is in the bass, the 3rd (the 4th in the sus chord), the ♭7 and the colour note are there, no 13 holds an 11, and the plain 7 is exactly its formula.
- **Call & Response 9**: “a chord tone on every beat” with one note a beat means every note is a chord tone: the lines are arpeggios joined by a step. **Level 8**: an off-beat note may also be a chord tone (a third from the note before), so that a phrase of four or six notes can end on one.
- **The jazz line book**: the turn line is taken whole (25 notes, heard once; one unheard note is forgiven as in Arpeggio Runner). The displaced versions and the chromatic enclosures (one chord, not a II–V–I) are not in the book.
- **`['tunes',11,11]` could not be a step of its own**: the sweep refuses a game twice in one stage, and Tunes by Ear 9–10 is in stage 5. The step is `['tunes',9,11]` and its reason (`tunes:9`) carries the new paragraph; there is no `tunes:11`.
- **Tunes in time**: a later phrase has its own place in the bar (Joy to the World's second phrase has a pickup, Oh! Susanna's second does not); the sweep checks the stored pickup on the first phrase and on half and whole tunes. In 3/4 the count-in is two bars (six clicks), in 6/8 two bars of two.
- **Hear the Interval 12**: the written generator has its own key prefix (`eikw-`), so the two tasks are not one item. **Hear the Mode 9**: the first mode is never the answer and is not offered, so the first generator has two choices; the note question uses the four pairs that differ in one note and asks the degree as it is in the second scale. **10**: the line ends on 1, 3 or 5 (Locrian: 1 or ♭3).
- **Minor bar (Hear the Degree 12)**: 1 to ♭6 with the raised 7 under the tonic; ♭7 is asked in the lines, not in the bar (the digit 7 can then only mean the raised 7). In a line the ♭7 can stand under the upper tonic; only the note under the lower tonic is raised.
### Could not do, or not done
- Nothing in the list was left out. Not verified: any of it on a real guitar or by ear (the balance of the root note and the grip, the piano standing in for a guitar, whether 25 notes heard once is a fair ask). The three old `ectx-` items stop coming back. The synthetic player takes a random question; the takes that passed include Happy Birthday in 3/4 with its pickup (a six-click count-in), Danny Boy (pickup of a beat and a half), When the Saints (three beats) and Oh! Susanna.

## The review of the ear games: build 4 (23 Nov 2026, brief `.claude/ear-build-4.md`; built in the working tree, not committed)
“Singing from the page, intonation, the outer voices”: seven levels **appended** (Sing the Page 9 → 13, Sing It 15 → 16, Bass & Top 10 → 12), cents kept for every sung answer, and the top voice of Bass & Top made a line in place. No insert, no renumbering, no migration, no existing item key changed. **Neither singing game has ever been tested with a real voice, and nothing here was sung or played on a real guitar**: sweep, sampled questions and notes read as text, every new or changed level started headless and muted (`tests/ear_start.py`), the new notes figures looked at as rendered images, and the synthetic player (`tests/john_player.py`, which now also prints `cents`, the stored `ct` and the timing offsets) on Sing the Page 10, 11 (both generators), 12, 13, Sing It 16 (and 13, 14) and Bass & Top 8: all PASS.
The code is the block between `/*E4_BEGIN*/` and `/*E4_END*/` (right after `/*E3_END*/`), plus the intonation helpers beside `VOICE_GAMES` and `topLine` beside the Bass & Top code; the sweep block is between `/*E4SW_BEGIN*/` and `/*E4SW_END*/` at the end of `tests/sweep.js` (no template literals, no backslashes; written from `/tmp/ear4/sw.js` and proved by breaking the code nine ways: each break is caught).
### Sing the Page 10–13
- **10 “Wider skips”**: `singPageQ({id:10, gen, must, tell})` (`o.gen` = the level's own line maker, `o.tell` = a sentence for the explanation). `ssWide(len, lo, hi)`: a skip is two notes of one chord, I, IV or V7 (`SSW_CH`, scale steps mod 7; `ssInCh`), a 3rd to a 6th, never a tritone (`ssSkipOk`); **every skip is followed by a step back** (`ssStepBack`); every line has a skip of a 4th or more that is not inside the tonic triad (`ssWideOut`, `ssHasWide`); ends on 1, 3 or 5. Keys `ss-10-<steps>`. `ssSkipTell` names the wide skips (“5 up to 2, a 5th inside V7”).
- **11 “Four bars”**: `singPage4Q(minor)` (two generators), `ss4Line`: two phrases of seven notes (`SS4_DUR`), the first ending on the tonic triad, the second on the tonic, the join a step or a skip of at most a 5th. Major: each half is level 6 or level 10 material, at least one half level 10. Minor (A, E, D, G, **C, F**): tonic-triad skips, at least one of ♭6 ♭7 raised 7, never a step between the raised 7 and the ♭6 under it. Keys `ss-11-<steps>`, `ss-11m-<steps>`; `q.ss4`.
- **The staff of 11–13 is the score engine**: `ssScore(root, ns, dur, minor, {meter, lead})` = `scoreBars`/`scoreSVG` with every note spelled as its degree (the form of `mdmStaff`), so C minor prints B♮ and F minor E♮. Levels 1–10 keep `staffRhySVG`.
- **12 “Tunes from the page”**: `singTunePageQ()`, `sstPage(t, i, key)`, pool `sstPool()` (made on first use: 63 phrases of 21 tunes; left out: a phrase with fewer than three different notes in a row, and the three tunes without stored bar lines). The phrase is written with its meter, bar lines and pickup (`tuneMeter`: the rest in front is `off`), in a key in which it sounds inside A2–E4 (`SST_LO`, `SST_HI`; keys `SST_KEYS`, signatures up to four), written an octave higher as the game writes its lines. Minor, modal and blues tunes take the minor signature; other notes carry their sign. Key `sst-<tune id>-<phrase index>` (one item in every key); `contour`; a repeated note is one slot; the name is in the explanation only. `q.sst`.
- **13 “In time”**: `singTimeQ()`, `ssrRhythm` (two bars of quarters, halves on beats 1 and 3, eighth pairs on a beat; always at least one pair; the last note a quarter or longer), a level 9 line. `metro: 60`, a four-click count-in, `grid`, and two new grading fields read by `micGradeOne`: **`q.beatTol`** (how far a note may start from its place, in beats; 0.25 where absent) = `SS_TOL` 0.3, and **`q.timeAll`** (every heard note must be on time; the three-in-four rule elsewhere). Key `ssr-<values>-<steps>`; `q.ssr`. No leading tonic (a clicked take is graded by `micGradeC`); what is sung in the count-in is dropped as everywhere.
### Intonation
- **`centsOff(mf, m)`** (distance of a heard float pitch from a target, any octave), **`takeCents(q, r)`** = the mean absolute cents of the matched sung notes (a two-part take: the sung part; “sing it, then play it”: the first note). `perfLog` stores it as **`ct`** (one decimal) in the performance log entry of the voice games; the entry goes to the LOGDB archive as it is. Nothing else in the store changes.
- **The level rows of the two singing games** show it as their second bar: `lvKind` returns `'cents'` for a voice game, `lvModeStats` carries `ct` (the last 20 entries of the level), `lvRow` draws “Pitch ±N cents”, green mark at `CENTS_STD` = 20. **Without a stored value the bar is empty and is not held against the level** (answers before this build carry no cents); with values over 20 the level is not at the master standard.
- **Sing It 16 “Match the note”** (`singMatchQ`, `open:true`, key `sm-<pitch class 0–11>`): one note in A2–E4, sung back in any octave; `q.cents = MATCH_CENTS` (35; its comment says it is to be set on the user's voice). `micGradeLine` (one note, the last held): right note name and within `q.cents`, else `r.centsOut`; `r.cents` is shown by `micFeedbackExtra` with its direction (flat or sharp). Other levels keep the nearest half step.
### Bass & Top
- **The top voice is a line** (`topLine(seq, minor)`, used by `outerChords`, so by levels 3–8, 11 and by `earSongQ`): the first note as before (`pickTop`, G4–F♯5); each next chord takes, from its tones and `topTens`, a note within a whole step of the last (`topAllowed`); **at most one leap** (over a whole step) in a phrase, on purpose now and then (a chord tone a 3rd to a 5th away) or where nothing lies within a step; a line that never moves over three or more chords is drawn again. Range E4–G5 (`TOP_LO`, `TOP_HI`). `ch.topM` = the pitch (`voiceTop(pcs, pc, topM)`, `playOuter(chs, len, t0, bassM)`); level 8's target is `topM`. Level numbers, keys and saved data unchanged.
- **11 “The bass is not always the root”** `outerInvQ('deg' | 'ct')`: twelve stored progressions, `OB_INV` (eight with first and second inversions) and `OB_PED` (four with a pedal: `'T'` tonic, `'D'` dominant), each chord `[half steps, quality, bass]`. `obMake`, `obBassPc`, `obTok` (V/3, I/5; a pedal that is not a chord tone: “V over 1”), `obSym` (G/B). First generator: the bass as degrees (row 1–7, key `oi-<id>`, all twelve); second: root, 3rd or 5th for each chord, the chords named by their numerals without the bass (key `oic-<id>`, the eight of `OB_INV`). `q.ob`.
- **12 “How the two lines move”** `outerMotionQ()`, `omMake`, **`motionOf(b1, t1, b2, t2, tonic)`**: oblique (one stays), contrary, parallel (same direction and the same interval by number, counted in scale steps: `scStep`), similar. Two diatonic triads, root position or inverted; the kind is drawn first, so the four answers are equally frequent; parallel examples are 3rds, 6ths and 10ths. Fixed row of four (`OM_KINDS`), key `om-<motion>-<chord>-<chord>`, `q.om`. No cadence is played.
### Goals, notes
- `GOALS.B` stage 5: `['outer',9,12]` (`GOAL_WHY` `outer:9` extended); appendix `sightsing 1–13` and `['sing',16,16]`. Seven `LEVEL_NOTES` (and Bass & Top 4 rewritten: the top is a line), `desc` of the three games, rule lines on the three lesson cards, the level-page notes of both singing games, and chapters: Sing the Page “Wider skips: inside IV and V7”, “Four bars”, “Tunes from the page”, “In time” · Sing It “In tune: cents, and Match the note” · Bass & Top “The bass is not always the root”, and “Both voices and how they move” now teaches four motions with a computed table and names level 12. All tables and figures computed.
### Decisions the brief did not settle (told to the user)
- **Level 10**: thirds inside the three chords are allowed as small skips beside the 4ths, 5ths and 6ths; the step back applies to every skip. 4 and 7 (a tritone inside V7) are never joined.
- **Level 11**: 14 notes (six quarters and a half, twice). C and F minor were added so that a natural sign occurs; the old staff cannot draw one, so levels 11–13 use the score engine.
- **Level 12**: every note is spelled as its degree (not by `srTunePage`'s rule); 6/8 is written as Sight Reading writes it. The phrase is sounded nowhere: a cadence and the tonic only.
- **Level 13**: `timeAll` (every note, not three in four); eighth pairs are in every line; no leading tonic.
- **Cents**: the level row counts against the master standard only once values exist. `sing:16` has no step in a goal (the brief names none): it is in goal B's appendix. Goal R's appendix still says Sing the Page 1–9 (the brief names goal B only).
- **Level 16** draws a pitch, not a key; a pitch class is not asked twice running.
- **Bass & Top 11**: the second task shows the numerals (roots) and asks only the eight inversion progressions; a pedal's bass is often no chord tone. All in major keys. The bass that sounds is `bassLine` of the written bass notes (the written bass by the shorter way), not of the roots. **12**: “the same interval” is by number (a major 10th to a minor 10th is parallel); some chords are inverted so that the bass can hold or step.
- `['outer',11,12]` could not be a step of its own (a game once a stage): the step is `['outer',9,12]`.
### Could not do, or not done
- Nothing in the list was left out. **Not verified: any of it with a voice.** The time limit of level 13 (0.3 of a beat; a sung note has no sharp attack, and the built-in detector has never placed a sung onset against a click; the click also reaches the microphone without headphones) and the 35 cents of level 16 are first settings. The synthetic player's pure tones measure 0 cents and land 7 to 34 ms early.
- `lvKind` lives in the views half and is not reached by the sweep: the cents row was checked in a headless page (empty without data, “±15 cents” green, 31 cents not green).

## Scale Runner: the fingering diagram can be hidden (24 Nov 2026, user: “the fretboard layout gives away the layout… I only need which fret/string to start and with which finger”)
Option “Hide the fingering diagram: show only where to start” on the level page (`opts.hideNeck`, Scale Runner only, off by default). `scVis(diagram, position, first spot)` wraps the `visual` of `scQ`, `scOctQ` and `scModeQ`: with the option on the question shows a card (`.scstart`): the first note's string and fret, its finger (`scFingerTxt`: fret − position + 1, or finger 1 / 4 “stretched” one fret outside), and the position; the diagram is returned as `q.after` and appears with the answer. Nothing else changes (keys, grading). Checked in Chrome on levels 1, 6, 10 and 16 and on the answer screen. Modes, Blues Boxes and Along the Neck still always show their maps.

## On the guitar a level opens on accuracy; the speed gate is for the screen (24 Nov 2026, user: Reading 7 at 100 % on the guitar, 99 answers, and level 8 locked with “6 more timed answers to unlock”)
The speed gate of the recall games (`fluency`: six clean answers timed against a target, median at or under it) counts screen answers only; a played answer has no such target (`fluTarget` is 0), so **in guitar input the gate could never be met**: `g.ht[7]` stayed empty however much was played. Earlier levels had opened only because they had also been answered on screen. `fluency()` now returns “not needed” when the game is in guitar input (`guitarMode`); the guitar row keeps showing its own speed. `passCatchUp(gid)` (top of `vLobby`, round levels only) opens the next level when the frontier's record already meets the rule, which until now was only looked at when an answer came in. **A second fault in the same picture**: the Screen row showed “93 played, 76 %” with no screen answer given at that level (`g.lc[7]` = 0 screen, 99 guitar): the stored spots (`sf-<string>-<fret>`) are shared between first and second position, and the row's fallbacks read another level's answers; `lvModeStats` now shows nothing for the screen when the level's own count says it was answered on the guitar only. On the user's store: Reading 7 → 8 when its page opens, no other game moves.

## Reading: one position a level (24 Nov 2026, user: “level 7 changes position a lot… why not make them separate”; 11 → 13 levels)
Level 7 “Positions II, III, IV” drew a new position for every question. The mixing taught nothing: it came from merging three steps into one. Now **7 Second position · 8 Third position · 9 Fourth position**, each one generator (`sightPosQ` with its window); 10 Fifth (old 8) · 11 Seventh · 12 Ninth, high ledger lines · 13 Two notes at once. `REORDERS29` (flag `r55`; its block stands **after** the `moveReading` call in `G()`, because that move writes the 11-level numbering) shifts old 8–11 to 10–13. `GOALS.R`: `sight` 7–10 (stage 3), 11–13 (stage 4); `GOAL_WHY` key `sight:11`. Level notes for the three positions name their stretch notes (III: B, E, A, B at fret 7; IV: G, C, F, G at fret 3; checked against `scPos`). On the user's store: 1,261 answers before and after, frontier 7 both times (now second position only), nothing in skip, other games untouched, second load a no-op; a made-up player on old 9 with 10 open lands on 11 with 12 open and 8, 9 open-not-passed. **Sight Reading 7 “Positions II to IV” still draws lines in position II or IV from question to question** (two generators): told to the user, not changed.

## Note spelling is typed (24 Nov 2026, user: “with the current method I can click one box in between for a 7 chord. I want to be aware of the note I am inputting”)
The note buttons stood in alphabetical order, so a seventh chord was “click, skip one, click” and a scale every box in a row: the eye answered. Every note-spelling question (`ui:'spell'` without `tokens`: Chord Spelling, Intervals & Keys, Chord–Scale Reflex) now shows only the slots and one line of typing help; the buttons come back with “Show the note buttons” (`spBtnOn()`, `STORE.spBtn`, action `spbtn`; default on only where the pointer is a finger). Typing was already there: the letter, `-` or `[` then the letter for a flat, `=` or `#` for a sharp, twice for a double, Backspace. **Speed target** (`fluTarget`): 1.5 s + **0.7 s** a typed note (1 s a note with the buttons on): a triad with its root given 2.9 s (was 3.5), a seventh chord 3.6 (4.5), a mode 5.7 (7.5). Questions with their own `q.flu` are unchanged. Stored ratios (`g.ht`) were measured against the old target and are **not rescaled**: each level's window is twelve answers and renews itself; a passed level stays passed. Token questions (numerals, degrees) keep their buttons. Checked in Chrome on a copy of the live store: A♭7 typed note by note passes, the toggle brings the buttons and the 4.5 s target back. Not built: the scale question “which notes are sharp or flat” (offered, not chosen).

## Reading Rhythm 16 “Everything so far, in 4/4” (24 Nov 2026, user request; 28 → 29 levels)
After the three sixteenth levels (13 to 15) the ladder changed meter without ever mixing the 4/4 figures; the old “Everything so far” (now level 20) comes after 6/8, 12/8 and cut time. The new level 16 is one stream of every 4/4 figure of levels 1 to 15 (`cCells` of 23 cells: eighths and rests, ties and dots, the three triplets, syncopation, sixteenths, the dotted figures, sixteenth rests). `REORDERS28` (flag `r54`, after `r53`, the same code; it also sets `g.last` to the new level when that lies under the frontier) shifts the old levels 16 to 28 up by one. `GOALS.R`: `rhyread` 13–20, 21–25, 26, appendix 27–29; `GOAL_WHY` keys `rhyread:21`, `rhyread:26`. On the user's store: 3,914 answers and 3,226 clean before and after, frontier “6/8, with sixteenths” 16 → 17, the new level 16 open and not passed (skip [7, 9, 10] → [7, 9, 10, 16]), the level page opens on it, tempos per level unchanged below 16, every other game byte-identical, second load a no-op.

## A frontier left behind by the old input-skip rule is put back (24 Nov 2026, user: Hear the Degree showed levels 2 to 5 open with nothing played on them)
Until 9 Nov `modeSkip` moved the frontier past a level the current input could not ask and wrote the levels it passed into `g.skip`; when the rule was removed, frontiers it had moved stayed (Hear the Degree: frontier 5, skip [1, 2, 3, 4], all 25 answers on level 1, in every backup since 10 Nov). One-off `mig.fb1` in `G()`: when the lowest level of `g.skip` that is not passed by its own record has nothing played above it, the frontier goes back to it and the skip list is cut there. A frontier with answers on or above it (the reorders' open-not-passed levels: Scale Runner, Triad Shapes, Drop-2, Hear the Progression) is left alone. On the user's store: Hear the Degree 5 → 1, no other game changes, no answer touched, second load a no-op.

## A continuous level at the master standard is passed (24 Nov 2026, user: Reading Rhythm 15 showed a green outline, 95 % and ±29 ms over 366 answers, and level 16 stayed locked)
In the continuous rhythm games a level opened only on 24 clean bars in one run (`grooveUnlock`). His best run at level 15 was 21 bars, so the level stood at the master standard (`lvStd`: accuracy and timing over the recent blocks, the green outline), which is meant to lie above the unlock, and was still not passed. `stdCatchUp(gid)` (called at the top of `vLobby` for games with continuous levels) moves the frontier past every frontier level whose row is green; the 24-bar run still opens a level at once during a session. Texts that state the rule (Pulse & Feel's level note, Reading Rhythm's notes, the masters' chat prompt) say both ways. On the user's store: Reading Rhythm 15 → 16, the other five rhythm games unchanged, second load a no-op.

## Six sets of levels from the course notes (22 Nov 2026, brief `.claude/marbin-levels-brief.md`; snapshot: tag `v-2026-11-22-before-marbin-levels`, store copy `backups/store-before-marbin-levels-2026-11-22.json`; built in the working tree, then committed and published at the user's word the same day)
**Re-read this section before each item.** The user's rules for the build: working tree only, no commit or push; every migration tested on a copy of the real store; after each item sweep `bad 0`, `pages_check` `all ok` and a note here; browser tests headless and muted; every note, fret and chord computed; if the brief cannot be done as written, stop and ask. The games are Fretwise's own material: no caption text, no video links, no teacher's name in them.
- **The migration, one for the four changed games**: `REORDERS26` (flag `r52`, in `G()` after `r51`), each entry `{n: old level count, m: old → new, nw: the new levels}`. The block remaps `opts.bpmL`, `altN` and `unl0` itself, calls `migrateReorder` (which covers `hist ht hs hm lc rl`, items, rounds, the log, `unl`, `last`), puts the frontier back on the level it was (`mp(was)`, the r50 rule; levels above stay in `unl`), and writes `g.skip` = the old list renumbered + the new levels under the frontier (open, not passed). The LOGDB archive keeps old level numbers, as in every earlier reorder.
- **Tests**: `tests/marbin_migtest.py` (the store copy through the page, headless, muted: totals per game before and after, other games byte-identical, second load a no-op, and made-up players at other frontiers) · `tests/marbin_start.py game:levels…` (starts the levels on the migrated copy, screenshots to `tests/out/mb-*.png`) · the sweep block “THE SIX SETS OF LEVELS FROM THE COURSE NOTES” at the end of `tests/sweep.js`.
### Item 1: triad diagonals (Triad Shapes, 10 → 14 levels), done
- **Ladder**: 1–5 as before · **6 The diagonal, in C major** · **7 Diagonals: any major, and minor** · **8 Roll the inversions** · 9 Voice leading (old 6) · 10 Every root (7) · 11 Diminished & augmented (8) · **12 Diagonals: augmented and diminished** · 13 Spread triads (9) · 14 Inversions up the neck, played (10). 6, 7, 8, 12 and 14 are guitar-only levels (the lobby's Switch banner, as level 14 had).
- **`triDiag(root, q, start)`** (block “TRIAD DIAGONALS”, after `triRunQ`): the tab, string 6 first. The starting chord tone on string 6, then on every next string the next chord tone above the last pitch. Octave: string-6 fret 8–15 where there is one, else the lower one, else the upper one when the lower would go below the nut. Helpers `tdParts` (midis, degree and name of a pitch, dots), `tdNeck`, `tdSlices` (which inversion each three strings are), `tdSym`, `tdWhy`, and for the notes `tdFig(root, q, start, shift)` / `tdTab`.
- **Generators**: `triDiagQ({roots,q,start})` the diagonal as an arpeggio, one note a click after a count-in, up six and back five (keys `tdg-<q>-<start>-<root>`); a diagonal that holds B2 or starts above fret 15 is not asked · `triRollQ({roots,q})` the diagonal as four chords on three neighbouring strings, up and back, seven strums two beats apart, **exact voicings** (no alternatives), only diagonals from fret 8–15 so every note is C3 or higher (`trl-…`) · `triDimQ({roots})` major, diminished, major on one string set in one inversion, three timed strums: the root stays, the 3rd and 5th fall one fret (`tdm-<inv>-<set>`) · `triOpenDiagQ()` the two spread triads on every other string of a diagonal (strings 6–4–2, then 5–3–1), two untimed strums (`sp-dg-…`).
- **Decisions**: the arpeggio and the chord levels use a count-in, not the free start of “Inversions up the neck” (the brief: every played level with a count-in) · level 6 = three generators (root, 3rd, 5th), level 12 = augmented in C, diminished in C, both on eight roots, C–C°–C in C and on eight roots · **the spread-triads level got the played generator**: in guitar mode that level already strums a named spread triad, so it still asks one action; it is untimed like the rest of that level · a diagonal with B2 in it is left out (the brief's “no target of B2”): E from the 3rd, G from the 3rd and the like are asked only as far as their other diagonals go · chord names in the roll are slash symbols (C, C/E, C/G), the augmented triad is written C+ and the diminished C° · B♭° is spelled B♭ D♭ F♭ (correct; a root that would need a double flat is not asked).
- **Why the roll is safe for the detector**: going up, the note that is let go is an octave under the new top note (ignored by `chordCheck` as a weaker lower octave); going back it is an octave over the new bass (an overtone). Still untested on a guitar.
- **Texts**: `LEVEL_NOTES.triads` (four new notes, the spread note's first line), the lesson card's rule, `LESSON_NOTES.triads`: a new chapter “The diagonals: every inversion in one line” (computed figures and table), “Their diagonals” and the major–diminished exercise in “Diminished and augmented”, “Spread triads on a diagonal”. Goal C: `triads 6–10` in stage 2, appendix `11–14`; `GOAL_WHY` `triads:6` rewritten.
- **Sweep**: `triDiag` equals the brief's table for C in four qualities and three starts; for every root: six notes, one a string, rising, chord tones in order, the upper three strings an octave above the lower three, each three neighbouring strings the close inversion in order, the augmented one fingering; the arpeggio (eleven notes, no B2, perfect passes, a wrong note and late notes fail); the roll (two notes stay, named inversions, perfect passes, late / wrong note / another inversion fail); major–diminished (root kept, the others one fret down, three majors fail); the open pair; which levels are guitar-only.
- **On the user's store** (copy of 22 Nov): Triad Shapes 887 answers before and after (730 clean, every total and count equal), frontier 7 → **10** (“Every root”), open and not passed 6 → **6, 7, 8, 9**, items and records of old 6 and 7 on 9 and 10, other games byte-identical, second load a no-op. (The brief said 863 answers: he has played since.)
- **Not played on a real guitar.**
### Item 2: connect the arpeggios (Arpeggio Runner levels 5–7), done
- **Levels** (after “II–V and II–V–I”): **5 Connect the triads** (Dm | G | C, a quarter note a beat) · **6 Connect the sevenths** (Dm7 | G7 | CMaj7, and the minor cadence Dm7♭5 | G7 | Cm6) · **7 Connected, in eighth notes** (triads, sevenths, the minor cadence, eight notes a chord). Each level: a C-only generator first, then the keys (`CON_MAJ`, `CON_MIN`).
- **The rule is in `soloGrade`** (a question carries `q.solo` with `spec.near`): chord tones only (`pool:'ct'`); inside a chord the note must be the neighbouring chord tone of the note before; at the first note of a new chord it must be one of the two **nearest tones of the new chord, just above or just below** the last note (`nearTones(prev, C)` → `[below, above]`; the same pitch is neither). A note whose predecessor was not heard is not judged. The existing rules stay: never a note twice, three different notes in a bar, one unheard note in sixteen (none in the twelve-note levels). The message names the rule and the two notes that would have been right, and how far the played note was.
- **Code** (block “CONNECT THE ARPEGGIOS”, before `SOLO`): `nearTones`, `nearLine(chs, lens, div, dirs, lo, hi, start)` (one line that keeps the rules: the example and the sweep's proof; `dirs[b]` = up or down at chord b), `conChord(r, q)` (a chord object in `soloGrade`'s form, tones spelled by `degSpell`), `CON` (specs `tri sev min` and `tri8 sev8 min8`), `connectQ(spec, {keys})` (keys `con-<spec>-<key>`), and for the notes `conExample()` and `conRows()`.
- **Decisions**: its own generator, not a `SOLO` entry (triads and m6 have no `SOLO_SC` scale; the grader is shared) · the prompt is in the tag form with the rules in the small line, and the chord tones are shown before playing (spelling is not what the level tests) · quarters at 72 or slower, eighths at 66 or slower · the example stays at C3 or higher and the hint says to · the band (`q.vamp`) is there but opt-in, as in Improvise · **his third level (scales through the changes) is not built**: Improvise's scale-line levels cover it.
- **Texts**: three level notes, the lesson card's and the lobby's line, a chapter “Connecting the arpeggios” in `LESSON_NOTES.arp` (the staff example and the two nearest-note tables are computed), the outline levels' numbers in the notes set to their final 12–14.
- **Sweep**: for every spec and key the example passes; all four combinations of above/below at the two changes pass; a jump to the new root fails with the nearest-note message naming the two right notes; a skipped chord tone, a repeated note and a note outside the chord fail; one unheard note passes in eighths and fails in quarters, two fail; C then E major gives B below and E above; the notes' example and tables.
### Item 3: enclosures (Arpeggio Runner levels 8–10; the game is now 8 → 14 levels), done
- **The ladder**: 1–4 as before · 5 Connect the triads · 6 Connect the sevenths · 7 Connected, in eighth notes · **8 Enclosures on a triad, in C** · **9 Enclosures through II–V–I** · **10 Chromatic enclosures** · 11 Bebop dominant (old 5) · 12 Outlines, in C (6) · 13 Outlines round the cycle (7) · 14 Minor outlines (8). `REORDERS26.arp = {n:8, m:{5:11,6:12,7:13,8:14}, nw:[5..10]}`. Goal A: `arp 1–2` (stage 1) · `3` (2) · `4–7` (3) · `8–13` (4) · `14` (5); `GOAL_WHY` `arp:4`, `arp:8` rewritten, `arp:14` (was `arp:8`).
- **The model** (block “ENCLOSURES”, before the connect block): `ENC_SC` (maj, dor, mix, aeo = Dorian with a ♭6, mixb = Mixolydian ♭2 ♭6), `encTone(root, scale, n)` for chord tone 1, 3 or 5 → the tone, its **neighbour** (the scale note above), its **approach** (a half step below; spelled as the raised lower letter: D♯ under E), the chromatic note between (only where the neighbour is a whole step above) and the scale note below; `encFig(T, tone, kind)` → four notes with their role: `plain` neighbour-tone-approach-tone · `above` 2 ♭2 7 1 · `below` 7 2 ♭2 1 · `cnf` ♭2 2 7 1 · `dbl` (the 3rd: 4 2 ♯2 3). `encTriLine` (root, 3rd, 5th, root above), `encFit` (the line inside five frets, else six, no open strings), `encNeck` (gold root, plain chord tones, blue ring = approach, faint = neighbour), `encMake` (exact pitches, eighths at 66 or slower, count-in, `tol` from 16 notes; `q.enc` = the role of every note). For the notes: `encRow`, `encFigTxt`, `encStaff`.
- **Generators**: `encTriQ({q})` level 8, C major (major scale) and C minor (Dorian), 16 eighths (keys `enc-tri-M-C`, `enc-tri-m-C`) · `encCadQ({keys,dark})` level 9: the plain figure on the root and the 3rd of IIm, V7, I, one bar each, landing on the root of I, 25 notes; `dark` = IIm with a ♭6, V7 with Mixolydian ♭2 ♭6 (`enc-cad-p|d-<key>`; keys `ENC_CKEYS`, without A: the 3rd of E7 would need a double sharp under it) · `encChromQ({keys,kinds})` level 10: one of the three chromatic figures on the root, the 5th and the root above, the double approach on the 3rd (`enc-chr-<kind>-<key>`).
- **Decisions**: the whole line's spots are shown on the neck before playing, as the other fixed-line levels of the app do, and the prompt gives the figure in degrees and in note names · level 8 is C only (major, then minor), as the brief names it · the octave is the one whose lowest note is C3 or higher and that fits one area (C major: frets 6–10; the cadence in C: frets 1–5) · ♭2 and ♭6 are spelled by degree (D♭, A♭; F♭ in E♭, C♭ in B♭) · one home per topic: the enclosure as a figure lives in Arpeggio Runner's notes now; Improvise keeps its chapter on approach notes inside your own line, with a pointer.
- **What the brief got slightly wrong (told to the user)**: in level 9 the “darker” line differs from the plain one by **one note** (A♭ for A over G7). The ♭6 of Dm is the neighbour of its 5th, and the level encloses only roots and 3rds; the prompt and the level note say so. Enclosing the 3rd and the 5th in the darker version would show both changes: his decision.
- **Texts**: three level notes, the lesson card's rule, the game's `desc`, a chapter “Enclosures” in `LESSON_NOTES.arp` (every table and line computed from `encTone`/`encFig`).
- **Sweep**: the brief's five scale rows and C Dorian; the ten figures in C by name and by degree; no chromatic note between E and F; the seven lines of levels 8–10 in C note for note; in every key: every approach a half step below its tone, every neighbour one or two half steps above, a chromatic note only under a whole-step neighbour, the same intervals as in C, no pitch twice in a row, nothing under C3, one area of at most six frets, the perfect take passes and one wrong note fails; the 14-level ladder.
- **Not played on a real guitar** (items 2 and 3). The connect levels are graded by rule: correct, not necessarily good.
### Item 5: the pentatonic ladder (Blues Boxes level 5; 7 → 8 levels), done
- **Ladder of the game**: 1–4 as before · **5 The ladder** · 6 The blue note (old 5) · 7 Major and minor in one place (6) · 8 Eighths and triplets (7). `REORDERS26.boxes = {n:7, m:{5:6,6:7,7:8}, nw:[5]}`. Goal A's appendix: `boxes 2–8`.
- **`penLadder(rp, kind)`** (block “THE PENTATONIC LADDER”, after `boxQ`): fifteen spots, a two-string cell three times (strings 6–5, 4–3, 2–1; each spot carries its pair `p`). `LAD_CELL`: `'23'` = ♭7 1 on the lower string, ♭3 4 5 on the upper; `'32'` = ♭3 4 5, then ♭7 1. The cell starts at its lowest place on string 6; the octaves fall two and then three frets higher by themselves (string 4 is 10 half steps above string 6, string 2 is 9 above string 4). `ladBoxes(rp, spot)` = the boxes that hold a spot, `ladCover(rp, cell)` = the two neighbouring boxes a cell lies across (A minor, two-then-three: boxes 5 and 1, then 1 and 2, then 2 and 3; three-then-two: 2 and 3, 3 and 4, 4 and 5).
- **`boxLadQ({roots,kinds})`**: up and back, 29 quarter notes, over the I7 band, key `bx-lad-23` / `bx-lad-32`. Generators: A minor, then `LAD_ROOTS` (G, C, D).
- **Decisions**: free start with the running click and the Scale Runner allowance, as every other Blues Boxes level (the lobby text of the game says “start on any click”), not a count-in · 72 or slower · **a ladder is asked only when its top fret is 17 or lower and it holds no B2** (the line passes each note twice and the allowance is one unheard note). That leaves A (both cells, the three-then-two up to fret 17 as in the brief), G (both), C (two-then-three), D (both). **E, B and F♯ are not asked**: each of their ladders passes the low B or runs to fret 19. The user's call if he wants E at fret 19.
- **Texts**: a level note, the lesson card's rule, `desc`, a chapter “The ladder” in `LESSON_NOTES.boxes` (two computed figures and a table of the boxes).
- **Sweep**: the brief's frets for both cells in A minor (= C major pentatonic from G); for all twelve roots: the three octaves are one shape two and then three frets higher, whole steps on a string and a minor 3rd across; the questions (29 notes, up and back, no B2, perfect passes, a wrong note fails, all seven key and cell combinations appear); the boxes of each cell.
### Item 6: one octave, three times (Along the Neck level 4; 8 → 9 levels), done
- **Ladder of the game**: 1–3 as before · **4 One octave, three times** · 5 Shifting positions (old 4) · 6 The whole neck · 7 Free travel · 8 Modes and melodic minor · 9 Free travel, any scale. `REORDERS26.along = {n:8, m:{4:5,…,8:9}, nw:[4]}`. Goal A's appendix: `along 1–9`.
- **`alPairSpots(pc0, semis, low)`** (block “ONE OCTAVE, THREE TIMES”, before `alShiftQ`): a one-octave cell (half steps above its first note), `low` of its notes on the lower string of the pair and the rest on the upper, at its lowest place on string 6, three times. Null when a fret is above 17 or the line holds B2.
- **Generators**: `alPairQ({chords,inv})` a seventh-chord arpeggio (m7, Maj7, 7) two notes a string, from the root, 3rd, 5th or 7th, 23 notes up and back (keys `alp-<quality>-<inversion>`) · `alPairScaleQ({keys})` a major scale as three notes and four, 41 notes (`alp-sc`). Level: Am7 from the root · Am7 from the 3rd, 5th, 7th · any chord (`AL_PROOTS`; 60 of the 120 root × quality × inversion combinations fit under fret 18 without B2) · the scale in F · the scale in F, B♭, A♭. Count-in, quarters at 72 or slower, the allowance (`tol`), and the answer screen shows the three shapes side by side (`alPairAfter`).
- **Decisions (the brief left them open)**: the scale version keeps three octaves and uses **F, B♭ and A♭** (`AL_PKEYS`): there the top fret is 12, 17 and 15 and there is no low B. C would reach fret 19 and A passes B2, so “C or A first” holds for the arpeggio (Am7, as in the brief) and the scale starts in F · the game still shows notes, never fingerings.
- **Texts**: a level note, the lesson card's rule, `desc`, a chapter “One octave, three times” in `LESSON_NOTES.along` (Am7 figure, the table of the four cells of Am7, F major figure, all computed).
- **Sweep**: Am7's frets as in the brief; the major scale's shape moved to C equals the brief's frets and C itself is refused (fret 19); every question: 23 or 41 notes on the clicks, up and back, the octaves equal, only chord or scale notes, the degrees of the named inversion, no B2, at most fret 17, perfect passes, a wrong note fails, three shapes on the answer screen; `REORDERS26` covers every level of the four games exactly once.
- **Not played on a real guitar** (items 5 and 6).
### Item 4: Chord Forms (`caged`): NOT BUILT. The brief cannot be done as written; the user was asked and chose “Leave it out for now” (22 Nov 2026)
- The brief asks for strummed answers with two or three accepted partial grips per form **and** for “a strum of the right chord in the wrong area fails (that is the point of the game)”. The microphone hears pitch, not strings, and the five forms of one chord are largely the same pitches on other strings: the C form of C (x-3-2-0-1-0) is note for note the lower five strings of the G form (8-7-5-5-5-x); the A form (x-3-5-5-5-3) is the lower five strings of the E form (8-10-10-9-8-x); the brief's own partial grip of the G form, 8-7-5-5-x-x, is the bottom of the C form.
- Measured with `chordCheck` on all 12 roots, major and minor (480 ordered pairs “asked form X, played form Y”): with the partial grips the brief asks for, a strum in the wrong area passes in **244 of 480**; with the complete forms only, exact, in **53 of 480** (the extra top note of the G or E form is an octave above a note of the C or A form, and the chord check takes that for an overtone).
- The options put to him: a two-input game (the form marked on the neck on screen, where the area is checked exactly, and strummed on the guitar) · guitar only with the area on the player's honour where the mic cannot hear it · complete forms only, exact · leave it out. **He chose to leave it out.** Nothing of item 4 is in the code; goals C and D have no `caged` entry. If it is taken up again, start from this measurement, and note the brief's own remark that whether he should think in this second map is his teacher's call.
