## YoutubeNotes: course notes from a channel's lessons (22 Nov 2026; tried by the user in the test copy, then committed and published at his word the same day)
The user's brief (kept in the session): act as a Berklee teacher, make each video whole from its captions (repair the caption engine's errors, reconstruct what is played but not said, keep *said* / *inferred* / *unclear* apart), merge the videos into course notes in learning order, check every musical fact by computing it, reference the videos in every chapter, and put it on a page “YoutubeNotes” next to Metronome.
- **Page**: tab `#nav-yt` (action `ytnotes`, view `{name:'ytnotes',set}`), `vYT()`; it reuses the notes page's classes and `noteItem`, the chapter list jumps with `notesec`. Every chapter ends with “From the videos”: links that open YouTube at that second in a new tab (no embedded player).
- **Data**: `YT_NOTES` (core, between `/*YT_NOTES_BEGIN*/` and `/*YT_NOTES_END*/`, just before the VIEWS marker): one `push` per set = `{title, sub, videos:{id:{t,len}}, chapters:[{h, p:[note items], vids:[[id,[[second,label]…]]…]}], end:[note items]}`. The first set is **“@marbinmusic - Triads, arpeggios, CAGED, the neck”**: 23 videos, 16 chapters (the notes of the neck · intervals as shapes · four ways to move · triads · CAGED · the pentatonic inside the forms · the diagonals · open triads · diminished triads in position · seventh-chord arpeggios · a progression in one area · from forms to jazz chords · voice leading · enclosures · triads in place of the chord · triad pairs), 15 figures, 21 tables, about 5,400 words, and a closing section listing what was reconstructed, what was left unclear, where the notes differ from what he says, and what was left out.
- **Nothing is typed that can be computed.** Its own checked helpers (inside the set's closure): `shape(tab, root, degrees)`, `spots('string/fret=Name …')`, `degs('string/fret=degree …', root)` compare every label with the pitch it sits on and push to `NF.err` on a mismatch; the interval table is computed from the tuning, the twelve chord formulas and the triad-pair tables from `tr`. Sweep: figures draw with no `NF.err`, table rows fit their heads, every video reference exists with times rising inside the video, every listed video is used, house rules of the text.
- **How it was made**: captions fetched for the 23 videos (`videos/marbin/_triads_group.tsv`), four agents wrote one digest per video to `videos/marbin/digest/<id>.md` from the brief `videos/marbin/_DIGEST_BRIEF.md` (all git-ignored), then the curriculum was written from the digests and every shape recomputed. A further group of videos = the same steps and another `YT_NOTES.push`.
- A reconstructed example is tagged `reconstructed` in its caption (`.ytinf`): he names notes, degrees and forms; the frets are worked out and are right as music but may not be his fingering.

## The test copy: http://localhost:8777/ (22 Nov 2026, user: “a test environment where I can check new functionalities before committing”)
`.venv/bin/python tools/preview.py` serves **the working tree** on this Mac: `/` is `src/fretwise.html` read fresh on every load (so an edit shows after a reload, nothing is built or committed), with `piano/`, `vendor/` and `art/` beside it as on the live site (nothing else of the repo is served). It is another origin than the live site, so **its saved progress is its own**: nothing done there, a migration included, can touch the real progress. On the first visit, and after the band's “Reset to my live progress” (`/?reset=1`, which reads the live store again), it starts from a copy of the live progress (`tests/dump_store.py` → `.preview/seed.json`, git-ignored). A yellow band at the bottom says TEST COPY and the tab title starts with TEST. The mic works (localhost is a secure page). Sprites and the hall load there (the plain `src/` page under a file server does not reach `art/`).
- **Paused on 24 Nov 2026 at the user's word** (“let's disable this test copy format for a bit. I will open it if I see fit in the future”): do not hold work for him to try at the test copy and do not offer its URL. Build, run the automatic checks (sweep, `pages_check`, the tests the change needs), then commit and push; tell him what went live. The tool and its server script stay; he will ask for the test copy again if he wants it. (Before: build in the working tree, give him the URL, commit only when he had tried it and said so.)
- The server runs only while its process does (started in the background of the session that needs it; the user can start it in a terminal of his own). Port 8777; if it is taken the script says the copy is probably running already.
- Fix-list reports written in the test copy stay in the test copy's own storage: `tests/read_fixlist.py` reads the live site's.

## YouTube transcripts are planning material, not app content (22 Nov 2026; a video library was built into the full notes and removed the same day at the user's request: “I have different plans with the youtube related stuff. they will be more relevant for our planning we do here, not directly added as they are to fretwise”)
- **Nothing from YouTube is in the app.** `VIDEOS`, `videosHTML`, the embedded player and their CSS and sweep check were taken out again (they are in git history, commit `a4f88e6`, if an embedded library is ever wanted). **Do not add videos, embeds or points taken from a video to Fretwise unless the user asks for that.** **Exception the user asked for (27 Nov 2026): “YouTube Notes” of a game, a textbook entry per video lesson reached from that game's lesson card (Scale Runner only so far; section “YouTube Notes for Scale Runner”). Links to YouTube, no embedded players. Still ask before adding video material anywhere else.**
- **What stays, for planning**: `videos/<channel>/` (git-ignored): `_list.tsv` (the 879 videos of `@marbinmusic`), the captions of the six pilot videos as timed text (`<id>.txt`), and `_digest.md` (each video's points in my own words with times, where he differs from what Fretwise teaches, and what no game covers). Transcripts are the creator's words: they stay local.
- **The pipeline**: `.venv/bin/yt-dlp --flat-playlist --print "%(id)s\t%(duration)s\t%(title)s" https://www.youtube.com/@marbinmusic/videos > videos/marbin/_list.tsv` → per video `.venv/bin/yt-dlp --skip-download --write-subs --write-auto-subs --sub-langs "en.*,en" --sub-format json3 -o "%(id)s" <url>` in that folder (no video is downloaded) → `.venv/bin/python tools/captions.py videos/marbin` (a stamp every 30 s). `yt-dlp` is installed in `.venv`. **YouTube answers 429 quickly** (two of twelve requests with two seconds between videos): a batch must go slowly and resume. Auto-captions mangle music terms (“dionic”, “melidian”, “trone”, “a void note”): read by context.
- **The sweep's text checks were partly dead** (found while adding the video check, and kept): inside the sweep's evaluated template string a regex escape needs two backslashes; `\b` and `\w` written with one never matched, so the lower-case-numeral checks (level notes, goal texts) and the voice lines' slot check tested nothing. Repaired; one level note tripped it (“IIm, not ii”, reworded). **Write `\\b` in that file.**

## The companion: bigger, a pixel speech bubble, and she speaks on her own (21 Nov 2026, user: “why is she not saying random stuff. the character should be even bigger and make the chatbox prettier”, with a pixel-art bubble as reference)
- **Random lines were too rare** (a clean answer, six quiet answers, then one chance in five). Now: one clean answer in three once she has been quiet for three answers (`buddyJudge`), and **by herself** when 20 to 34 seconds have passed without a line (`budChatter`, a one-second timer; rounds only: not in the continuous rhythm games, not on the results page, not in a hidden tab).
- **Bigger**: an animated master's frame is 1.62 × the side margin (240–440 px; 373 px at 1440 wide), shifted left by 9 % of itself so the mic stand stays off the page (`#buddy.budbig`; **not `.big`: that class belongs to the stat tiles and shrank her**).
- **The bubble** (`.budsay`): a pixel-art balloon after the user's reference, peach fill, a darker inner rim, a dark stepped outline, a pixel tail over her head. Drawn as two inline SVGs of one-unit rectangles (generated by script: an 18 × 18 nine-slice with 8-unit stepped corners used as `border-image` at 3 px a unit, and a 7 × 6 tail in `::after`), a light 1 px rim so the dark outline shows on the dark hall, a hard drop shadow, and a three-step pop for every new line (`budpop`, `budpop2`). The text sits in a `<span>` with negative margins (the wide border would eat the room). For the animated master the bubble is anchored 12 px from the screen's left edge (it ran off screen when centred on her) and is at most 240 px wide so it stops at the page's edge.

## Jessica animated in the game (21 Nov 2026, the user's own drawings: “the grid on each section is in continuity with default_game… make the character look larger… take initiative on timing… a streak should keep admiring in the max admiration pose”)
- **Art**: `art-src/ear-game/{base,admire,cry,fedup,improve}.png` (copied from the user's `~/Desktop/Character designs/`; anime-style drawings on flat green, not pixel art). `base` is a 6×6 grid (36 idle frames in reading order: glances and a blink, a sly smile, a hand on the hip with a wink, bubble gum blown and popped, a hand on the mic); each emotion is a 3×3 grid (9 frames: 0 the base look, 4 the peak, 8 back). **`.venv/bin/python tools/sprites_anim.py [id]`** keys the green (soft edge, spill removed), scales and shifts every sheet so the body (saturated pixels, the grey mic stand left out) stands on the same feet line, middle and height (measured on each sheet's first frame; `cry` takes the other sheets' scale because its first frame is bent), puts the mic stand behind the crying frames (they were drawn without it; taken from `admire`'s first frame), and writes **`art/masters/ear-game.webp`**: 9 × 8 cells of 352 px, 72 frames, about 1.1 MB. Frames 0–35 base, 36–44 admire, 45–53 cry, 54–62 fedup, 63–71 improve. `build.py` embeds `art/masters/*.webp` too.
- **`MASTERS.ear.game = {file, cols, rows, clips}`**, clips as lists of `[frame, ms]`: `base {loop}` (two or three seconds on a still frame, the scenes at speed: a blink 120 ms, the gum grows over a second and pops in a fifth; one pass is about 35 s) · `streak {loop}` (the peak of admiring, held) · `talk` (the hand-on-the-mic frames: start, end and random lines) and `admire cry fedup improve`, each `{in, hold, out}`. Admiring and improving snap in at about 100 ms a frame and sit on the peak; crying wails back and forth; fed up stops for 700 ms on the blank stare, explodes, and sighs out slowly (1.1 s on the tired frame).
- **The sequencer** (`gsRun`, `gsLoop`, `gsEmote`, `gsMin`, `gsFrame`, `gsStop`; `el._gs`): `in` once, `hold` round and round until the line has been up long enough, then `out`, then her resting look (`budRest`). A line is never shorter than one full pass of its clip. **Her resting look follows the streak** (`BUD.rest`): from five in a row she stays in the held peak of admiring (an admire line then skips its `out`) until the streak ends; in the rhythm games the same from eight clean bars. Reduced motion: the peak frame, still.
- **Larger**: an animated master's frame is 1.25 × the side margin (210–330 px; 288 px at 1440 wide, where the six-frame sprites are 192), drawn smooth (`.spr.gspr`, no pixelated scaling, a soft shadow); the frame's clear space may lie over the page's edge. Only the in-game companion uses it: **the hall and the master's page still show the six-frame pixel sprite** (the user's `main_screen` drawings of four masters are not used yet).
- Checked in Chrome on the offline build (the dev page under `src/` cannot reach `art/`: test sprites on `dist/` or the live layout): the frames shown over time for the start line, the idle loop, admire → the streak hold → cry → base, fed up, improve; screenshots of each look.
- **For the next master**: five sheets in the same form into `art-src/<id>-game/`, run the tool, add `game:{…}` with its own frame timing (the frame numbers follow the tool's order).

## The masters' voices in play: five emotions, placed by what happened (21 Nov 2026, user: “the master's comments are not inspired… very smart placement… admiring on a win streak, fed up when you make the same mistake many times, crying when you lose a streak, improving when you stop making a mistake you've done many times”)
The companion beside a round used to cheer on every right answer and say a short line on 30 % of them and on every miss. Now she speaks **only when one of five things has really happened** (core block “THE MASTERS' VOICES IN PLAY”, just before the VIEWS marker, so the sweep can test it):
- **`buddyJudge(c)`** (pure): **cry** = a miss that ends a streak of 5 or more · **fedup** = a miss that is a repeated mistake (the same mix-up for the 3rd time, `S.diag`; or an item missed 3+ times in all and twice in its last four tries, `it.w`, `it.r`) · **improve** = the third clean answer in a row on an item that had been missed 3+ times (`/0111$/` on `it.r`: fires once, at the moment it stops being a mistake) · **admire** = the streak reaches 5, 10, 15, 20, 30, 40, 50 · **random** = one clean answer in five once she has been quiet for six answers. A plain right or wrong answer: no line, no pose. A miss that is both: a streak of 10+ cries, else the repeated mistake wins. Improving beats a streak mark.
- **Lines**: `MASTER_LINES[cat] = {admire, cry, fedup, improve, random}`; slots `{n}` (admire, cry: the streak) and `{mistake}` (fedup, improve: `budMistake(q)` = the right answer's short name, e.g. “minor 6th”; empty when it would not read as a name, and then only slotless lines are drawn). `budPick` never draws a line with an empty slot, never the same line twice running, uses every line of a mood before repeating, and prefers a line that names the thing when it is known. **Jessica (ear) has her five moods** (a femme fatale out of an old picture; film lines riffed, not quoted; her `voice` for the AI talk says the same). **The other four masters still fall back to their old short `say` lists** (`budLines`): the user fills in their personalities with the prompt he was given, then their lines go into `MASTER_LINES`.
- **Hooks**: `answer()` → `buddyAnswer(q,it,ok,hinted)` (`S.prevStreak`, `S.budSince`); the continuous rhythm games → `buddyGroove('hold'|'break',bars)`: admire at 8, 16, 24… clean bars (improve the first time 8 are held after three broken runs), cry when a run of 8+ breaks, fed up at every fourth broken short run, nothing else (the player is reading). `buddyReact` keeps only `unlock` and `end`. A line stays 1.9 s + 50 ms a letter (6 s at most); the pose is held as long.
- **The six looks**: moods `base admire fedup cry improve talk`. A master without an animated sheet shows them as the old six still frames (`sprPose`, `MOOD_FRAME`: admire = amazed, fedup = angry, cry = wince, improve = cheer). The animated sheets are described in the section above (the row/fps format first built here was replaced by it the same day).
- My reading of “remove angry and crying”: the lost-streak emotion is **crying, replacing angry**. To be confirmed by the user.
- Sweep: nineteen trigger cases, every line (length, slots only where they belong, a slotless line in every mood), the picker. Checked in Chrome (headless, muted): a real round in Hear the Interval gives the start line, admire at 5, cry on the miss after it; fedup and improve name the mistake; the line clears and the base look returns; a test grid animates and returns to base.

## Review of the Sight Reading build (20 Nov 2026, user: “review, fix the broken stuff… make it flawless”; the build below was done by another session)
Checked: the sweep and page checks rerun; the notation looked at example by example (values, rests, ties, triplets, sixteenths, 3/4, 6/8, changing meters, ledger lines, a page with signs) and on seven line levels; the move on the user's **live** store (1,145 answers = Reading 1,066 + Sight Reading 79, as reported); 960 generated pages read note by note against their chords. The engine, the line levels and the move were right. Changed:
- **A long page could not be read without scrolling** (a 32-bar page was 1,229 px tall and began 385 px down: two and a half of eight lines showed; nobody can scroll while playing). `srPageMake`: from five systems the page is **an open book**, two columns of half the systems each (`.srspread`; `scoreSVG`'s new `only:[a,b)` draws those systems only), the play screen takes the wide layout (`q.srWide` → `html.wide2`, the companion hidden), and every page is fitted to the window's height (`.srfit`, `.srspread`: `max-height:calc(100vh - 322px)`). The page prompts are one bold line and a small one (`.qsub`). On a page of eight bars or more the **count-in is shown at the top** (`html.srpage #cin`, fixed); it was under the page, out of sight. Checked at 1440×820: levels 26–29 all fit. Under 1180 px wide the columns stack and the page scrolls again.
- **The generated pages wrote wrong notes over chords outside the key** (`srFormPage`): off the strong beats any note of the *key* was allowed (F natural over D7♯11 in C). Now a note must belong to **the chord's own scale**: the key's notes that fit the chord's family (`srChordsOf` → `c.sc` from `formSoloBars`, so a dominant leading to a minor chord takes harmonic minor from its 5th; `SR_WSC`: a minor seventh chord is written with Dorian or Aeolian, never the ♭2) and its chord tones. Over A♭m7 D♭7 in C that gives the notes a teacher would write.
- **Spelling**: a note outside the key, and every note over a chord whose root is outside the key, is spelled as a degree of its chord (`srSpell`): B♭ under Gm7 and C7, A♭ C♭ under A♭m7 D♭7. It was spelled by the signature's side (G♯ under D♭7, A♯ under Gm7). A note pushed into the next chord is spelled from that chord.
- **Register**: the melody lies on the staff (sounding E3 and up, strings 5 to 1); it had been written down to the sixth string, under the staff, where a lead sheet never is and the laptop mic hears poorly.
- **Rests** (`scoreBars`): neighbouring rests are joined where print writes one (a half rest on beat 1 or 3, not in 3/4; a quarter rest on a beat; an eighth rest on a half beat). Compound meters and tuplets untouched.
- Sweep (`SRPAGE`): every note in its chord's scale (a pushed note: the chord it is pushed into), nothing under E3, a spelling is the pitch it stands for, no sharp written under a flat-root chord.
- `tests/sr_player.py 27 28` after the changes (the live listening path, headless, muted): the 16-bar page passes clean (58 of 58 notes), the road map passes, read straight through it fails.
- **The deploy**: the publish step asks GitHub for the artifact a fraction of a second after the upload step finished it, and GitHub intermittently answers “0 artifacts” (six failures that evening, the upload successful every time; `gh run list` shows a run's *last* attempt, so a run that succeeded on a retry can read as failed). Updating the actions (`checkout@v7`, `configure-pages@v6`, `upload-pages-artifact@v5`, `deploy-pages@v5`) did not cure it. `pages.yml` now waits 20 s before the publish step and tries it once more after 60 s if it fails. **To know what is live, read the build id on the site** (`curl -s https://ealkan23.github.io/fretwise/ | grep -o "const BUILD='[^']*'"`), not the run list.
- **Still not played on a real guitar.** Not changed: the generated melodies are reading material, not good tunes; the texts are the other session's and unread by the teacher.

**Re-read this section before each phase.** The user's answers to the brief's four questions: (1) **move** Reading's played-line levels into the new game; (2) name “Sight Reading”, id `sightread`; (3) the traditional `TUNES` as written pages: yes; (4) **no audio interface**: laptop mic, do not retune the detector (the `lowOct` rules stay). Rules from the user for this build: after every phase sweep `bad 0`, `pages_check` `all ok`, commit, push, write here; stop after phase 2 for screenshots until he says “continue”; browser tests headless and muted; if the brief cannot be done as written, stop and ask.

The levels that move (7, 8, 9, 10, 12, 13, 14, 16, 17, 18's line generators, 20) hold 79 answers (all guitar).

### Phase 2: the score engine (pitched notation), done
In the core, just before `READ_BARS` (after `staffRhySVG`). `staffRhySVG`, `staffLineSVG` and their callers (`readLineQ`, `singPageQ`, `melDictQ`) are **unchanged**; the new engine is separate because `staffRhySVG` only knows 4/4 with eighth pairs and `rhySVG` is a one-line percussion staff on another scale. It takes `rhySVG`'s event model (`{t,d,rest,tup}`) and adds a pitch.
- **Units: quarter notes everywhere.** `SC_METER[m]` = `{len, beat, beam, top, bot, cut?, cmp?}` for `4/4 3/4 2/4 C| 6/8 12/8` (6/8: len 3, beat and beam 1.5; cut time `C|`: len 4, beat and beam 2). The click of a compound or cut meter is `beat`; set `metro`/`grid` in those beats in phase 3.
- **A line** (what a generator writes): `{meter | meters:[per bar], ks (sharps > 0, flats < 0, up to ±4), pref?, swing?, notes:[{m: sounding midi | rest:1, d, tup?: group id, w?: written spelling}], chords?:[{t: absolute beat, sym}], signs?:{barIndex:[{k:'rs'|'re'|'v1'|'v2'|'segno'|'coda'|'toCoda'|'txt'|'end', len?, txt?}]}}`. The spelling is `writtenOf(m, pref)` (pref = sign of `ks`, sharps in C) unless `w` is given. A tuplet note's `d` is its real length (1/3 for an eighth triplet, 2/3 quarter triplet, 1/6 sixteenth triplet); its written value is `d*1.5` (`scWv`). Tuplet groups must not cross a bar (the checker says so).
- `scoreBars(line)` → **bars** `[{m, len, ev:[{t (in the bar), d, rest, m, w, tup, tieTo, tieFrom, src (index of the line's note), acc}], ch:[{t,sym}], sg:[…]}]`. Notes crossing a bar line, or that cannot be one written value where they start, are split and tied by `scPiece(t,d,M)`: on a beat any value that fits (whole only on 1, dotted half on 1 or 2), off the beat a quarter or dotted quarter (print style `e q e`, `e q.`: this is what the CLAUDE.md open item about `rhySVG` asked for), on the 2nd sixteenth `.75 .5 .25`, on the 4th only `.25`; in 6/8 and 12/8 nothing crosses a dotted-quarter group except the dotted half (on 1, or 1 and 3 of 12/8) and dotted whole. A bar of rests that fills its meter becomes one whole rest (`whole:1`).
- `scoreAcc(bars,ks)` (called by `scoreBars`): **accidentals by bar memory**: `acc` = the alteration to print (−1, 0 = ♮, 1) or `null`; compared with the bar's memory at that staff step (same octave only) or else the key signature (`scKsAlt`, letter orders `SC_SH`/`SC_FL`); a sign holds to the bar line; a tied continuation prints nothing and does **not** set the new bar's memory (so a later note at that step in the new bar gets its sign again). No courtesy accidentals. `accSVG2(a,x,cy)` draws ♮ as shapes (♯ ♭ through the old `accSVG`).
- `scoreLayout(bars,{per,ks})` → systems `{from, bars, x0, x1, bx[], pos[][], showTs, tsIn[], full}`: **four bars a line** (`SCL.per`), but a line takes fewer when its bars would be squeezed under 85 % of their natural width (dense sixteenths: 2–3 bars a line). Spacing per written value (`SC_SP`), +13 for an accidental; full lines stretch to `SCL.W` (1000 units); a last, short line keeps the full lines' scale. Clef and key signature on every line, time signature on the first line and wherever the meter changes (at a line start in the header, inside a line before the bar).
- `scoreSVG(bars,{ks, swing, title, per, mark, barOk, label, zoom})` → one `<svg class="staff score">` with all systems stacked; the viewBox width follows the widest line (a two-bar line is not stretched to a page). Draws: heads (hollow from the half), dots, ledger lines, stems by position (a group by its note farthest from the middle line; below the staff stems reach the middle line), flags, **flat beams by the beat** (dotted quarter in 6/8/12/8, half in cut time), double beams and stubs for sixteenths, tuplet number on the beam side or a bracket when the group is not one beam (quarters, a rest inside), rests of every value as shapes (`.sc-qr` zigzag quarter rest, eighth and sixteenth from `rhySVG`'s shapes, dotted rests), ties on the side away from the stem (also over a line break: half tie to the end of the line, half tie from the header to the note), bar numbers at the start of every line but the first, chord symbols above (`.sc-ch`, at the note starting on or after their beat), repeat signs, 1st/2nd ending brackets, segno, coda, To Coda, text (D.C./D.S./Fine), “Swing”, a title, final bar line. Feedback: `mark[src]` = `'ok'|'bad'|'miss'` colours the note (green, red, orange outline); `barOk[bar]` = true/false shades the bar. `aria-label` says bars, meter, signature, number of notes.
- `scoreData(bars)` → the sounded notes `[{bar, beat (1-based, quarter units), m, name, len (ties summed), src}]` (plain data for tests and grading). `scoreCheck(bars, meter)` → a list of faults: a bar that does not fill its meter, a gap or overlap, a piece that is not a written value, a tuplet group that is not .5, 1 or 2 beats or has an unwritable value, a tie that does not reach the same pitch. `scoreOf(line,o)` = `scoreSVG(scoreBars(line), …)`.
- CSS: `svg.staff.score` (no 360 px cap), `.sc-*`, `.scn.ok/.bad/.miss`, `.sc-bar.ok/.bad`. The treble clef is the existing `𝄞` glyph (as every staff in the app); nothing else uses the Musical Symbols block.
- **Checks**: `tests/score_demo.js` (13 fixed examples: values, rests, ties over beat, bar and line, bar memory in C, G, four flats and four sharps, eighth/broken/quarter/sixteenth triplets, sixteenth figures, 3/4, 6/8, 12/8 → cut time → 2/4 → 3/4, ledger lines, a 12-bar page with chords, swing, repeat and endings, segno, To Coda, D.S. al Coda, coda, feedback colours) rendered by `.venv/bin/python tests/score_demo.py` (headless, muted) to `tests/out/score-1440.png` and `score-1000.png`; every example passes `scoreCheck`. The sweep's `SCORE` block: bar memory (C, G, another octave, a tie into the next bar, four flats), splitting (six cases), good lines pass the checker, a short bar / a tie to another pitch / a tuplet that does not add up are caught, `scoreData` lengths, no `undefined` in the SVG. Looked at by eye at 2× zoom: the first quarter rest and cut-time sign were wrong and were redrawn.
- Known limits of the engine: flat beams only (no slant); no courtesy accidentals; no slurs, dynamics or articulations; triplet spacing follows the written value (a triplet beat is wider than a straight beat, as in print).
### Phase 3: the game, the moved levels, the move out of Reading, done
- **`sightread`** (`cat:'neck'`, `mic:true`, John; in `GAMES` right after Reading). **Phase-3 numbering, 13 levels** (phase 4 inserts the new-figure levels with a `REORDERS` table; the table of the brief is the target order): 1 Two notes (old Reading 7) · 2 A line of even notes (8) · 3 Quarters, halves, eighths (9) · 4 Lines in G and F (10) · 5 Dotted quarters and off-beats (14, its first-position generator) · 6 Positions II to IV (12) · 7 D and B♭; one accidental (13) · 8 A and E♭ (new: A in II, E♭ in III) · 9 Fifth position (16 + 14's fifth-position generator) · 10 Swing eighths and pushes (17) · 11 Seventh position; G and D (18's line generators) · 12 A♭ and E (new: A♭ in IV, E in VII; `READ_KEYS` gained `E:[4,4]`, `Ab:[8,-4]`) · 13 Ninth position (20). Target positions after phase 4: 1 2 3 5 6 7 8 12 13 14 16 19 20.
- **Reading** keeps 11 levels: 1–6 as before, 7 Positions II–IV (old 11), 8 Fifth position (15), 9 Seventh position (old 18 without its lines: `sightQ` only), 10 Ninth position (19), 11 Two notes at once (21). `desc` says lines in time are Sight Reading. `readLineQ` is no longer used by a game (kept; `staffRhySVG`/`staffLineSVG` still serve Sing the Page and Hear the Degree).
- **`srLineQ(o)`** (block “SIGHT READING”, right after `readLineQ`): `o = {win, strings, key (name | list), rhy (undefined = even quarters, 1 = READ_BARS, or RCELL cells), swing, len, free, chrom, nat, meter, bars, per}`. Same pitch rules as `readLineQ` (key notes in the window, steps and thirds, `noDup`, `chrom` = one note the half step under the next). Even lines are quarters in 4/4 with the last bar filled by rests (a quarter + half rest, as print does; the rest has no target); the two-note level is two quarters and a half rest, free start. **Keys are `readLineQ`'s exactly** (`rl<len>-<win0>-…` free, `rl-<win0>-<key->x-r-|j-|s-><midis>`): the moved answers stay with their questions; new keys/positions make new keys. `chrom` without `o.nat` rejects any line that would need a ♮ (the naturals level comes in phase 4). Grid from `scoreData` in click beats (`SC_METER.beat`), swing off-beats at .67, `metro` 60 (swing 90) as before. `q.sr = {bars, ks, swing, srcs (score note of each target slot), barOf}`.
- **The answer staff** (`after`): `srMarks(q)` reads `S.micResult.per`: right = green, right pitch off the beat = amber (`.scn.late`), wrong = red, not heard = orange outline; each bar shaded green or red. The question staff stays above it (as Reading did).
- **Registered**: `COURSE` 201, `BADGE` life/core, `SHORTN`, `STD.sightread = {acc:.95, ab:50}` (Reading's `STD.sight` lost “lines in time”), `LESSONS.sightread`, `LESSON_NOTES.sightread` (first version, four chapters: what sight reading is, reading ahead, never stop, choosing the position; finished in phase 6), `LEVEL_NOTES` moved with their levels (new notes for Reading 9, Sight Reading 5, 8, 9, 11, 12), the reference chart (`refChart`) on its level page too. Not in `PLAY_GAMES` (guitar only), not in `HAT_GAMES` (a full click while reading is learned), no `STYLE` (general).
- **Goals (minimal now, phase 6 rebuilds goal R)**: R stage 2 `['sightread',1,5]` (was Reading 7–10), stage 3 `['sight',7,8]` + `['sightread',6,10]`, stage 4 `['sight',9,11]` + `['sightread',11,13]`; C's appendix `['sight',1,6]` + `['sightread',1,4]`. `GOAL_WHY`: `sightread:1`, `sight:7` (new text), `sightread:6`, `sight:9`, `sightread:11`; the old `sight:11`, `sight:18` are gone.
- **The move, `moveReading(g)`** (flag `g.mig.sr1` on Reading, `STORE.sr1`; called at the end of `G('sight')` after all of Reading's earlier migrations, and `G('sightread')` first calls `G('sight')` when Reading has no `sr1`). From the old 21-level numbering: `SR_MOVE` (7→1 8→2 9→3 10→4 12→6 13→7 14→5 16→9 17→10 18→11 20→13), `SR_KEEP` (1–6, 11→7, 15→8, 18→9, 19→10, 21→11). Items by level and label (`srItemTo`: level 18 only its lines `/^\d+ notes in |^Line in /`; level 14 first position → 5, else 9); the log `pl` by its item (a map of moved keys) or level; rounds, history points, `hist`/`ht`/`hs`/`hm` windows, `lc` by level (level-wide records of 14 go to 5, of 18 stay in Reading; 18's line answers are taken out of its `lc.g`); totals `n c ms pn pc time` from the moved items and rounds; **days split exactly**: the moved log entries give each day's n, c and timing fields, the moved rounds its ms and practice time; frontier: the new game's level = the highest moved level reached, moved levels passed stay passed, an unpassed one under it goes to `skip`; Reading's frontier = the first kept level not passed. Not moved (not stored by level): mix-ups `cf`, per-key `rk`, per-string `sp`. **LOGDB is not touched**: the archive keeps the old entries under `sight` with the old level numbers.
- **On the user's data** (`tests/sr_migtest.py`, a copy of `backups/store-before-sight-reading-2026-11-20.json`, headless, muted): answers 1,145 = Reading 1,066 + Sight Reading 79; clean 1,028 = 968 + 60; answer time, played, practice time (6,794,305 ms = 5,990,384 + 803,921), log 300 = 221 + 79, rounds 72 = 66 + 6, history points 72 = 66 + 6, items 202 = 154 + 48, every daily field adds up; every other game byte-identical; only new top-level key `sr1`; second load a no-op. **Reading's frontier: level 8 (“Read a line”) → level 7 “Positions II, III, IV”** (the next kept level; the user had not reached it before only because the line levels were in the way). **Sight Reading: level 1 passed, frontier level 2 “A line of even notes”**.
- **Fix list**: `fixContext` carries `score` (`q.sr` as plain data: meter, signature, every event `[t, d, midi, tup, tie]`); `tests/fix_takes.py` maps an old Reading line report (game `sight`, levels 7–20 of the old numbering) to `sightread` (`_SR`; **phase 4 must update this map to the final numbering**). The one Reading report on file is level 6 (stays in Reading).
- Checks: sweep (the two-note level's grading checks and the key / accidental line checks now target `srLineQ`), `pages_check`, `tests/sr_start.py` (every level started headless and muted on the migrated data, no page error; a graded take rendered: wrong note red, late note amber, bars shaded).
### Phase 4: the new-figure levels and the four- and eight-bar lines, done (25 levels; the phase-3 numbering above is superseded)
- **The ladder now** (the brief's table): 1 Two notes · 2 A line of even notes · 3 Quarters, halves, eighths · **4 Rests and ties** · 5 Lines in G and F · 6 Dotted quarters and off-beats · 7 Positions II to IV · 8 D and B♭; one accidental · **9 Naturals; an accidental holds for the bar** · **10 Eighth-note triplets** · **11 3/4** · 12 A and E♭ · 13 Fifth position · 14 Swing eighths and pushes · **15 Ties into the beat** · 16 Seventh position; G and D · **17 Sixteenths** · **18 6/8** · 19 A♭ and E · 20 Ninth position · **21 A shift inside the line** · **22 Cut time** · **23 Quarter-note and broken triplets** · **24 Four bars** · **25 Eight bars**. New figures are in C (the user's C-first rule); 24–25 mix keys.
- **`REORDERS25`** (flag `r51`, `SR_NEW`): phase-3 levels 1→1 2→2 3→3 4→5 5→6 6→7 7→8 8→12 9→13 10→14 11→16 12→19 13→20; `opts.bpmL` remapped first; the frontier is put back on the level it was (the r50 rule) and new levels under it go to `skip` (open, not passed). `moveReading` still writes the phase-3 numbering (`SR_MOVE`), and r51 runs after it in the same `G('sightread')`: a store moved before or after phase 4 ends the same (checked: `tests/sr_migtest2.py` runs the backup through the phase-3 code (commit 0292a1c) and then the current code, and directly through the current code: both identical, second loads no-ops, other games untouched). A made-up player at phase-3 level 9 with 10 open → level 13, 14 open, 4 9 10 11 in `skip`, tempo and history moved.
- **Generators** (block “SIGHT READING”): `srRhythm(o,len)` (even quarters with rests filling the bar; `rhy:1` = `READ_BARS` repeated for `bars`; RCELL cells bar by bar with tuplet ids kept unique; `'c68'` = 6/8 groups from `SR_68`, a dotted half sometimes in the last bar). `srTie(rh,n,off)` joins `n` neighbouring notes over a beat into one note with `split:[a,b]` (a visible tie; `off` = the first starts on an “and”: ties into the beat). `srMake(o,line,…)` builds the question (grid from `scoreData` in clicks, `rhyStrict`, `tol` when `o.long`, `freeStart` when `o.free`, metro = `levelBpm('sightread', o.lv)`). `srLineQ` options added: `meter`, `bars`, `ties`, `tieOff`, `nat` (the chromatic note may be a half step above or below the next note; at least one ♮ printed; the chromatic note is spelled by direction: sharp leading up, flat leading down), `cells` (a key mark for new cell vocabularies), `long`, `per`. `srShiftQ({wins:[A,B]})`: a two-bar line from one position into the next (up or down), every note before the shift only in the first window, every note from it only in the second, the position numerals (`lab`) over the first note and the shift note. Generators carry `gen.o`; after `GBY` each `o.lv` is stamped with its level.
- **Keys**: settings that came from Reading keep `readLineQ`'s key; every new setting has `sr-<win0>-<key->x-><meter-><tN[o]-><n-><cells-><Nb->midis` or `sr-sh-<from>-<to>-…`.
- **Engine additions**: a line note may carry `split` (written as those tied values) and `lab` (a numeral over the note, `.sc-lab`).
- **Grading**: every Sight Reading question has **`q.rhyStrict`**: a note half a beat or more from its place fails the time (otherwise the three-in-four rule let one note a whole beat late pass). **This also applies to the levels moved from Reading** (stricter than they were). Long lines (24, 25) carry the Scale Runner allowance `tol:{miss:1,late:.15,lateMin:2}` (`missMax = max(1, floor(n/16))`), with `rhyStrict` after it.
- **Tempo per level**: each level has `tp:[lo,hi,start,why]`; `tempoAdvice('sightread',L)` reads it; the level page shows `srTempoRow` (the select stores `opts.bpmL[L]`, the range and the reason, a warning outside it) in place of the mic options' general tempo. Starts: 60 for most, 56 triplets and shifts, 66 3/4, 90 swing, 50 sixteenths, 6/8 (dotted quarter) and cut time (half note).
- **Notes and goals**: `LEVEL_NOTES.sightread` in the 25-level order (12 new notes). Goal R (interim, phase 6 rebuilds it): stage 2 `sightread 1–6`, stage 3 `sight 7–8` + `sightread 7–14`, stage 4 `sight 9–11` + `sightread 15–20`, stage 5 adds `sightread 21–25`; C's appendix `sightread 1–5`; `GOAL_WHY` `sightread:1`, `:7`, `:15`, `:21`. `tests/fix_takes.py` `_SR` maps the old Reading levels to the final numbering (7→1 8→2 9→3 10→5 12→7 13→8 14→6 or 13 16→13 17→14 18→16 20→20).
- **Sweep `SIGHTREAD` block** (every generator, 40 questions): `scoreCheck` clean; **the page read back as a reader would** (signature, then the bar's signs; a tied continuation carries its pitch) gives every target; targets match the written notes, no pitch twice in a row, every note inside the position window; a perfect take passes, one wrong note fails (a pitch that differs from its neighbours: a wrong note equal to the next one reads as a held note with one missing, which the allowance forgives), one note a beat late fails, on long lines one late note over the allowance fails; the naturals level always prints a ♮, the ties levels always a tie, 24/25 have 4/8 bars.
- Looked at by eye (`tests/sr_start.py` → `tests/out/sr-q*.png`, `sr-a*.png`): rests and ties, the natural, triplets, 3/4, ties into the beat, sixteenths, 6/8, the shift numerals, cut time, quarter-note and broken triplets, the eight-bar line on two systems with per-bar shading. All 25 levels start headless and muted on the user's data without an error.
### Phase 5: the pages, done (29 levels, appended: no migration)
- **26 Tunes you may know** (`srTuneQ`, `srTunePage(tu, meta)`): `TUNES` written as pages. `SR_TUNE[id] = [meter (4, 3 or '6/8'), pickup in quarters]` (TUNES stores lengths, not bar lines); the page opens with a rest the length of the bar minus the pickup (so the count-in counts the pickup in) and closes a short last bar with a rest. Row, Row, Row is written in 6/8 (its .33/.67 lengths are triplet eighths: `d → round(d·3)/2`). Key and position: any of `SR_TKEYS` (major C G F D; minor and blues A E D with the relative major's signature) × windows I, II, V, VII that hold every note; a note outside the key is spelled flat for a `b` degree, sharp otherwise (the raised 6 and 7 of minor). **Repeated notes**: consecutive equal pitches are one target on the first one's beat (the detector cannot hear the same pitch struck twice), `q.sr.srcs[i]` is then a list of written notes, all coloured with that slot's result; the prompt says so. **Left out, and why**: Swing Low (its stored phrase is 9½ beats, it does not fall into bars as written), Scarborough Fair (the second phrase, 11 beats, does not fit 3/4 after the first), God Rest Ye Merry, Gentlemen (15 beats after a one-beat pickup do not fill 4/4 bars). 18 tunes are written. **Hook for the user's own tunes (7.4, not built)**: `srTunePage` takes any object of the `TUNES` form (`{id, n, m, ph}`) plus a `[meter, pickup]`; a tune from storage in that form needs only an entry in the pool (and chords, later, as `line.chords`).
- **27 Sixteen bars and 29 A 32-bar page** (`srFormQ({forms, bars, swing})`, `srFormPage(f, nb, o)`): a melody written on the changes of `FORMS` (`SR_FORMS16`: Blue Bossa, Autumn Leaves, Take the A Train, Satin Doll, the first 16 bars; `SR_FORMS32`: A Train, Satin Doll, Autumn Leaves, rhythm changes). `srChordsOf(f, nb)` = chords with their beat and length (`formQual` → `CH` tones). The form's key signature (`SR_FKEY`). Each 8-bar section: a two-bar **motif** (rhythm from the jazz cells `SR_JAZZ`: Charleston, pushes, dotted quarters, eighth runs), a bar more, an **ending bar** (`SR_END`: a long note on beat 1 or 3, mostly then a rest), the motif **again over the next chords** (same rhythm, the same steps from the nearest allowed pitch), a bar, an ending. Pitches: **a note struck on, or held over, beat 1 or 3 is a chord tone of the chord there**; others scale or chord tones; steps and thirds preferred, never the same pitch twice; **every phrase ends on 1, 3 or 5 of its chord** (the 5th from the chord's own formula: ♭5 on m7♭5). A section with the same chords as an earlier one is **copied note for note** (the A returns); one that differs only in its last two bars is copied and gets a new ending. **The bridge** of a 32-bar form (third section) uses longer values (`SR_BRIDGE`) in position VII (frets 7–11) above the middle of the A's range, and the A stays in the lower three quarters of position IV–V (frets 4–8): a page whose bridge is not 2+ semitones higher on average is written again. Accidentals where the chords leave the key (spelled by the signature's side). Chord symbols above (`line.chords`), title “Reading piece on the changes of …”, the prompt says the melody is the app's and only the chords are the tune's. 29 is swing (grid .67).
- **28 The road map** (`srRoadQ`): `CHARTS` (all nine), `chartOrder`, `chartSigns` (converted to the score's per-bar signs: repeats, endings, segno, coda, To Coda, D.C./D.S., Fine). Every written bar different (rhythm and pitches); a bar before a jump ends on a long note or a rest; no pitch twice in a row along the played order. Targets = the written bars' notes in played order; `srcs`/`barOf` point at the written note; a note played twice keeps its worst result on the answer page.
- `srPageMake(o, line, ov)`: the shared question builder for pages (targets, grid, srcs given); `rhyStrict`; the Scale Runner allowance from 16 targets.
- **Question fields**: `q.srMap` (road), `q.srForm` (form). Not `road`/`form`: `q.road` is Along the Neck's free-travel grader (a crash found by the sweep).
- **Tempo** (`tp`): 26 60–100 (72), 27 60–110 (80), 28 50–90 (66), **29 80–120 (100)**: at 140 the simulated 32-bar take failed on time with every note right (swung eighths about 140 ms apart: four unheard, timing misjudged), at 120 two takes of 88 and 114 notes passed clean.
- **Sweep `SRPAGE`**: 1,400 generated pages (200 per 16-bar form, 150 per 32-bar form): `scoreCheck`, bar count, strong beats on chord tones, no pitch twice in a row, every note in frets 4–11, the last note 1/3/5, the A sections equal where the chords are equal, the bridge higher; the road map: reading straight through fails, written bars all different; every written tune page clean in six random keys/positions. The `SIGHTREAD` block reads every page back by `srcs` (targets in played order). Also made robust: the Tunes by Ear “one unheard note in a whole tune” check now drops a note whose neighbours differ (equal neighbours merge into one held note; it failed about one run in four, unrelated to this build).
- **Simulated player** (`tests/sr_player.py [level[@bpm] …]`, after `build.py`): a plucked take (six harmonics, decaying) rendered in the page at the question's tempo and grid, count-in included, fed to the microphone input; the app listens, Basic Pitch detects (GPU, headless, muted), the app grades. Results: L3 (60), L10 triplets (56), L17 sixteenths at 50, 60 and 80, L25 eight bars (35 notes, 60), L26 Auld Lang Syne (28 written notes, 22 targets, 72), L27 16 bars (68 notes, 80), L28 road map (44 notes, 66) all pass; **L28 played straight through the page fails** (15 missing, 18 late); **L29 32 bars: 98 notes at 100 (81 s) pass with one note unheard; 88 and 114 notes at 120 pass clean; 101 notes at 140 fail**. A synthetic take is cleaner than a guitar in a room: these are upper limits.
### Phase 6: goal R, the notes, done (the build is complete)
- **Goal R** is built on the three games: Reading (the notes), Reading Rhythm (the figures), Sight Reading (both, on the guitar). Stages: 1 Notes and values (`fretboard 1–6`, `sight 1–6`, `rhyread 1–5`, `rhytheory 1–3`, `intervals 1–2`, `pulse 1–2`) · 2 First position, in time (`fretboard 7–9`, `sightread 1–6`, `rhyread 6–12`, `rhytheory 4–5`, `pulse 3–4`) · 3 Positions (`scales 1–2`, `sight 7–8`, `sightread 7–14`, `rhyread 13–19`, `pulse 5–6`) · 4 The high neck and jazz rhythm (`sight 9–11`, `sightread 15–20`, `jazzrhy 1–3`, `rhyread 20–24`) · 5 **The page** (`rhyread 25`, `jazzrhy 4–6`, `sightread 21–29`). Each Sight Reading figure level sits in the same or a later stage than the Reading Rhythm level that taught the figure (triplets RR 8 → SR 10, syncopation RR 11–12 → SR 15, sixteenths RR 13–15 → SR 17, 6/8 RR 16 → SR 18, cut time RR 18 → SR 22, broken triplets RR 20 → SR 23). Stage texts rewritten; `GOAL_WHY` `sightread:1/7/15/21`, `sight:7/9`. C's appendix `sight 1–6` + `sightread 1–5`.
- **Notes**: Reading's chapters “Rhythm on the line” and “How to read” moved to `LESSON_NOTES.sightread` (one home per topic: playing a line in time is Sight Reading's); Sight Reading's notes = What sight reading is · Rhythm on the line · How to read · Choosing the position (with “Moving between positions”) · Reading a page (phrases, chord symbols, the A that returns, the road map with a figure) · How the game listens (pitch and time rules, the allowance on long lines, repeated notes, the string not checked, the low-string octave, sixteenths). Reading's lesson card no longer talks about lines (“Whole lines read and played in time are the game Sight Reading”). The texts are mine; the teacher has not read them.
- **Not touched**: `readLineQ` (no game uses it now), `staffRhySVG` (Sing the Page, Hear the Degree, and two figures of the moved chapter). The LOGDB archive keeps the old Reading entries under `sight` with the old level numbers.
- **Not tested on a real guitar: all of it.** The simulated limits are upper limits (a synthetic take is cleaner than a guitar in a room).

## The blues takes the blues scale (20 Nov 2026, user: “why wouldn't I want the blue note to be allowed?”)
Improvise 8, 9, 12 and 13 (`SOLO.blues`, `chorus`, `blues8`, `chorus8`): between the strong beats the key's blues scale (1 ♭3 4 ♭5 5 ♭7) is accepted over I7, IV7 and V7 beside each chord's Mixolydian (`SOLO_SC['b-I']`, `'b-IV'`, `'b-V'`, written from each chord's root; a `bars()` entry carries the set as its fourth element). Before, a note outside Mixolydian passed only as a half-step approach, so ♭3 → 1 over I7 failed. **The rules that mark the changes are unchanged**: the 3rd or 7th on beat 1 of every bar, a chord tone on beat 3 in the eighth-note levels. Still excluded: the ♭2 and ♭6 of the key. Sweep: the sets hold the blues scale and every chord tone, ♭3 falling to the root passes, a blue note on beat 1 does not pass as a guide tone.

## Review of the six-item build (20 Nov 2026, user: “take what he built and make it flawless”; the build below was done by another session from `.claude/build-brief.md`)
Read and tested: the outlines in every key, the scale table of every standard form, the grading of the new levels. The music was right (outline notes, guide tones, the scale family of each chord in all six forms). Changed:
- **Long improvised takes let unheard notes through** (`soloGrade`): one note in sixteen may be missing (`missMax=floor(slots/16)`: 3 in the quarter-note chorus, 6 in the chorus in eighths, 8–9 in a whole form; none under 16 notes), as Scale Runner does. Before, one note the detector lost failed a 144-note form. A broken rule still fails. A chromatic note whose next note was not heard is not judged, and an unheard note is not held against “three different notes in a bar”. **This also loosens the existing Improvise 9.**
- **Eighth-note blues (Improvise 12–13)**: a chord tone on beat 3 as well as the guide tone on beat 1 (`strong:[0,4]`; it was beat 1 only, which left seven free notes in eight).
- **Half a tune and the whole tune (Tunes by Ear 9–10)** carry the Scale Runner allowance (`q.tol`), and the strict-rhythm rule (`q.rhyStrict`) is applied after it (the allowance would have undone it).
- **Scale families**: a plain minor chord (`f-mm`) also takes the ♭6; a dominant written ♯9 or alt takes the altered scale (`f-7a`). Neither occurs in the six forms today.
- **Outlines stay off the lowest string** where another octave fits (no line goes under C3 now; the laptop mic hears those notes poorly).
- The rule line of a question no longer says the same thing twice (“the 3rd or 7th on the first beat… a chord tone on beat 1”); a take of more than 32 hidden notes shows one line before it is played, not a wall of empty boxes.
- Sweep: the allowance (allowed misses pass, one more fails, short lines let none through), the chord tone on beat 3, a late note in a whole tune still fails.
- All 14 new levels started headless and muted on the user's data without an error. **Still not played on a real guitar.**

## Foundations for the goals: six items of the build brief (20 Nov 2026, `.claude/build-brief.md`; the user chose six items from a list of missing skills, everything else is parked)
**14 new levels, all appended; no migration, no reorder, no existing key changed.** Each item: sweep bad 0, `pages_check` all ok, every new level started headless (muted) without a page error, committed and deployed one by one.
1. **Path fixes** (`GOALS` only): A requires Scale Runner 13–15 (`more` keeps 16); B requires Sing It 3–5, 6–9, 10–12 (its `more` entry removed); C requires Pulse 3–6 / 7–12, Reading Rhythm 1–5, Jazz Rhythm 1–3; D requires Chord Spelling 8–10, Rhythm Theory 1–5 / 6–11, Harmony & Analysis 5–7 / 8–10 (its `more` entry removed). `GOAL_WHY` entries for every new step; `A scales:13` rewritten.
2. **Outlines** (Arpeggio Runner 6–8, `outlineQ`, `olLine`, `OL_SH`, `OL_MAJ`, `OL_MIN`): nine straight eighths through II–V–I written as scale steps (II four eighths in one of three shapes, V = the four steps under II's last note, landing one step lower = the 3rd of I); minor uses harmonic minor on V only. ≤66 bpm, count-in, exact pitches, one position (`fitWindow`; all 12 major and 7 minor keys fit every shape). Keys `ol-<shape>-<key>`, `olm-…`. Level 6 is C only.
3. **Eighths in Improvise** (10–13, `SOLO.e8iiV`, `e8turn`, `blues8`, `chorus8`). **A chord's length**: a `bars()` entry is `[root, quality, beats?, scale?, ext?]` (`soloForm`; spec `per`, default 4); slots, grid, the “bar x beat y” text, `guide` (first slot of each chord), the three-notes-a-bar rule (now per four-beat bar) and **the band** (`vampSched`: chords may carry `len`, four-beat chords play as before) all follow it. `soloLine` (the example) was rewritten: the nearest allowed note in the direction of travel (it used to leap an octave when two chords shared a note).
4. **A standard's changes** (Improvise 14–15, `SOLO.std8` / `stdAll`, `forms:WHOLE`; keys `imp-std8-<tune>` / `imp-stdAll-<tune>`): one note a beat, the 3rd or 7th on the first beat of every chord (two beats a chord when a bar has two), chromatic approach allowed, beat 3 free. `formQual(sym)` reads a symbol (quality whose tones are strict + scale family), `formSoloBars(f,nb)`; the families are `SOLO_SC['f-…']` (maj: major+♯4; m7: Dorian∪Aeolian∪Phrygian; mm: melodic minor∪Dorian; hd: Locrian+♮2; dim: whole-half; 7m: harmonic minor from its 5th ∪ Mixolydian, for a 7 whose next different chord is a minor one a 5th below; 7l: Lydian ♭7 for 7♯11; 7: Mixolydian+♯4). A plain major triad (rhythm changes' B♭, E♭) takes 6 and maj7 as chord tones and guide tones. `degSpell` names the notes of these scales. **The scale choices are mine (the brief's list), not checked by the teacher.** One take of up to 144 notes (All The Things You Are, 110 s at 80): a synthetic plucked take ran through `bpAnalyse` headless in 3.8 s with every note heard and graded clean; the capture buffer and `endAt` follow the grid. The chords are previewed for the first eight bars only.
5. **Tunes by Ear in time** (7–10, `tuneTimeQ` via `tunePhraseQ({time})`, options `half`, `all`, `only`): heard with the click, played back after a count-in with the click; consecutive equal pitches (any octave when octave-free) are one slot on the first one's beat (`tuneTimeSlots`), said in the prompt; durations of .33/.67 are snapped to thirds. Octave-free targets work with a grid. **`q.rhyStrict`** (new, these questions only): a note half a beat or more from its place fails the time, on top of the usual three-in-four rule; without it one note a full beat late passed. Keys `tu-<id>-<i>t`, `tu-<id>-half`, `tu-<id>-all`. A one-phrase tune's “half” is the whole phrase.
6. **Shells 14 “The turnaround, two chords a bar”**: `cadQ` rhythm `two` (grid 0 2 4 6, one strum each), with the one-chord-a-bar turnaround as the warm-up generator. **Correction told to the user**: level 13 already had the turnaround with the Charleston, one chord a bar; only two chords a bar was missing.
- `tests/pages_check.py` now launches Chrome with `--mute-audio` as well (it was headless only).
- **Not tested on a real guitar: all of it.** Improvising is graded by rules (correctness), not quality. Repeated notes in Tunes by Ear count once.

## Pulse & Feel 5 would not open: a run's tightness is measured per subdivision (20 Nov 2026, user: “is it a bug that level 6 doesn't open”)
The user held **69 clean bars** on “Switching subdivisions” (24 needed), every 8-hit block within 14–20 ms, and the level stayed locked. `grooveMeets` measured the run's tightness around **one** centre; his lean is about 25 ms early in quarters and about 25 ms late in sixteenths, so the whole run read as over the 25 ms standard while the level row showed ±17 ms (per block). `runSpread(hs)` now groups the run's hits by the number of written notes in their bar, measures each group around its own centre and averages by size (a group under 8 hits is pooled with the other small ones); bars that are all alike give one group, so every other level is judged as before. Reading Rhythm and Jazz Rhythm bars of different density are also grouped: slightly looser there. One-off `mig.ps5`: Pulse & Feel level 5 → passed where a run of ≥24 clean bars is on record and the last blocks are all clean (the user: `g.lvl` 5 → 6, 1,628 answers kept, second load a no-op). Sweep: a different lean per subdivision passes, a loose player fails, alike bars unchanged.

## The ⓘ of a goal step: where it fits in the goal (20 Nov 2026, user: “on each goal level a circled i… explain where it fits within the framework of the goal, like a Berklee teacher… have a complete picture of the elements of the goal”)
`GOAL_WHY[k]` (core half, right after `GOAL_KEYS`): `parts` = the six elements the goal is made of (`[key, name, what it is]`; A: the neck, the harmony, material under the fingers, time, the ear, making a line · B: hearing in the key, intervals, melodies, rhythm, bass and chords, names for what you hear · C: the melody on the neck, chord knowledge, the grips, voice leading, colour, harmonising · D: time, chord knowledge, the grips, voice leading, comping rhythm, the forms · R: pitch on the staff, where it is on the neck, rhythm on the page, time, notation knowledge, the road map) and `steps['gameId:from'] = [part key, text]`: for each of the 119 steps, what those levels give the goal and why it cannot do without them (written from the real level lists). Shown by a small circled *i* (`giBtn`, `.gi`) on every box of the tree (a sibling of the box, top right) and beside every step heading of a goal page; hover or focus shows one floating box `#itip` (`itipHTML`: goal and stage, game and levels, the element, the text, the goal's elements with this one picked out), a click or Enter pins it, Escape, a click elsewhere or scrolling closes it. The goal page also lists the elements under its progress bar (`.qgparts`). Sweep: one entry per step, a valid element, every element used, no stale entry. **When a step of `GOALS` is added or its range changes, write or re-read its `GOAL_WHY` entry** (the key is the step's first level). The texts are mine; the teacher has not read them.

## The goals rebuilt: five goals, shared levels, an appendix, no session plan (20 Nov 2026, user: “add a sight-reading goal… it's ok if different goals have the same levels… dissolve foundations, go strict, an appendix of levels that add to it though not necessary, remove the session plan, I don't look at it”; snapshot before it: tag `v-2026-11-20-before-goals-rebuild`)
**This supersedes the older notes below wherever they say every level sits in exactly one step, mention Foundations (`F`), the 28-session `PLAN`, the session runner or plan ticks.**
- **`GOALS`** = `A` Solo over the changes, `B` Learn a tune by ear, `C` Play chord melody, `D` Comp a tune in time, `R` **Read at sight** (a line from a chart, played on the guitar in time); `GOAL_KEYS`. Each: five stages of `steps:[[gameId,from,to]]` = **only what the goal cannot be done without**, and `more:[[gameId,from,to,why]]` = the appendix (“Also helps, not required”).
- **Levels are shared between goals** (Fretboard 1–6, Chord Spelling 1–5, Pulse & Feel… sit in several): passing them once moves every goal that needs them. Rule inside one goal: a game's steps run without a gap from level 1 and never go back a stage. Foundations is gone: its levels are in the goals that need them, in an appendix, or in the list “In no goal” under the tree (computed, nothing hand-kept).
- **The Goals tab** (was “Plan”): the skill tree only (`vPathPage` = `vTree`), 124 boxes, one row per goal, the appendix as dashed chips under each row (`moreChip`, `.sk-more`); the goal page (`vGoal`) ends with the appendix section (`#gs-more`, chip “Also helps”). `SKILLS` box ids are per goal (`at[k:gid:L]`), so the same levels are a box in each goal that needs them.
- **Removed**: the session calendar and the “Play session” runner (`PLAN=[]`, `runButton()` returns '', `runSession` −1; `STORE.plan` is left in the store, unused). The masters' facts carry the goals' stages (`goals`) where they carried plan ticks. Playing back to back is the goal page's “Play next”.
- Fixed on the way: the goal page's stage chips did nothing (their action had been renamed with the CSS classes).
- Sweep: five goals of five stages, valid ranges, contiguity from level 1 per goal and game, an appendix never repeats a required level of its goal, box prerequisites stay in the goal. 270 distinct levels are required by some goal. On the user's data: A stage 1 (30/95), B stage 1 (18/80), C stage 2 (24/54), D stage 1 (23/69), R stage 2 (42/76).

## Scale Runner: the one-octave forms in C first (20 Nov 2026, user asked whether twelve keys add anything; agreed to a C level before it; 16 levels)
Level 5 “One octave from the root, in C” = `scOctQ({keys:['C'],maxPos:10})`: nine of the ten movable forms (the root on string 5 under the fourth finger does not fit C below fret 12; `maxPos` 10 lets string 4 / first finger in). Level 6 “One octave from any root” is the former level 5 (twelve keys: now about finding the root). `REORDERS24` (flag `r50`) shifts the old levels 5–15 up by one. **`migrateReorder` walks the frontier up through every open level**, so with the user's state (frontier 8 not passed, level 9 open) it would have counted the frontier as passed: `r50` puts the frontier back on the level it was (`F`) and keeps the levels above it in `g.unl`. **Do the same in any later insert below a frontier that has an open level above it.** On the user's data: 985 answers kept, frontier “Thirds, in C” (9), level 10 open, the new level 5 open and not passed, the level page opens on it, second load a no-op.
