Level 8 “Thirds, in C” = the thirds level with every generator in C major only (eight positions); level 9 “Thirds: C, F and G” is the former level 8. `REORDERS23` (flag `r49`) shifts the old levels 8–14 up by one; a player whose frontier was the old level 8 (the user) keeps 8 as the frontier with the old one open as 9; under a higher frontier the new level is open, not passed. `GOALS` (`['scales',8,11]`, `12,12`, `13,15`) and `LEVEL_NOTES.scales` moved with it. On the user's data: 982 answers kept, the 3 answers of the old thirds level are on level 9, second load a no-op. The user asks for a C-only step before a level adds keys (also level 1, 17 Nov): **offer it when a new Scale Runner level starts in several keys.**

## Strummed chords: measured while they ring (20 Nov 2026, user: “the triad shapes strummed guitar version is so bad”; fix-list #91–95)
The five reported Triad Shapes takes showed three faults in the chord path, none of them the playing:
- **The measuring window ended before the chord was complete.** The user rolls the chord (three strings over 300–650 ms); `chordStrums` averaged 60–290 ms after the first string. Now a strum is measured **while it rings**: from 60 ms to `CHO.ring` (1.5 s) after it, or to just before the next strum; `act[m]` = the mean of the upper half of the note's frames in that window (a note that enters late still counts, a blip does not).
- **Handling noise was graded as a strum** (−50 to −60 dBFS against real strums at −21 to −35), including a take with nothing played (#92). A strum under `CHO.minDb` (−46 dBFS) or more than `CHO.near` (26 dB) under the loudest strum of the take is dropped; the live listener's attack test has the same floor, so a touch of the strings no longer starts the 1.8 s countdown that ends the take; no strum left = `silent` = “Nothing heard”, not graded.
- **A fainter note an octave under a sounding one** (E3 under E4) counted as an extra note: `chordCheck` ignores a note one or two octaves under a target while it is weaker than that target (a chord played an octave too low still fails: its low notes are the stronger ones).
- Results: `tests/fix_takes.py 91–95` 5/5 right (were 0/5; #92 not graded); graded straight from the recordings, each real chord passes and five wrong chords against it fail (3rd changed, 5th up, root down, an octave lower, the next inversion); `tests/chord_live.py` all pass (four-note chords, one note off and other chords still fail). Affects every strummed question (Drop-2, Shells, Tension Grips, cadences in time): there the window is bounded by the next strum. **Not re-tested on timed cadences with a real guitar.**
- **The detector tests run hidden now**: `tests/fix_takes.py`, `tests/live_takes.py`, `tests/chord_live.py` launch headless Chrome with `--use-angle=metal --enable-gpu --ignore-gpu-blocklist --mute-audio` (Basic Pitch loads; same results as the windowed run on `chord_live`). No window, no focus change, no sound. Note: in a replay the live listener's attack detection is not identical to the user's session (a replayed take can end earlier than the recorded one); for doubt, grade the recording directly (`bpRaw` → `chordStrums` → `micGrade`).

## Style badges (20 Nov 2026, user: “a badge on the games if they are specifically targeted for Blues, Jazz, Rock & Pop”)
`STYLE[gid]` = a list of `jazz` / `blues` / `rock` (`STYLE_TXT`), drawn by `badgeChips` after the course and kind badges (`.badge.b-st`: Jazz violet, Blues blue, Rock & Pop coral) on the game cards and the level page. Only games whose material belongs to a style have one; the general games have none. **Jazz**: Shells, Guide-Tone Voice Leading, Drop-2, Tension Grips, Arpeggio Runner, Walking Line, Improvise, Repertoire, Harmony & Analysis, Chord–Scale, Modes, Jazz Rhythm, Comping. **Blues**: Blues Boxes, Bends & Vibrato, Phrase and Space, Call & Response. **Rock & Pop** (as a second badge): Blues Boxes, Bends & Vibrato. My choice of which game gets which, told to the user; the sweep checks the table's ids and names. **A new game that belongs to a style gets a `STYLE` entry.**

## The games fixed and proof-read (19 Nov 2026, user: “yes please [fix the game faults]. after that a rundown on games to find error - proof read. correct blatantly wrong stuff”; snapshot before it: tag `v-2026-11-19-before-game-fixes`)
Nine agents, one per group of games, each in its own git worktree (`.claude/worktrees/`, git-ignored), merged into `main` (two one-line conflicts resolved by hand). Each fixed the known faults of its games and then sampled every generator by script (about 400–800 questions each; roughly 330,000 questions and 85,000 generated bars in all) with `/tmp/fwrun/games/gamedump.js` (prints, per level, each question's prompt, choices, expected answer as pitches / names / degrees, explanation and hint, and the bars a continuous level's spec produces; not in the repo). Rule: correct what is plainly wrong, no redesign, no level or `key` or `STORE` change. **No question key, level or migration changed.** Sweep bad 0, `tests/bend_check.js` 19 pass, `pages_check` ok, and the first, middle and last level of every game started in Chrome without an error.
- **Wrong answers that were being asked** (now right): Call & Response 9 `crLongQ` built each bar on the key's tonic (now on the chord's root; bar 2 placed next to bar 1) · Arpeggio Runner 5 `bebopRun` started a 4th high with wrong names · **Scale Runner 13–14 and Blues Boxes 5, 7: a derived scale skipped a note inside the run** in fingering types 2 and 3 (melodic and harmonic minor, blues: 72 of 864 cases; the blues ♭5 in one octave of boxes 2, 4, 5): `scPos` now takes it one fret under the position as a first-finger stretch, `boxScale` one fret above the box on the string of the 4 (**this replaces the earlier “a derived scale can miss a note at the edge” behaviour**; the sweep's check was adapted and a no-skipped-note check added) · Hear the Progression 3: the bass often moved against the answer (`bassOf` folds roots into one octave; the question now plays its own line, `motionBass`) · Hear the Degree 10: lines could not end on a triad tone (odd lengths only now) and level 6 asked chromatic degrees early · Reading 21: dyads were drawn an octave low; level 13: one line in eight needed a natural sign (rejected now) · Walking Line: right bars repeated a note, two “wrong” choices passed the level's own rule, duplicate choices, an empty first beat in the prompt · Hear the Mode 6 marked a second true answer wrong; level 7 named the mode · Call & Response 8 ran above the neck; level 3 now stays in the tonic triad with at most one step note · Intervals & Keys 3 asked minor intervals in the “major intervals” level · Chord Spelling 9–10 and Repertoire 6 could offer two right choices · Shells: `shellCycleQ` always a cycle now; cadences and `shellSeqQ` no longer draw ten-fret jumps under “the hand barely moves” · Drop-2: impossible “octave lower” tabs with negative frets, non-voice-led cadence draws · Shells 11: drop-2 was always A · Bends 8–9 in G sit in the box · Two Hands 4 triplet 3 · Comping swing levels (1–4, 7, 8) expect the swung “and” at .67 (`compBar(p,chord,swing)`), `hemiBar` tied over the bar line and swung.
- **Spelling**: note names follow the chord or key in Modes (`mdSpell`), Blues Boxes, Walking Line, Bends 8–9, Hear the Interval (`ivSpell`), Bass & Top, Guide-Tone Voice Leading, Tension Grips; they were sharps-only or flats-only. Locrian's ♭5 was labelled ♯4; the blue note ♯4 in Call & Response 5. `anChordOf` writes a borrowed root that would be C♭ or F♭ as B or E (♭II7 in B♭ = B7), as it already wrote A and D for B𝄫 and E𝄫; the descending passing diminished chord has a flat root (`dimJobQ`).
- **Explanations that stated one rule for every case** (guide tones “the 7th falls, the 3rd holds” for every pair; a major/minor rule shown for diminished triads; one blues sentence for every form; “down a step (11 half steps up)”), and about ninety stale or false texts in descriptions, level notes, lesson cards and level-page notes, corrected. The full notes were updated where a fix changed a fact.
- **Shared code fixed after the merge** (reported by the agents): `rhySVG` keeps a long value whole only inside its bar (dotted half in 3/4) · `chordMidis` and `earChord` play a 13 without its 11 · `grooveBars` finds the written length of a swung “and” (a long note there was never length-judged in Jazz Rhythm) · `beatName` names the third triplet note “-let” (was “&”) · `rhyBar`/`rhyBarCells`: a bar of rests gets a note of the first rest's length · `grooveMarker`: Reading Rhythm's bar marker is on through level 10 (was 7: levels were inserted under it).
- **Open, the user's or the teacher's to decide** (reported, not changed): **`rhySVG` draws eighth–quarter–eighth as tied eighths** and “e q.” as an eighth tied to a quarter, where print writes a quarter / dotted quarter (several level texts say “recognise it by its look”) · `bendAnalyse` passes an uneven vibrato and a bend that only crosses the band (needs real takes) · Repertoire names chords outside the key by raw distance (G7 = VII7 in A♭) and its whole forms differ in small ways from many lead sheets (plain G7 for Dm7 G7 and the like) · Tension Grips 7: three of the four coloured II–V–I paths cannot keep the top note within a step · Bass & Top allows a 9 or 13 on any m7 (out-of-key top notes on IIIm7, VIm7) · Reading 21 draws a dyad as two staffs · Triad Shapes 3 asks seventh chords · Jazz Rhythm 11 plays only 4/4 tunes and labels every tune swing · Hear the Degree 10 is thinner (3- and 5-note bars) · “Dameron turnaround” played with all dominants · Ionian's colour note given as 7 · the section is still named “Ear & Solfège” · unplayed round generators of the continuous games are stale (sweep only) · two `comping:` keys in `LESSONS`.

## The full notes proof-read (19 Nov 2026, user: a caption under a ties figure named the wrong beats; “proof read yourself right now. these notes should be all correct”)
- **The tool**: `/tmp/fwrun/notes/figdump.js` (not in the repo; rebuild it from this description if needed) evaluates a game's notes with the figure functions wrapped (`NF.grip`, `NF.neckSVG`, `NF.staff`, `NF.circle`, `NF.table`, `rhySVG`, `rhySVG8`, `staffRhySVG`, `boxSVG`, `scPosSVG`, `fretSVG`, `chartVis`…) and prints the notes as plain text with, after every figure, **the data it really draws**: each string's fret, note and interval of a chord box; every dot of a neck diagram; every note and rest of a rhythm bar with its count and length; the staff's notes; table cells. Nearly all 590 figures log data (some bend curves and Repertoire charts are drawn by helpers and were read from their code).
- **The pass**: nine proof-readers, one per group of games, read every item (about 2,430) against that data, recomputed the tables with the app's music functions, and checked claims about the games against the generators. **115 corrections**: Jazz Rhythm 8, Comping 6, Aksak 0 · Fretboard 0, Reading 3, Intervals on the Neck 1, Triad Shapes 1, Arpeggio Runner 2 · Pulse 1, Reading Rhythm 1, Rhythm Theory 1, Two Hands 5 · Intervals & Keys 1, Chord Spelling 4, Harmony & Analysis 4, Chord–Scale 1 · Shells 2, Voice Leading 1 (+3 figure layouts), Drop-2 2, Tension Grips 2 (+2 layouts) · Hear the Degree 5, Interval 0, Chord 6, Progression 2, Mode 0 · Bass & Top 3, Sing It 2, Sing the Page 1, Tunes 4, Call & Response 7 · Improvise 3, Phrase 2, Bends 6, Walking 2, Repertoire 1 · Scale Runner 10, Along the Neck 2, Modes 2, Blues Boxes 4. The big spelling tables (triads and sevenths in every key, the scales, the interval table) had no error; no chord box had a wrong inversion, top note, string or fret. Most faults were prose or captions: a wrong beat or count, a rule contradicted by its own example, a statement about a level that the code does not do, a figure showing something its level never asks.
- **Layout claims**: a caption that says “Top row / Bottom row” is now over figures built as separate rows (one `NF.grips` per row), and `NF.row` of two or three figures never wraps (`.nfrow.nfpair`), so “Left / Right” holds at any width. Checked in Chrome at 1440 and 1000 px for every such caption. Highlighted dots are filled blue: captions no longer say “ringed”.
- **Still not done**: a second reader for the same text (each game was read once); the teacher's eye on taste and emphasis. Names of tunes and players were kept only where the reader was certain.
- **Faults in the games found on the way (open, not fixed)**, besides the earlier list: **Call & Response level 9 `crLongQ` builds each bar's notes on the key's tonic, not on the chord's root** (over Dm7–G7 it plays C Dorian then C Mixolydian: wrong pitches) · Hear the Degree 10: lines with an even number of notes cannot end on a triad tone (`ssLine` parity), and level 6's third generator asks all twelve degrees two levels early · Two Hands level 4: the first beat of each triplet bar is drawn without its 3 (`hStick` rounding) · Bends: `bendAnalyse` passes an uneven vibrato and a bend that only crosses the band; `bendDegQ` in G spreads over frets 5–15 · Walking Line: about 3 % of “pick the bar” questions have fewer than four distinct choices, and a third of the offered right bars repeat a note on beats 2 and 3 · Jazz Rhythm: `tunesBox` says level 12 (it is 11), level 11 skips tunes in 3/4 and 5/4, `hemiBar` does not tie over the bar line · `rhySVG` draws a dotted quarter on the last beat across the bar line · stale descriptions: Arpeggio Runner, Triad Shapes, Reading, Chord–Scale, Jazz Rhythm, Pulse level 5, Drop-2 level 16, Hear the Progression level 3 · `TG['13'].how` (the 13 replaces the 5th in both grips) · an older dead `upperQ` with wrong labels · Scale Runner's `oct` explanation says a position holds “two or three” octaves (one or two).

## Highlighter in the full notes (19 Nov 2026, user: “a highlighter where I can highlight stuff, and it stays there forever”)
On the notes page: “Highlighter: on/off” (`STORE.hlOn`), three colours (`STORE.hlCol`: y g p). With it on, releasing the mouse on a text selection inside one chapter stores a highlight (`hlMouseUp`); a click on a highlight opens a small box to remove it or change its colour (`#hlpop`, actions `hlrm` / `hlset`). **`STORE.hl[gameId] = [{c, s, k, col, t}]`**: chapter index, the text itself, which occurrence of that text in the chapter, colour, time. It is in `STORE`, so it is saved with the progress and is in the export; the import keeps both sets. `hlApply(gid)` runs after every render of the notes page and wraps the text in `<mark class="hl">` (`hlNodes`: the chapter's text nodes, SVG figures left out; `hlWrap`). **Selecting text that is wholly inside highlights removes those highlights** (20 Nov 2026, user request; a selection that also reaches plain text adds a new one as before). A highlight is found by its words: it survives edits elsewhere; **if a chapter's chapters are reordered or its own words rewritten, the highlight stays stored but is not drawn** (nothing deletes it). Checked in Chrome: a paragraph, two bullets and a caption highlighted by dragging, kept after a reload, one removed, switched off.

## Overview: time practised per day (19 Nov 2026, user request)
`timeChart()` above “Answers per day”: green bars, minutes per day for the last 14 days with the number over each bar, and one line under it (total, days practised, average on a practising day). It reads the per-day totals `g.days[day].dur` of every game. **`activityChart` now reads `g.days` too** (`n`, `c`): it summed `g.sessions`, which keeps only the last 80 rounds a game and undercounted busy days (9 Nov: 190 of 235 minutes by rounds).

## Fix list #67–86: sounds that are not notes, the other run form, the allowance (18 Nov 2026)
Twenty reports, nineteen Scale Runner and one Fretboard in time (#76). Four causes, all in `micGradeOne` / `scQ`:
- **Sounds that are not the player's notes** (`ghost(x,i)` in `micGradeOne`, for clicked exact-pitch questions): (1) free start: a sound more than a beat and a half before the run begins (#68, 69, 73, 74, 79: a string touched while getting ready); (2) after the last note: an open-string pitch (the hand letting go, #75); (3) inside the run, while a played note is still sounding or within 160 ms of the next: an open string's pitch in any octave (E A D G B: the mic often hears the octave of a low string ringing in sympathy; #76, 77, 78, 83, 86) or the octave of the note beside it (#67). **Trade-off: in eighths and triplets every sound is within 160 ms of a note, so an open string really hit by mistake between two notes is forgiven there**; at one note a click it still fails (sweep: `ring` passes, `alone` fails).
- **A low note read as its third harmonic** (#70, G2 heard as D4): `heardAs` accepts an octave and a fifth up, as it already accepted the octave, for targets at or below `BPO.lowOct`.
- **The other form of the same run counts** (#84, 85, 86: the bottom-to-top run was asked, the user played the run from the tonic; #80–82: the lower octave of a one-octave run was played): `scQ` gives `targets` for `tonic` ↔ `full` and for the other octaves of `oct`; `micGrade` takes the best. The detector's `want` set includes them, so open-string pitches of the other form are no longer folded away as sympathetic (that is what lost D3 and B3 in #80–82).
- **Not heard: one in sixteen on long runs** (`missMax=max(tol.miss, floor(n/16))`: two in a 33-note run; #67, 71, 72): in fast triplets the model itself loses up to two notes of a run that was played whole. **This loosens the user's rule of 14 Nov (“at most one note not heard”); told to him, his to confirm.**
- **`tests/fix_takes.py`**: when a report's target is no longer produced by any level it rebuilds the question on a clicked run of the game from the report (target, grid, tempo), and a matched question is judged on the report's own subdivision; the windowed test browsers are muted (`--mute-audio`). Replays: #67–75, 77–79, 83–86 all right now (were all wrong); #76 right in an offline grading of its heard notes (its question still does not replay); #80–82 cannot be replayed (no level asks a C major one-octave run now) and are **unverified**; #63 still fails on the wrong note the user reported in it; Note Hunt #14 and #29 still open; #60–62 fail in the replay on timing only (no tempo stored in those old reports).

## A goal as one ladder, and the options beside the levels (17 Nov 2026, user: “for each skill tree goal a screen like the level page where I can play them back to back… put the options on the side”)
- **Goal page** (`vGoal`, view `{name:'goal',k,gid,sel}`; opened from the goal buttons above the skill tree, the tree's ★ goal box, action `goal`): the goal's five stages in order, each step a game heading (“Game page” link) with its levels drawn as on the level page (`.rung.grung`, the same `lvMeter` bars and green outline; tags “next” and “passed”). `goalLevels(k)`, `goalLock`, `goalNext(k,gid,L)` (the first open, unpassed level after a level in the goal's order, else from the top), `goalFit` (switches the game's input when the picked level needs the other one, as the session runner does), `goalOpen`. A click picks a level (action `gsel`), a double click or “Start” plays it; “Play next” at the top plays `goalNext`.
- **Back to back**: `GOALNAV={k}` is set when a round or session starts from the goal page (cleared when one starts from a level page). Then the results page shows `goalBar()` (“Next: … Play next ▶ / Back to the goal”, action `goalnext`), a continuous session ends on the goal page with a short summary and the same button (`grooveEnd`), and leaving a round with no answers returns to the goal page (`quit`).
- **The side panel** (`setupHTML(x,g,sel,head)`, `.lob2` / `.sidecol`): the options block that sat under the ladder (time per question, round length, mix, answer by, the mic and tempo options) and the Start button are now a panel to the right of the levels, sticky under the header, on both the level page and the goal page; from 1180 px wide the page widens to 1380 px (`html.wide2`, set in `render`) and under that the panel sits below the list as before. The panel's actions work on `view.gid` / `view.sel`, which the goal page sets to the picked level.
- **Inside the panel every option is stacked** (18 Nov 2026, user: the two-column rows overflowed the 350 px panel): `.sidecol .optrow` is one column (a small upper-case label, the control under it at full width), selects and button groups fill the width, the mic options' field labels match, notes are small. All of it is CSS scoped to `.sidecol`; the option builders (`tapOptsUI`, `micOptsUI`, `playOptsUI`) are unchanged. Checked by rendering the panel for six kinds of game with no element past its right edge.
- Classes of the goal page start with `q` (`.qgstage`, `.qgbar`…): `.gbar`, `.gcount` and others already belong to the rhythm screens. `tests/pages_check.py` renders the five goal pages.
- Checked in Chrome on the user's data: open goal A, pick a level, start, leave (back on the goal), a finished round shows the bar and “Play next” starts the next level. Not built: Details notes inside the goal page (they are on the game page).

## Scale Runner: a first level in C only (17 Nov 2026, user: “put a level 1 where it is the same but only for C major”; 14 levels)
Level 1 “The whole position, in C” = the whole-position run in C major only (the five basic fingerings, then the eight positions, and the bottom-to-top run); level 2 “The whole position: C, F and G” is the former first level without its C-only generator. `REORDERS22` (flag `r48`) shifts the old levels up by one; the new level is open and not passed (`g.skip`), the level page opens on it (`g.last=1`), and a player whose frontier was the old level 1 keeps level 1 as the frontier. `GOALS` and `LEVEL_NOTES.scales` moved with it. On the user's data: 713 answers kept, the 285 answers of the old first level stay with level 2, frontier 7 → 8, second load a no-op.

“Tonics, then one octave” (`scQ` with `['scale','tonics']` and `['scale','oct']` in the five basic positions of C) is gone; the ladder starts with “The whole position”. `REORDERS21` (flag `r47`, handled like `r43`: the old levels 1 and 2 become the new level 1, which counts as passed when either was) shifts everything down by one; `GOALS` ranges and `LEVEL_NOTES.scales` moved with it (the new first note is a full note). Checked on the user's data: 702 answers kept, frontier 8 → 7, levels 1–3 passed and 4–6 open as before, second load a no-op. **What no level asks any more**: finding every tonic of a position as its own exercise. ## The archive: no timing data is dropped any more (17 Nov 2026, user: “it should keep all my timing data. I don't want my data omitted”)
- **`LOGDB`** (IndexedDB `fretwise-log` / `entries`, records `{g, t, lv, k, ok, n, dr, ab, rt, gp, m}`): every entry pushed to a game's log `g.pl` is also written here (`plTrim(g,gid)`) and never removed. `g.pl` in localStorage stays the working window (300 a game, at least 20 a level) because localStorage holds about 5 MB for everything. `LOGDB.seed()` (flag `STORE.la1`, started from `openAllSync`) wrote once everything the logs held plus `PL_REC`. `LOGDB.count()` / `LOGDB.all(gid)`; the Overview page shows the count. **The archive is per browser and is not in the export text** (“Move your progress” carries `STORE` only): if the user changes browser, the archive needs its own export (not built).
- **`PL_REC`** (in the source: 514 Reading Rhythm and 32 Pulse & Feel entries, `[t, level, clean, hits, lean, spread]`, renumbered to today's levels): recovered from `backups/progress-2026-11-10.json` and `backups/store-before-openall-2026-11-15.json`. `plRecover` (flag `mig.pr1`) gave each level with no timed entry its last 20 back. On the user's data Reading Rhythm shows timing again on levels 1–3, 6, 8, 11–15; **levels 4, 5 and 7 were in neither backup and are lost** (6 shows from its history points), as are Pulse & Feel 1–2.
- Still capped, and **not** archived: `g.sessions` (80 rounds a game), the history points `g.rl` (1,500 a game), `g.days` (400 days). The raw entries in the archive are enough to rebuild timing; rounds are not rebuilt from them. **The user has said he wants no data omitted: do not add or keep a cap that drops data without archiving it first, and say so when one exists.**

## Timing kept for every level (17 Nov 2026, user: “Reading Rhythm has no time right now, should be a bug”)
The level rows read timing from the performance log `g.pl`, which kept the last 300 entries of the whole game: after 300 blocks at Reading Rhythm level 15 every other level showed “Timing —”. `plTrim(g)` (both places that push to `g.pl`) now keeps at least the last 20 entries of every level: over 300, the oldest entry of a level with more than 20 goes first. `rlTiming(g,L,inp)` = the level's timing from its history points (`g.rl`: `ab/tn`, last 12 points), used by `lvModeStats` and `lvMetrics` when the log has no timed entry for the level. On the user's data Reading Rhythm 6, 8, 11–14 show their timing again; **levels 1–5 (and Pulse & Feel 1–2) have no timing stored anywhere** (their sessions predate the timed history points) and show it after the next session there.

## Green outline at the master standard (17 Nov 2026, user: “when a game is master level both on accuracy and time put a green overlay around it… same for the skill tree”)
`lvRow` records `lvRow.last={n,met}` (every bar of the row green: accuracy, and the speed or timing where the level has one); `lvMeter.met` = one of the level's inputs is met: the better of screen and guitar counts (user, the same evening: “best of the two”; first built as every played input). The rung gets `.rung.std` (green border and glow; a selected one keeps its brass border with a green ring). **`lvStd(gid,L)` now returns exactly that**, so the level page, the skill tree (`sk-std`: every level of the box) and “Learned” share one definition; the old `lvStd` judged the two inputs together. `.sk-node.sk-std` is a clear green box, and “ready to start” (`sk-avail`) lost its green ring (grey now): green means the master standard only. A rung that is faded because the current input cannot ask it keeps the green outline (`.rung.std.locked`, still faded; user, the same evening). On the user's data: 24 levels are green (Fretboard 1 2 4–8, Reading 1–6, Rhythm Theory 1 2 3, …), no whole tree box yet.

## The full notes, made simpler (17 Nov 2026, the same day; user: “very complicated… Scale Runner can be explained in way simpler terms… don't repeat the same things in different games… some are good, don't force changes… without omitting important information”)
An editing pass over all 39 games (nine editors, one brief): about 162,600 → 139,500 words, 474 chapters, 590 figures, 313 tables. **The rules the notes now follow, and that any new note must follow:**
- **The plain idea first**, in ordinary words; one idea at a time in the order a learner needs it; the complete system (all twelve fingering types, every case) comes last as a look-up reference. A figure is not re-described in prose. How the grader measures is said only as the rule the player must follow.
- **One home game per shared topic**; other games keep the two or three sentences they cannot do without and may point to the home by its display name. Homes: the neck's layout, the G–B shift, octave shapes → Fretboard · the staff, ledger lines, reading positions → Reading · half and whole steps, interval names, the major scale, key signatures, the circle of fifths, the minor scales → Intervals & Keys · chord formulas → Chord Spelling · diatonic harmony, function, secondary dominants, substitutes, modal interchange → Harmony & Analysis · positions and Leavitt's fingering types → Scale Runner · the modes → Modes (practical: one chord, its scales, the colour note) and Chord–Scale Reflex (the reasoning and the full table; assumes Modes) · pentatonic and the five boxes → Blues Boxes · guide tones through II–V–I → Guide-Tone Voice Leading · pulse, subdivision, counting, 2 and 4, swing eighths → Pulse & Feel · written figures and their counts → Reading Rhythm (Rhythm Theory keeps meter, beaming, signs, dictation) · the Charleston and pushes → Jazz Rhythm (Comping keeps the hands and the chords) · hearing the key → Hear the Degree · the blues forms and song forms → Repertoire · hear, hold and play back a phrase → Tunes by Ear · general practice advice → nowhere as a chapter.
- Scale Runner was reworked most (the user's example): it opens with what a position is and one shape (C major in position VII), then stretches, then the game's order; the twelve types are the last chapter.
- Each editor reported what it cut that “might be missed” (per-key tables, some second figures); the originals of this pass are not kept in the repo, the version before it is commit `The full notes rewritten as textbook chapters` (git history).
- One more game fault reported and still open: Comping's swing levels seem to grade the off-beat at the straight eighth (`COMP` onsets at .5; `cComp` does not apply the 0.67 shift).

## Speed shown in real seconds on mixed levels (17 Nov 2026, user: “Intervals & Keys level 1's speed metric is so low for no reason”)
`g.ht[L]` holds ratios (time ÷ the question's own target). Every display multiplied the median ratio by the level's median target (`lvTargetSec`); on a level whose questions have very different targets (Intervals & Keys 1: one note 2.5 s, which degree 7.7 s, a whole scale 8.5 s) that showed 19 s for answers that take about 7. Now: `g.hs[L]` stores the same clean answers in seconds (from now on; `migrateReorder` remaps it), `lvMixed(gid,L)` = targets differ by more than 1.5×, and `lvTargetEff(gid,L)` (used by the lobby row, the stats page, the masters' facts) = real seconds ÷ ratio on mixed levels (until `hs` has three answers: the level's average answer time), else `lvTargetSec`. The unlock rule itself (median ratio ≤ 1) is unchanged. On the user's data Intervals & Keys 1 reads 7.2 s against 2.9 s (was 19.0 against 7.7). **Open question put to the user**: the one-note target (1.5 s + 1 s = 2.5 s) against their 6.5 s average, and “which degree” on a number key having the looser 7.7 s choice target.

## The full notes are textbook chapters (17 Nov 2026, user: “full-fledged textbook information… chord shapes, infographics, the clef and musical notation… never fluff, don't reduce”; snapshot before it: commit df… `Reading Rhythm: quarter-note and half-note triplets`)
- **A page of its own**: the lesson card's button “Open the full notes” → view `notes` (`vNotes`, `notesBody` cached in `NOTES_HTML`, actions `notes`/`notesec`): the master's name, a chapter list, the chapters. The collapsed `<details>` inside the lesson card is gone.
- **Content**: `LESSON_NOTES.<id>=[{h,p:[…]}]`, one statement per game in the block “THE FULL NOTES” just before the VIEWS marker (some are IIFEs with helpers; many table rows are computed when the script loads, so the block must stay after the music core and the generators). 39 games, about 160,000 words, 665 figures, 377 tables; the script went from 1.3 to 2.2 MB. The `const LESSON_NOTES={…}` literal holds only the retired Guide Tones' old notes. An item of a chapter: a string (paragraph), an array (bullets), `{sub}`, `{fig:()=>html,cap}`, `{tbl:[head,rows],cap}`, `{tip}` (“From the player's chair”); `noteItem` renders one, `noteText` gives plain text (the masters' chat prompt carries the notes).
- **The figure kit `NF`** (before `const LESSON_NOTES`): `grip(title,tab,root,labels)` / `grips` (chord box with the interval in every dot; **labels are verified against the sounding notes**), `neck('6/8=C* 5/7=E!')`, `neckSVG(dots)`, `scale(root,formula,lo,hi,{names,hl,strings})`, `where(notes,lo,hi)`, `staff('C4=1 E4 | C4+E4+G4',{ks})` (treble staff as written for guitar, key signature, naturals), `rhy('q ee | trip q h',{meter,ts})` (from `RCELL`; a bar must fill its meter), `circle({key}|{minor})` (three rings: major, relative minor, diminished, the count of sharps or flats; the seven chords of a key picked out with numerals), `table`, `row`. Faults go to `NF.err`; the sweep fails on any, and `tests/pages_check.py` renders every notes page.
- **How it was written**: nine agents, one per group of games, from one brief (complete coverage of the subject, shapes from the app's own generators, the user's conventions, no copyrighted melodies, nothing attributed to a book unless already in the notes), each draft checked (≥8 chapters, ≥1,800 words, ≥8 figures or tables, 5 for ear games, ≥3 tips, no em dashes, Berklee numerals) and looked at as rendered pages. I looked at eight chapters across eight games after the merge; **the text has not been proof-read line by line by me or by the user's teacher**.
- **When levels are added or changed, the game's chapters need the same change** (they describe what the levels ask). To edit a chapter, edit its statement in the block; keep it one statement per game.
- **Faults in the games that the writers reported and that are still open** (not fixed here): Arpeggio Runner level 5 `bebopRun` starts a fourth above the named root and shows wrong note names · `rhySVG` splits a dotted half in 3/4 from the second bar on (`t%4`) and never draws eighth–quarter–eighth with a quarter · `CH['13']` holds the 11 (Hear the Chord level 6) · `shellCycleQ` from string 5 falls back to a II–V–I question about half the time · `gtVLQ` IMaj7 → VI7 accepts only the upward move · Hear the Mode level 7's prompt names the mode · the descending passing diminished chord is spelled ♯II°7 · `COMP.bossa.d` says “and 3” for beat 4 · stale texts: `LESSONS.scales.how` (“not from the root”), `LESSONS.sing.how` and Sing It's area (“solfège”), `IVREF` and `LESSONS.eariv` (song mnemonics), `GAMES` descriptions of arp, triads, cr, comping, jazzrhy; several `LEVEL_NOTES` (analysis 8, fretboard 13, sight 17, cr 1, jazzrhy 2, comping 7, scales 2) · `FORMS` note for All The Things You Are says C is a major third below A♭.

## The blues route (18 Nov 2026, user: “I want to study the blues / Frusciante / Hendrix route as well… please add them, don't add double stops and fills”; snapshot before it: tag `v-2026-11-18-before-blues-route`; 40 games)
A second road beside the jazz goals; the user is “fully ok” with it shifting priorities. Four additions; **Double Stops and Fills was offered and declined.**
- **Blues Boxes** (`boxes`, neck, mic, 7 levels, COURSE 101, life/core): `penBox(rp,k)` = box k of the minor pentatonic: twelve notes in a row from the k-th scale note on string 6, two to a string (`BX_PENT`; `PENT` already existed as an object — name clash caught by the syntax check). `boxScale(rp,k,semis,wide)` = another scale inside the box's frets (blues scale; major pentatonic with one fret more below). `boxQ({boxes,scale,join,mix,div,roots})`: up and back; `join` = this box up, the next down; `mix` = minor up, major down in the same place. Over the I7 chord (`q.vamp`, shell voicing). Levels: box 1 · 2 and 3 · 4 and 5 · from box to box · the blue note · major and minor in one place · eighths and triplets. Sweep: A's five boxes equal the standard shapes (5-8 5-7 5-7 5-7 5-8 5-8 …), every box in every key, the blues and major shapes in A.
- **Phrase and Space** (`phrase`, applied, mic, 6 levels, COURSE 201, life/core): `PHR[id]` (bars, `play`/`rest` bar ranges, chord root per bar, scale, rules, a passing example), `phraseQ(id)`, `phraseGrade` (`q.phr`): silence in the rest bars (a new note there fails; a ringing one cannot be heard), ≥3 notes in each playing part, only scale notes (`PH_SC` blues + major 3rd; `PH_WIDE` adds 2 and 6), `rep` (same pitches, same place in the bar within 0.4 beat), `vary` (same but the last note or two), `open`/`land` (question not on the root, answer on 1 3 5 ♭7), `landAt` (beat 1 of bar 5 on the root, 3rd or ♭7 of IV7), `differ`. Count-in, the band with the bar's chord (`vamp.cin`). Levels: play two rest two · say it again · change the ending · call and answer · into the IV chord · a chorus from one idea (AAB over twelve bars). The bar map (`.phbars`) shows play / rest / free.
- **Bends in the key** (Bends & Vibrato 8–9, `bendDegQ`, `BEND_DEG`; `bendQ(kind,at)` takes a fixed spot): 4→5, ♭7→1, 2→3, 5→6 whole steps; then 4→♭5, the curl on ♭3, bend-release, bend with vibrato. The chord is heard first; **no band** (it would disturb the pitch curve).
- **The lick book** (Call & Response 10–11, `LICK_MORE` + `BLUES_LICKS` = 24 phrases, all written for the app; `crBlues({keys})` keys the item by lick, not by key, so weak licks return in any key). `REORDERS19` (flag `r45`) puts the two levels before the beta double-stops level.
- **The band is on by default** in `VAMP_ON` = modes, boxes, phrase (headphones); own-line questions (`q.own`, `msolo`, `solo`, `road`, `walk`) no longer pass the example's pitches to the detector as “asked-for” notes.
- Goals: Blues Boxes 1–4 and 5–7 and Phrase and Space sit in goal A (stages 2–4). Verified in Chrome: questions render, listening and the band start, migrations are no-ops on the user's data. **Not played on a guitar**: all four.

## Scale Runner: first- and second-finger shapes weighted (18 Nov 2026, the teacher: “pay most attention to the shapes that start with the index or middle finger”)
`scHomeFinger(rp,P)` = the tonic sits under finger 1 or 2 on string 6 or 5; `scPickPos` draws such positions half again as often (weight 3 to 2; the user asked for “slightly”), nothing is dropped. In C: II and VII (second finger), III and VIII (first finger). **Position VIII added** to `SC_SEVEN` (eight positions now; type 4C). `scOctQ` draws fingers 2 and 1 three times each to finger 4's two. Sweep: the four positions, the ratio, position VIII's type.

I had, in several places: levels were removed because they shared material with another level, though they asked for a different action. **Rule from now on: remove a level only when another level asks for the same action on the same material; singing, hearing, naming, marking on the neck and playing are different actions. List what would be lost and let the user decide.** The user then asked for only the clearly necessary ones back, staying lean.
- Restored in Scale Runner (`REORDERS18`, flag `r44`; 14 levels): **“Modes in every position”** (level 12: `scModeQ('home')` and `scModeQ('one')` — the Modes game only plays one octave from a root on strings 6 or 5), **seventh chords as their own level** (10; merged with triads, a level could be passed on triads alone), and the **bottom-to-top run** as a third generator of level 2 (the user had asked for both run forms).
- Left out on purpose (not clearly necessary; each can come back from tag `v-2026-11-17-before-audit-fixes` or `…-before-lean-neck`): mode seventh chords, Triad Shapes “Upper structures” on the neck, Intervals on the Neck “Double stops”, Reading “Every place it lives”, Arpeggio Runner's guide-tone line, Guide Tones on the Neck (retired, data kept). Still merged: melodic with harmonic minor, pentatonics with blues, the Along the Neck pairs.
- The Jessica proposal (analysis only, **not built**) withdrew its removals of “Sing intervals”, “Sing it, then play it” and Hear the Chord's inversions.

## The audit and its fixes (17 Nov 2026; user: “scrutinize everything… fix/remove/improve list on all games”, then “2-3-4-5-6”; snapshot before it: tag `v-2026-11-17-before-audit-fixes`)
A per-master audit of all 39 games from the real level lists (`/tmp/fwrun/audit.js` prints every level with its question types and number of distinct items). The user chose items 2–6; item 1 (play-testing) they do themselves as they play.
- **Reading, 22 levels** (`REORDERS15`, flag `r40`, new `RD_NEW`): `readLineQ(win,strings,rhy,swing,{key,chrom})` — `key` (`READ_KEYS`: G D A F B♭ E♭) draws the signature (through `staffRhySVG(…,ks)`) and uses that key's notes; `chrom` puts one written accidental in a C line (the half step under the next note). New levels: 10 lines in G and F · 13 lines in D and B♭, accidentals · 18 seventh position (was only a mark-the-note task inside the last level) · 21 dyads split from ninth position.

Shells 12–13, Drop-2 17. Generator edits: Scale Runner in C, F, G from level 4; Tension Grips on four roots from level 2; Improvise items per key.

## Scales, modes and the vamp (17 Nov 2026; the user's teacher: “the scale exercises are disorganized and miss the point”; snapshot before it: tag `v-2026-11-17-before-scales-rebuild`; 39 games, 119 boxes)
The user: understanding and correctly studying scales is the most important goal; unlock the neck and understand the modes. The teacher's points, as relayed: a run should start and end on the tonic; a scale is first of all do to do, not a position; **vamp on a chord and play its modes over it** (the user confirmed the reading: for each chord type, the scales that fit it).
- **Scale Runner rebuilt, 23 levels** (`REORDERS14`, flag `r39`; inserted levels `SC_NEW` open, not passed; **`g.hist`/`g.hm` cleared** because the run itself changed, counts and items kept). Order: 1 find the tonics · 2 one octave 1 to 1 · 3 the whole position from the tonic · 4 seven positions · 5 eighths · 6 triplets · 7 one octave from any root (movable forms) · 8 up to the 7th and back · 9 bottom to top · 10 first modes (2 to 2 Dorian, 5 to 5 Mixolydian) · 11–14 thirds, triads, sevenths (one octave from the tonic first) · 15–16 twelve keys in one position · 17–19 all modes · 20 melodic minor · 21 harmonic minor · 22 pentatonics · 23 blues scale.
- **Run forms** (`scRun(Pz,homePc,dir,j)`, `SC_RUNS`): `tonics` · `oct` (tonic to tonic and back) · `bh` (1 up to 7 and back, thirteen notes: in eighths the chord tones fall on the beats; from Barry Harris's teaching, per secondary sources) · `tonic` (lowest tonic → top note → bottom note → tonic; the teacher's form, now the default) · `full` (bottom to top and back, the old form, kept as level 9). Patterns take a third element `'oct'` = one octave from the lowest tonic. **I could not confirm from public sources which run form Leavitt's books prescribe; both are built.**
- **Other scales in position** (`SC_SCALES`, `scPos(keyPc,P,pcs)`): melodic and harmonic minor, major and minor pentatonic, blues scale are played inside the major key's fingering type (minor pentatonic and blues inside the relative major's). A derived scale can miss a note at the edge of a position. The blues ♭5 is labelled `♭5` (`scDeg`; `DEG_SEMI`/`DEG_STEP` have the entry, `DEG_ORDER` does not).
- **Movable one-octave forms** (`scOctQ`): root on string 6, 5 or 4 under finger 2, 4 or 1 and on string 3 under finger 1 (the others do not fit an octave inside one position; the sweep proves it); the finger fixes the position.
- **Modes** (`modes`, neck, mic, 12 levels, COURSE 201, life/core, in `HAT_GAMES`): `MD[name]` (half steps, degree labels, colour notes, parent scale), `MD_FAM[fam]` (chord, symbol, its scales; the first is the reference), `MD_BRIGHT`, `mdCare` (careful note = a scale note a half step above a chord tone; on dominants only the natural 4), `mdDiff`, `mdParent`, `octPath` (one octave from a root spot inside five or six frets), `neckMapSVG`. Questions: `modeRunQ({set,pair,bright,div})` (one octave up and back over the chord; `pair` = a second scale of the same chord straight after; `bright` = the next scale in bright-to-dark order over a bare root) and `modeSoloQ` / `modeSoloGrade` (`q.msolo`: two bars of the player's own eighths; only scale notes, the colour note at least once, no repeated note, land on a chord tone; two empty eighths allowed). Levels: II–V–I modes · major chord Ionian/Lydian · minor chord Dorian/Aeolian · Phrygian · dominant and half-diminished · one root bright to dark · eighths · own line · melodic minor modes · six dominant scales · symmetric and harmonic minor · own line on any chord.
- **The band** (`VAMP`, `vampStart/vampSched/vampStop`, `q.vamp={bpm,chords:[{root,pcs}]}`): piano chord on 1 and the “and” of 2, bass on 1 and 3 (low piano), hi-hat on 2 and 4, scheduled 0.35 s ahead. It starts with the click in `micListen` when `opts.vamp!==false` and stops in `clickStop`. **The mic hears it through speakers: headphones are required** (option “The band plays the chord under you”). The dominant is voiced as a shell so every dominant scale fits.
- **Vamp room** (`vVamp`, view `vamp`, `STORE.vamp`, actions `vamp/vampset/vampgo`; button on the level pages of `VAMP_LINK` games): root × chord × scale × tempo, the band loops, the scale over the whole neck (gold root, plain chord tones, blue ring colour note, faint passing notes), nothing graded. It stops when the page is left.
- **Paper levels from the two harmony books' contents** (appended, no migration): Chord–Scale 8–11 (`secDomScaleQ` — tensions of a secondary dominant computed from the key; `miScaleQ` — IVm7 Dorian, ♭VIMaj7 and ♭IIMaj7 Lydian, ♭VII7 and subV7 Lydian ♭7; `pentaQ` — the three major pentatonics inside a mode, computed; `upperQ`, `slashQ`), Harmony & Analysis 13–17 (`extDomQ`, `dimJobQ`, `decQ`, `domMoveQ`, `modQ`, `constQ`, `coltraneQ`), Tension Grips 10 fourth voicings (`QUARTAL` through `shellTenQ(mode,roots,TAB)`).
- Sweep: every key × position: the tonic run starts and ends on the lowest tonic, touches top and bottom, covers every note without a skip; the octave is tonic to tonic; the run to the 7th has 1 3 5 7 5 3 1 on the beats; derived scales stay in their set and their type's frets; the octave forms; every mode holds its chord, equals its parent scale, bright to dark lowers one note a half step; the careful notes match the textbook list; the improvising rules fail what they should; the harmony facts (tensions, upper structures, three shared seventh chords between keys a fifth apart, the E minor fourth voicing x-7-7-7-8-7).
- Verified in Chrome: the ladder renders, the band starts with listening and stops with it, the vamp room plays and stops on leaving, the migration on the user's data (frontier old 5 → 11, 465 answers kept, second load a no-op). **Not played on a real guitar and not heard by ear**: the band's balance, the graded vamp levels, the long tonic runs (33 notes).
- Not built from the books: playing outside, reharmonisation beyond the tritone substitute and Coltrane changes, the three named reharmonisations (copyrighted tunes), Dorian ♭2 / sus♭9.

## Ear and solfège additions (16 Nov 2026, user: three ear-training books' contents checked against the games; snapshot before it: tag `v-2026-11-16-before-ear-build`; 38 games, 117 boxes)
The user sent the contents pages of three ear-training methods and asked what was missing; then “go with all, no syllable switch” (degrees stay numbers). Nothing is copied from the books: only the topics.
- **Sing the Page** (`sightsing`, ear, mic/voice, 9 levels, COURSE 201, life/core): a line on the staff, the cadence and the tonic, sung from the page. `singPageQ(o)`; `ssLine(len,lo,hi,o)` makes the line as scale-step numbers (0 = tonic; steps, skips where `o.skips` allows — `o.triad` only inside the tonic triad — a line keeps its direction, never repeats a note, ends on the tonic triad; steps-only lines use odd lengths because of parity); `ssNotes(root,idx,minor)` gives degree, midi, name and staff position (a line that dips under G3 is written an octave higher); `ssKeySig`; **`staffRhySVG(ws,ok,dur,ks)` now draws a key signature** (`ks` sharps > 0, flats < 0). Levels: 1 2 3 → to 4 → to 5 → to 6 → whole scale → skips in the tonic triad → thirds and the fourth → minor (A, E, D, G: the raised 7 under the tonic carries a sharp; other minor keys would need a natural sign, which `ACC` cannot draw) → with written rhythm (pitches only are checked). Keys up to three sharps or flats.
- **Voice games**: `VOICE_GAMES` / `isVoice(gid)` replaced every `gid==='sing'` test (built-in pitch detector, cents shown, no octave/click options). **A new voice game goes in `VOICE_GAMES`.**
- **Melodic dictation with rhythm** (Hear the Degree L10–11, `melDictQ`): `ui:'rhywrite'` with `q.mel`: every written note needs a scale degree (buttons or keys 1–7, `rwPitch`, `rwPitched`; a new value is refused until the last note has its degree); the bar is drawn with the degree over each note (`rhySVG` draws `e.p` as `.rh-deg`); `melGrade` checks the rhythm with `rhyWriteGrade`, then the degrees in order. The answer is shown on a real staff in its key. No rests in these melodies.
- **Sing It L10–11** (`singArpQ`, `ARP_PROGS`): hear a progression, sing every chord from its root up (triads; then IIm7–V7–IMaj7 in four notes and a minor cadence). Progressions are chosen so no note repeats across a chord change (a repeated note is one grader slot).
- **Hear the Progression: 20 levels** (`REORDERS13`, flag `r38`, new levels `EP_NEW` open but not passed): 2 I, IV and V one at a time and in any order (`earOneQ`, `earFreeQ`) · 4 every chord of the key · 5 the diatonic cycle (`earCycleQ`, `CYC_MAJ`/`CYC_MIN`) · 6 inversions of I, IV and V (`earBassInvQ`: which chord tone is in the bass; dictation with tokens `I/3`, `I/5`) · 9 every chord of the minor key (`P_MIN`: Vm and V7 both) · 18 suspension, passing 7th, line cliché (`earDeviceQ`, `EP_DEV`) · 19–20 harmonise a melody (`harmQ(pool)`: the notes of each bar are tones of exactly one chord of the pool; shown as degrees per bar, the melody is played). Chord objects come from `dch(t,d,q,inv)`; numerals from `epTok` (= `bnum`).
- Name clashes found by the sweep while building (`sightQ` and `earInvQ` already existed): the new ones are `singPageQ` and `earBassInvQ`.
- Sweep: sight-singing lines are in their key, never repeat a note, no leap over a fifth, stay on the staff, the reference positions hold (C = ledger line below, G = second line, F = first space; signatures G 1♯, E♭ 3♭, D minor 1♭, E minor 1♯); a melody dictation fails on one wrong or one missing degree; sung arpeggios never repeat a note; every harmonised bar fits only its own chord; inverted chords have the named tone in the bass; the diatonic numerals and the cycle's root motion.
- Verified in Chrome: the staff with key signatures, a dictation typed on the keyboard and graded right, the migration on the user's data (no change: level 1) and on a made-up player at old level 6 (→ 11, open 9 → 14, inserted levels open, second load a no-op). **Not tested with a real voice**: sight singing and the sung arpeggios (up to 12 notes in one take). Not built: conducting patterns; solfège syllables (user: no).

## The rhythm rebuild (16 Nov 2026; the teacher's four points and the drum-book summary; snapshot before it: tag `v-2026-11-16-before-rhythm-rebuild`; 37 games, 114 boxes)
Sources: the teacher (2 and 4 makes no sense without music; advise a tempo per level with the reason; “be the click”; aksak), and the user's summary from drum reading methods (two-hand independence, a longer reading ladder, road maps). Nothing is copied from a book: the levels follow the usual teaching order and the bars are generated.
- **Notation engine.** `rhySVG` options: `bw` (beat width), accents (`e.acc` → `.rh-acc`), rests and quarters inside tuplets, compound dots with `noTup`, composite bars `sub`/`subTs` (changing meters), `signs` (`rhySigns`: repeat start/end, final bar, 1st/2nd endings, segno, coda, To Coda, text marks, one- and two-bar repeats; all drawn as shapes, never Musical Symbols glyphs), `nums` (bar numbers), `noCnt`. `rhySVG8` draws meters counted in eighths, beamed by `groups`. New `RCELL` cells: `rsss sssr rse esr trn tnr tnrn sext ste est c6a–c6d`.
- **Spec fields** (continuous levels): `tp:[lo,hi,start,why]`, `alt:[specs]` (one per session in turn, `g.altN[L]`; index 1 is played first, so lists are written last-item-first), `sub/subTs`, `accRead`, `groups/bw/ts`, `cinClick`, `accBeat`, `back:{tick?,band?}`, `hands`, `per`, `chart`.
- **Tempo advice** (`tempoAdvice(gid,L)`, `levelBpm`, `g.opts.bpmL[L]`): every continuous level shows a recommended range, a starting tempo and the reason; the tempo is remembered per level. From `c.tp` when given, else by rule from the closest two notes of the level. **The teacher should confirm the Pulse & Feel table.**
- **Reading Rhythm: 26 levels** (`REORDERS11`, flag `r36`, new levels in `RR_NEW` open but not passed): added 2/4, sixteenth rests, 6/8 with sixteenths, cut time, broken triplets, sixteenth-note triplets, 5/4 and 3/8, changing meters, three accent levels (mic), and **L26 “Road map, played”**: `cChart(names,cells)` → `mkChart` (every written bar has different attacks, so a wrong turn is wrong notes; the bar with the repeat dots keeps its last half beat free), the whole chart stays on the page (`chartPage`, `chartSys`; no place marker), the expected bars are `chartOrder(chart)` again and again.
- **The road-map model** (`CHARTS`, `chartOrder`, `chartSigns`, `CHART_TXT`): rep, rep2, end1, end2, dcfine, dsfine, dscoda, dccoda, full. A repeat back to the very beginning has no start sign. No template repeats a section inside a D.C./D.S. (the “no repeats on the D.C.” convention is only mentioned in the note). All charts are multiples of four bars, four to a line.
- **Rhythm Theory: 12 levels** (appended): 10 repeats, endings, measure repeats (`chartNextQ`, `chartCountQ`, `mrQ`, `roadFactQ('rep')`, chart drawn by `chartVis`), 11 D.C./D.S./Fine/coda and the fermata, 12 accents, dynamics, articulation (`artQ`, `artSVG`, `ART`, `DYN`).
- **Pulse & Feel: 17 levels** (`REORDERS12`, flag `r37`, `PU_NEW`): the 2-and-4 levels, drop-outs, swing and feel levels play over a band (`back`: walking bass `gWalk` over `GBLUES` on the low piano register, hi-hat `gHat`); L8–10 “Be the hi-hat” (the click on every beat for eight bars, then beat 1 only: the player is 2 and 4). No bass sample yet: the bass is the piano's low notes.
- **Two Hands** (`twohands`, rhythm, 19 levels, COURSE 201, warm/support, `hands:true`): keys F (left) and J (right), buttons `gtapL`/`gtap`; every expected hit carries its hand (`o.h`), only that key answers it (`grooveMatch(t,lv,press,hand)`); two stacked staves, right on top (`.gst2`), always the scrolling view. Bars: `{on,ev}` right, `{on2,ev2}` left; helpers `cH`, `hb`, `hOn`, `hStick`, `H_QT`, `H_HT`, `H_RIDE`, `H_DQ`. Ladder: together → alternating and stickings → two lines → 2 against 3, 3 against 4 → backbeat, ride pattern, clave, bossa → a pattern under syncopation → L19 uneven beats (aksak in two hands).
- **Aksak** (`aksak`, rhythm, 14 levels, COURSE 301, warm/side): meters in eighths from groups of two and three: 5/8, 7/8, 8/8 as 3+3+2, 9/8 as 2+2+2+3 (set against 3+3+3), 10/8 as 3+2+2+3, 11/8 as 2+2+3+2+2, 13/8 as 2+2+2+3+2+2. Builders on `c8(groups)`: `c8b` (one tap per group, click on the groups), `c8e` (every eighth), `c8r` (reading, click on beat 1 only); tempo in eighths a minute. Turkish names only where certain: aksak (9/8), curcuna (10/8). **The teacher should confirm the 11 and 13 groupings and the names.**
- **Hi-hat on 2 and 4** (`opts.hat24`; `HAT_GAMES` = scales, along, arp, improv; and Comping in 4/4): after the count-in the click is a hi-hat on 2 and 4 only (`mTick`, `clickRun`, `hatStop`; Comping swaps its spec's click in `grooveStart`).
- Sweep additions: every chart unrolls to its order written out by hand, signs stay on their line, chart bars all differ, every aksak bar fills its meter with groups of two and three.
- Verified with simulated players in Chrome (/tmp/fwrun/mig/th.py, ak.py, road.py, hat.py): right hands clean, swapped hands fail; aksak levels clean, skipped notes break the run; a player who follows the road is clean, one who reads left to right fails at the first turn; hats fall on beats 2 and 4. **Not heard by ear and not played on a real guitar or by the user yet.** With strumming into the mic the band needs headphones. Comping did not get the band.

## Loudness gate (6 Nov 2026, fix-list reports #30–36)
Basic Pitch reads notes into near-silence (takes peaking at −52 to −58 dBFS produced B2, G3, D6…). `bpGate(ev,x,sr,offMs)` in `bpAnalyse` drops any note quieter than `BPO.gateDb` (−46 dBFS) or less than `BPO.gateAbove` (10 dB) above the take's noise floor (20th percentile of 50 ms frames); a note's level is its first 50–200 ms. An empty take is never graded: `stopListening` reports `silent` whenever no notes/onsets came in (not only when it stopped by itself), and `micListen` shows “Nothing heard”. `tests/fix_takes.py` treats a not-graded take as right when the report says nothing was played. Live recordings check went from 6 to 4 errors.

## Fix list (how the user reports problems)

The user writes problems in the app: a box under every answer ("Tell Claude…") and the **Fix list** page on home. Reports are stored in the browser's IndexedDB (`fretwise-fix`/`reports`) with the question context (`fixContext`: game, level, prompt, answer, what was given, mic target/heard/per-slot/detector) and the take's audio for mic questions. When the user says "check the fix list", run `.venv/bin/python tests/read_fixlist.py`: it copies Chrome's storage to a temp profile (read-only) and writes `fixlist/reports.json` + WAVs (git-ignored). Work through the reports; the user deletes fixed ones on the Fix list page. After a fix, `.venv/bin/python tests/fix_takes.py` replays every reported take into the same question through the live path and prints old vs new grading. Note Hunt changes must also keep `tests/hunt/skip2.js` (cuts single spots out of the takes; run from tests/hunt/) catching skipped spots: leniency that passes every take but stops catching skips is not a fix.


## Nov 2026 additions (audit items 1–4)

- **`triadQ` name clash fixed**: the Triad Shapes generator is `triadShapeQ`; `triadQ(ns)` is the quality helper. The sweep now fails on any question text containing code, `undefined`, `NaN` or a `?` quality (proved by reintroducing the clash: 1,503 bad).
- **Chord grading (beta)**: `CHO`, `bpRaw`, `chordStrums`, `chordCheck`, `strumGrade`. Questions tagged `grip:true` become “strum it” in play mode (`q.strumSeq`, `q.strumAlts`; option “Strum chords”, `opts.strum`). Notes below C3 are reported as not checked (laptop mic). Tuned on recordings/chords.wav: right 9/10, other chord 0/20, one-note-off 0/74. Regression: `.venv/bin/python tests/chord_live.py`.
- **Bends & Vibrato** game (`bendQ`, `bendAnalyse`, `bendGrade`, `bendSVG`, `BEND`): graded from the YIN pitch curve (`opts.curve`). Verified on synthetic curves only: `node tests/bend_check.js` (verdicts and the numbers shown). Real-guitar tuning still needed: recorder takes `bends`, `vibrato`.
- **Call & Response L8–9**: blues phrases in time (`crBlues`, `BLUES_LICKS`), double stops graded as chords (`crDoubles`; recorder take `doubles`).
- **Musical checkups** (`CK_TASKS`, `ckStart/ckStop/ckSave`, IndexedDB `fretwise-checkups`): six recorded tasks every 28 days, first vs latest playback. Only the time task is measured (onsets vs beats: hits, mean, spread, drift; relative measures verified with synthetic players, the absolute offset depends on the real speaker→mic delay).


## Nov 2026 consolidation (audit item 7)

33 → 25 games. `MERGES` + `migrateMerge(tid)` (run once from `G()`, flag `g.mig.m25`) copy each merged game's items, level histories, rounds, speed log, keys and mix-ups into the new game with levels renumbered; what was unlocked stays unlocked (`g.unl` holds levels opened above `g.lvl`; the lobby, stats and unlock logic honour it). The old games' raw data stays in STORE (`mergedInto`). Verified on the user's real data: every answer accounted for, untouched games byte-identical, second load a no-op.
- Reading (`sight`) ← Treble Clef · Harmony & Analysis (`analysis`) ← Chords in Keys · Triad Shapes ← Inversions & Voicings (triad theory) · Drop-2 Grips ← Inversions & Voicings (seventh/drop-2 theory) + Name That Chord · Time & Groove (`rhythmtap`) ← Two and Four, Subdivision, Notation & Meter · Guitar Essentials removed (capo/barre dropped).
- New levels (all appended): Fretboard “Notes in time”, Reading “Read a line” ×2 (`staffLineSVG`, `readLineQ`), Note Hunt “In time” (`inTime`), Triads “Voice leading” (`tvlQ`), Hear the Degree “Hear it, play it” (`degPlayQ`), Hear the Progression “Write it down” and Analysis “Write the numerals” (token dictation: `ui:'spell'` with `q.tokens`, Berklee numerals), Intervals on the Neck “Double stops” (`dsQ`), Shells “through ii–V–I” (`shellSeqQ`, strummed sequence), Arpeggio Runner “Through ii–V–I” (`arpIIVIQ`), Walking Line “Walk it yourself” (`walkPlayQ`/`walkGrade`: rule-checked, not one answer). Intervals & Keys builds/spells by recall (`recall1`, `recallScale`). Chord Spelling shows the triad on the neck after the answer (`triNeckSVG`). Time & Groove option `opts.tapMic`: strum muted strings instead of tapping.
- Not built (need other work first): Repertoire → tune-learning workflow and voice leading through a whole tune (need the play-along mode); comping graded on chord + time; chord-scale “play it over the chord”; Hear the Chord with guitar audio (blocked by the piano-only sound rule); drop-2 melody-on-top (chord melody track).

## Nov 2026 pedagogical audit (ladders, path, fluency)
- **The path** (`PATH`, `stageOf`, `lvPassed`, `pathState`, `pathNext`, `vPath`, `lobbyPath`): five stages (Ground → Triads → Sevenths & function → Jazz harmony & voicings → Advanced); each step is a level range of one game. Home shows the current stage, three next steps (one per area where possible) and the full stage table; each lobby rung carries a stage tag and the lobby links the games of the same stage. Gates are soft: nothing is locked by the path. The sweep checks that every level of every game sits in exactly one step and that stages never go backwards within a game. **When you add or move levels, update `PATH`.**
- **Two pages** (user request): header tabs *Games* (`vHome`, the game cards only) and *Path* (`vPathPage` = stages panel `vPath` + skill tree `vTree`, view `path`). Keep progression UI off the games page and out of the lobbies.
- **Master standard per game** (`STD`, `STD_N=12`, `stdSeconds`, `stdText`, `lvStd`, `stdOf`): the bar a practising musician would call solid, well above the 85% unlock rule. Accuracy 85–95% over ≥12 recent answers per level, plus the game's speed measure: time as a share of the fluency target (fretboard 0.6 ≈ 1.8 s a note, spelling 0.6 ≈ 1.9 s a triad, triads/drop-2/shells 0.45–0.5 ≈ 4.3–4.8 s), ± ms from the click (Time & Groove 25 ms, Comping 35, arpeggios in time 50, Call & Response 60), or s/note (Note Hunt 1.2, arpeggios 1.0). A skill node is green (`sk-std`) only when every level meets its game's standard; numbers are coloured against the standard; the detail table prints each game's standard and ★ per level. The sweep checks every game has one.
- **Skill metrics, not mastered/not** (`lvMetrics`, `sumMetrics`, `metricBits`): each skill node and each level row shows clean % (recent window, grey under 5 answers), then the game's own speed measure — time vs the fluency target (− is faster), else ± ms from the click (tap/strum), else s/note (played), else average answer time for recall levels without timed data yet — and answers played; the bar is levels passed.
- **Skill tree** (`SKILLS`, `SK_TRACKS`, `skCol`, `skState`, `vTree`; button “Skill tree” in the path panel, view `tree`): the same levels as `PATH` drawn as 59 skills in four tracks (Time & groove, Soloing & the neck, Chords & comping, Harmony & ears) × the five stages + a Goals column. A node bundles level ranges (possibly from several games) of one stage; `pre` = skills it builds on (may cross tracks; cross-track lines show only for the selected skill). States: mastered / in progress / ready / locked (soft: still opens) / goal (four “coming” capstones that need features not built yet: play-along, chord-melody track, tune learning). No decay (the user chose no fading). The sweep checks coverage (every level in exactly one skill), node column = stage of its levels, earlier levels of a game sit in ancestor skills, no cycles. **When you add or move levels, update `PATH` and `SKILLS`.** `lvPassed`: passed = below `g.lvl`, or the level's own record meets the unlock rule (an out-of-order open level is not passed).
- **Fluency in mastery** (`FLU_GAMES`, `fluTarget`, `levelFlu`, `fluency`, `g.ht[L]`): in the recall games (spelling, intervals, analysis, chordscale, fretboard, sight, triads, drop2, shells, neckiv) a level unlocks at 85% *and* a median clean-answer time at or under target over ≥6 timed answers (letters 1.5 s + 1.0 s per note you enter — was 1 + 0.7 until 9 Nov 2026, when the user's fastest clean dim/aug triad was 1.33× the target: it measured the mouse, not the recall; `mig.fl2` rescaled the stored ratios of spelling and intervals by 0.686; numerals 1.5 s + 1.2 s each; name a note 3 s; click a spot 4 s; choices 4.8 s × factor). Played/tapped/heard/marked questions are untimed for fluency. Lobby meter, results panel and coach notes show it.
- **Ladder reorders** (`REORDERS` + `migrateReorder`, flag `g.mig.r26`, runs in `G()` after the m25 merge and legacy migrations): old level → new level per game, unlisted levels keep their number. Hist/ht/items/sessions/perf log are remapped; everything open stays open; an inserted level opens if a later level was already open; a moved level not yet reached stays locked (`g.unl` gap). Verified on the user's real data (copied Chrome profile): answer counts unchanged, untouched games byte-identical, second load a no-op.
  Reordered: Reading (lines with rhythm after each position: `readLineQ(win,strings,rhy)`, `staffRhySVG`), Triad Shapes (minor root position split out; mark-it in every recognition level; voice leading right after every root/every set), Intervals & Keys (major scales → signatures → intervals), Chord Spelling (name the triad before the fast level; extensions recalled), Drop-2 (paper and neck levels interleaved), Shells (comp II–V–I at L4), Hear the Degree (play it at L4, chromatic after dictation), Hear the Interval (6ths/7ths before the tritone), Hear the Progression (12-bar blues at L3, writing numerals from L1), Harmony & Analysis (sevenths + numerals one level; `anDiaWriteQ`, `anWriteQ(keys)` throughout), Sing It (minor key split from chromatic), Call & Response (blues phrases at L6), Bends (curl before pre-bend), Arpeggio Runner (one idea per level), Time & Groove (drop-out right after “4 only”), Comping (Freddie Green first).
- Leaks fixed: Chord Spelling L6/L7 formula pools; Chord–Scale quality question takes a pool (`G_cs2.quality(pool)`), characteristic notes only after spelling the modes. Hear the Progression shows Berklee names (`progName`; PROGS keys stay the stats ids). All prose numerals are Berklee (sweep fails on lower-case numerals). `bnum` spells ascending passing diminished chords ♯I°7 ♯II°7 (`DIM_UP`), `anChordOf` spells their roots with sharps.
- Still open: the 38 old Chords in Keys answers now inside Harmony & Analysis L1–2 were given against the old broken triad questions; the user has not decided whether to drop them.

## One input per game (user rule, Nov 2026)
If a game is answered on the guitar, every question is played or strummed into the mic; if it is answered on screen, nothing is recorded. Never mix.
`guitarMode(gid)` (mic games always; Time & Groove when `opts.tapMic`; `PLAY_GAMES` when `g.mode==='play'` — Hear the Degree is in `PLAY_GAMES` for this), `isGuitarQ`, `fitQ` (converts with `toPlay` in guitar mode, rejects what the mode cannot ask). `pickQ` only returns fitting questions; review and stretch draw only from levels the mode can ask (`levelAvail`, cached per mode). A frontier level the mode cannot ask is skipped by `modeSkip` (recorded in `g.skip`; never jumps an askable unpassed level; a skipped level counts as passed only on its own record). Lobby marks such levels “guitar only / screen only”. The sweep checks every level exists in at least one mode and that no question of the other input appears in either mode (proved by re-allowing mixing: caught in 9 games).

## Intervals on the Neck reference (Nov 2026)
`NIV_SHAPE` is computed by `nivShapeTxt(semi)` from standard tuning (span 5/10/15/19 half steps for 1–4 strings up, 4/9/14 when the span crosses G–B); the old hand-written table had wrong G–B and 13th shapes. `jazzIvPanel()` on the lobby (user asked for it much cleaner): one small fretboard (`ivMapSVG`, frets 0–7) with C on string 5 fret 3 and the major-scale degrees jazz chords use around it (`IVMAP`: 3 5 7 chord tones, 6 9 11 13 colours, octave; never the 2 or 3 on the root's own string), plus one chip per interval with its shape from the root. `DEGL` now labels tensions (♭9 … 13). The sweep proves every shape from every start string and every `IVMAP` dot (right note, not on the root string).

## Spelling with the root given (Nov 2026)
`q.given` (set by `recallQ`): notes placed in the first slots when the question starts, only if they equal the first answer notes (`spGiven()`); the matching key is highlighted, Undo/Backspace cannot remove them, and the fluency target counts only the notes you enter.

## Practice time (Nov 2026)
A round's practice time is `activeMs(wall,n)` = wall clock, at most 2 min per answer (1 min floor): a tab left open no longer counts its idle hours. Sessions keep `dur` (counted) and `wall` (raw, only when capped); `g.time` sums `dur`. Migration `g.mig.t26` capped the saved rounds once and took the excess off `g.time` (the user's total went from ~2,300 to 624 min; nine rounds had been left open, one for 18 h).

## Lobby level bars (Nov 2026)
Each rung shows `lvMeter`. Games answered both ways (`dualMode`: the Answer-by games and Time & Groove) get two rows, Screen and Guitar (the current input's label bold; an input the level has no questions for says so); single-input games one row. A row (`lvRow`, numbers from `lvModeStats`): accuracy bar (dark tick = unlock 85%, green tick = master standard, fill green when met over ≥12), the level's own measure from `lvKind(gid,L,gm)` (`time` vs the fluency target, `timing` ±ms from the click, `gp` s/note, none for ear levels), and answers played. One caption on the frontier level says what still blocks the unlock.
Speeds read in seconds: `lvTargetSec(gid,L)` is the level's typical unlock target (median over its timed questions); the row shows your typical clean-answer time (median ratio × that target), greyed until FLU_N answers are timed, no text under the bars (user request): the dark tick is unlock, the green tick master; the tooltip explains. Only the inputs a level has get a row. Timing averages only notes that landed on their click (`onTime`); a missed entry is an accuracy miss, and older log entries above 1 s are ignored. The bar fills as you get faster or tighter.
Guitar rows always have a speed measure (`lvKind`): `timing` for metronome questions, `rt` (time from listening to the first right note or strum, from `g.pl` `rt`) for one note or one strum, `gp` (s per note) for several notes; none only for Sing It and Bends (pitch in cents is the measure). Master guitar speeds: `GSTD` per game via `gStd(gid)` (default rt 3 s, gp 1.5 s; Fretboard rt 2 s, Note Hunt 1.2 s per spot, Drop-2 rt 5 s…).
Accuracy for a row comes from, in order: the per-input window `g.hm`; for guitar the last played answers in `g.pl`; your most recent rounds at that level and input (`g.sessions` carry L, mode, n, c); the stored questions split by `pn/pc`; the level's combined `g.hist`. Screen speed falls back to the average answer time (greyed) until clean answers are timed. Never show “—” for a level that has answers. Strum grading (`strumGrade`) records the matched strum's time in `per[].t`, so strummed grips log time to play it.
Per-input data: `g.hm[s|g][L]` recent window per input; `g.lc[L]={s,g}` answers per level and input, counted at the answer's own level, rebuilt once by `countBackfill` (flag `c27`). **Only answers really played count as guitar** (`it.pn`, `g.pl`); a round's mode is not evidence before `ONE_INPUT_T` (the one-input rule, 4 Nov 2026 18:00): earlier “playing it” rounds were mostly screen answers (Triad Shapes: 162 answers in such rounds, 2 really played). Screen answers are placed by round levels, a “playing it” round weighted by the share of such rounds that was not really played. `pl` entries carry `m` (1 = guitar). `fitQ/levelAvail/lvKind` take the input as a parameter. Audit: /tmp-style script in the session checked Σ(s+g) = g.n for every game, guitar = really played, and no NaN/undefined in any lobby (both inputs), stats, path or skill page.

## Castle music hall background (Nov 2026, dark mode only)
The user's own image `art/castle-hall.webp` (made with ChatGPT, 1536×1024; deployed by pages.yml copying `art/`, embedded as `window.ART_DATA` by build.py) drawn cover-fit on a fixed canvas `#castlebg` (z-index -1) when the system is in dark mode (`html.castle`; light mode keeps the plain page). Animated in image pixel coordinates (`CASTLE`: flame tips, glass panes, beam quad, piano box, all measured from the art): candles breathe (glow, floor pool, tip flicker), the beam swells and shimmers with drifting dust, glints sparkle on the piano's gold (read from the image), and a translucent pixel-cloud strip drifts very slowly, clipped to each pane. A soft dark band sits behind `#app` so text on the page reads (at 1440 wide the left candles fall behind it). ~30 fps, paused when the tab is hidden, still with prefers-reduced-motion. To change the art: replace the image and re-measure `CASTLE` (flames by warm-pixel clusters, panes by the mullions' pink rim light, then check on screenshots). Local testing needs the live layout (index.html beside art/), e.g. a temp dir with symlinks.

## Streak fire (Nov 2026)
`fireSync()` runs at the end of `render()`: in a round with `S.streak>=FIRE_ON` (5) the panel burns, growing to full at `FIRE_MAX` (20, blue). Rewritten for quality (user: “one even outlay”, not many little fires): every canvas cell outside `.stage` gets its distance d from the panel and its perimeter position s; one flame height h(s,t) runs round the whole perimeter (`fireWaves`: periodic sines with integer cycles so the outline closes, sharpened into tongues); a cell burns when d < h, its colour band from d/h through six shades (`FIRE_BANDS`, white-hot at the panel → deep red/blue at the tips) with 4×4 Bayer dithering at band edges. Streak level `FIRE.lv` (0 at 5 → 1 at 20) sets height, motion speed and canvas opacity (0.45 → 1); milestones (5, 10, 15, 20) swell and settle (`burst`); a few slow embers. The badge (`.firebadge`) is one crafted three-lick flame drawn the same way, with the streak number. The HUD stays on top (`body.onfire` outline); everything is removed when the streak breaks or the round ends; prefers-reduced-motion holds it still. The round timer is a pixel health bar (`HP_HEART`, `.hp-track`: black outline plus dark halo, segments, highlight, pointed end; drains by width; `data-hp` g/y/o/r).

## All levels open: a switch (15 Nov 2026, user: “unlock all levels, I will revert this so don't forget the current levels I am at”)
`STORE.openAll` (switched on once by `STORE.oa1`). `openAllSync(id,g)` at the end of `G()`: on → the game's own opened list is put aside once in `g.unl0` (`g.oa`) and `g.unl` becomes every level above `g.lvl`; off → `g.unl` is restored from `g.unl0` (minus what has really been reached since). **`g.lvl`, the records and `lvPassed` are never touched**, so the tree, the stats and the plan stay honest, and reverting loses nothing. The switch is on the Overview page (`openAllBox`, action `openall`). Checked on the user's data: on = 0 locked levels, 70 levels passed before and after; off = every game's level and opened list as before (Pulse & Feel keeps its own `ur3` unlock). **When the user asks to revert: they can press the button, or set `STORE.openAll=false` in a migration.**

## The timing trace (17 Nov 2026, user: “I am not enjoying the new histogram… a line similar to heart rate”) — replaces the see-through deviation bars
The bars of 16 Nov were hard to read (a good hit was invisible, red was hard to tell, drag and rush looked alike). Now one **solid line like a heart-rate monitor** on the staff: it runs green along the middle line and every hit is a spike with a dot at its tip: **up and blue when late (dragged), down and orange when early (rushed)**, more vivid the further off (`devCol(v)`); a hit inside the level's standard is green (`DEV_OK`), so success is seen. Full scale (`devFull()` ms, unchanged) is the **top line of the staff for drag and the bottom line for rush** (16 units either side of the middle line; it was 8). Drawn on the canvas in `grooveFx` (scrolling staff, one trace per hand in Two Hands) and as an SVG overlay `devTrace(pts,W)` (`.gtrace`, staff units) in `groovePage` and `chartPage`. Missed notes keep the red ring, extra hits the red tick. The user wrote “drag is blue and rush is blue”; orange for rush is my choice, to be confirmed.

## Deviation bars on the staff (16 Nov 2026, superseded by the timing trace above, the user's teacher)
In the continuous rhythm games the green/amber dots under the notes are gone. Each hit is a see-through bar on the staff at its note: **from the middle line up when late (dragged), down when early (rushed)**, reaching the 4th line above or the 2nd line below at full scale; green near the line, through amber to red at full height; nothing for a hit on the click. `devFull()` = full scale in ms (three times the level's standard, never more than the hit window: 75–90 ms), `devOf(off)` −1…1, `devCol`. Drawn in `grooveFx` (scrolling staff, canvas) and as `.gdev` elements in `groovePage` (the page that stands still; `.gmarks` now covers the whole system). A missed note keeps its red ring under the staff, an extra hit its red tick. It shows the raw offset from the click, so the player's natural lean is visible in it; the unlock still judges steadiness around the player's own centre. Same engine for all four games, so Jazz Rhythm and Comping have it too.

The switch on the Overview page stays (off).

Opening levels at the user's request goes through `g.unl` (open, not passed: stats, `g.lvl` and the Path stay honest), run once in `G()` with a flag. Done so far: Time & Groove all levels (`mig.ur1`, 5 Nov 2026); then, at the user's request, all five rhythm games reset to zero and relocked (`RZ1`, flag `mig.rz1`, 6 Nov 2026); then every Pulse & Feel level opened again (`mig.ur3`, 15 Nov 2026), old `rhythmtap/subdiv/twofour/notation` entries deleted, `opts.lat/bpm/tapMic` kept.

## The plan: a 4-week calendar (user request, 6 Nov 2026)
The Plan tab (`vPathPage` = `vPlan()` + `vTree()`) replaces the stages/next-steps panel, which the user found confusing (`vPath`/`pathNext` remain unused; `PATH` is still the stage map behind the skill tree and the sweep). `PLAN`: 28 sessions × 3 games with a theme per session (the 7-cycle: mixed, reading or neck, harmony or chords, ear, jazz, play, review or repertoire). No date tracking (the user asked for none): each game has a checkbox the user ticks, kept in `STORE.plan[i]=[b,b,b]` (`planTicks`, actions `plancheck`/`planreset`); the header shows ticked/84 and the next unfinished session. `SHORTN` gives display names that fit the cells. Sweep: 28 days, 3 distinct existing games each, every game at least once. The skill tree was simplified: a box shows name, levels passed and the recent clean %, everything else in the tooltip and the detail panel.

## The musician's review, built (6 Nov 2026)
Seven changes from a player's-chair inspection of every ladder.
