(1) **Repertoire → play the changes**: `repPlayQ(forms)` strums every chord of a form in order; any common voicing counts (`chordVoicings(r,q)`: shells on 6 and 5, drop-2 on three sets, triads on all sets; `strumGrade` accepts alternatives per step via `q.strumAltSeq`). Repertoire L7–8, guitar input. (2) **Chord–Scale → play the scale**: `csPlayQ(pool)` (hear the chord, play the fitting scale one octave in position; modes, melodic-minor modes, symmetric, bebop, harmonic minor from the 5th) on L3–6 as the guitar input. (3) **Drop-2 plays from L3** (`d2Find` in L3–5 and 7; naming as review) and ends with **Melody on top** (`d2TopQ`: the inversion that puts a given note on top; chord melody's first step). (4) **A slow click under played questions** (`opts.click`, `clickRun/clickStop`, not graded) in every play game and the untimed levels of mic games. (5) **Hear → play**: Hear the Chord L9 (`earChordPlayQ`), Hear the Interval L8 (`earIvPlayQ`), Hear the Progression L11 (`earProgPlayQ`, the bass line). (6) **Reorders** `REORDERS4` (flag `r29`): Fretboard octaves at L3 (9 levels), Reading on the guitar from L1 with melodic rhythms after their positions (16 levels), Triad voice leading at L8, Bends vibrato at L2, Drop-2 as above. (7) **Tops**: Call & Response L10 `crLongQ` (two bars over II–V with the click), Shells L8 `shellCycleQ` (twelve keys, one strum each), Sing It L9 `singPlayQ` (sing, then play). `PLAY_GAMES` now includes chordscale, repertoire, earchord, eariv, earprog (their paper levels are screen input, the new ones guitar input).

Found while building it: `genDrop2` had reversed its string order and returned nothing, so Drop-2 L12 (m7♭5, °7, 6, m6) and the minor II–V–I combos silently fell back to maj7 sheet chords. Fixed; `genDrop2` returns `{a,k}` grip objects.

## Scale Runner and Tension Grips (10 Nov 2026: the user's Leavitt routine and chord sheet; 34 games, 86 skills)
- **Scale Runner** (`scales`, neck, mic, 12 levels, life/core). **Positions rebuilt on Leavitt's system on 12 Nov 2026** (the user asked for Leavitt as the authority, not the seven-shape sheet they had supplied, which had wrong notes): a position is the first finger's fret, four fingers on four frets, a fifth fret by a first-finger stretch down or a fourth-finger stretch up, never a shift; twelve fingering types put every key in one position. `scPos(keyPc,P)` generates the fingering by rule: type = `SC_TYPE[key − (P−2)]` (in position II: 1 C, 1A F, 1B B♭, 1C E♭, 1D A♭ with first-finger stretches; 2 G and 3 D with none; 4 A, 4A E, 4B B, 4C F♯, 4D D♭ with fourth-finger stretches); the family's stretch fret is allowed and a pitch reachable without a stretch is played without. This is written from knowledge of the method, not copied from the book: the family rule and the position-II reference cases are certain, the note-by-note choice at each stretch follows from the rule. C major: `SC_BASIC` = positions II, VII, XII, V, IX (types 1, 2, 3, 4, 1A), `SC_ALL` = all twelve. `scPattern` as before. **Every question is played with the click** (`metro`, quarters by default; the user found the click missing on some levels). `scQ({keys,pos,cycle,pat,div})`: `cycle` = twelve keys round the circle in one position (II, V or VII), which replaces the user's “pairs”. `scModeQ` uses positions II–X. `scPosSVG` rings the stretched notes in red. Sweep: all 144 key × position fingerings are in the key, have no skipped step, keep to the family's frets, and the reference cases hold (C in II: F under a first-finger stretch on strings 6 and 1; G and D in II without stretches; A in II with a fourth-finger stretch on string 4; F in II with B♭ on string 5 fret 1).
- **Tension Grips** (`tgrips`, voicings, 8 levels, warm/core, in `PLAY_GAMES`): `TG[name]` = intervals `f`, grips `g` as C tabs from the sheet (pages 4–6), `how` (the one-finger change from a tier-1 shape), `use`. `tgQ(names,'name'|'find',roots)` (name: the diagram shows only R until answered; find: mark-on-neck with alternatives, strummed in guitar mode), `tgGrips` transposes, `tgBox` draws with interval labels. Levels: sus chords, daily dominants, tonic colours, altered dominants, colours, any key, then `harmSeqQ('shell6'|'shell5'|'d2root'|'d2sev')`: one shape family strummed up a whole key (the sheet's clusters). Sweep: every fretted note of every grip belongs to its chord and the root is present. Not covered from the sheet: rootless voicings, the turnaround in grips.
- Sweep fix: a single strummed chord in the main loop is now tested with strum activations (it was fed single notes).

## Metronome (10 Nov 2026, user request; third nav tab)
`vMetro`, view `metro`, settings in `STORE.metro` (`MET_DEF`, `metCfg`). Engine: `metStart/metStop/metSched` schedule every sound on the audio clock beat by beat (25 ms timer, 0.15 s horizon), so settings change on the next beat. `MET_METERS`: simple meters (beat = quarter), compound 6/8 9/8 12/8 (beat = dotted quarter, three eighths), 5/8 and 7/8 (beat = eighth, grouped accents). Per-beat accent / normal / silent (`cfg.acc`, presets all, 2 and 4, only 1, last beat), subdivision 1–4, **swing 50–75 %** (`metSw`: the off-beat eighth moves, sixteenths split each half), tap tempo, silent-bar trainer (`gapPlay/gapMute`), speed-up trainer (`rampStep/rampEvery/rampMax`). **Drums** (`MET_DRUMS`, `metDrum`): synthesised kick, snare, hats, ride, rim, bell — the user asked for a drum backing, so this is the one exception to the piano-only rule besides the click; styles: swing ride, jazz waltz, bossa (two-bar clave), straight eighths, shuffle, funk sixteenths, ballad, Afro 12/8 bell, 6/8. **Animation**: `metDraw` computes the pendulum (beat at the extremes, weight height follows the tempo), beat lights and playhead each frame from `metNow()` = the audio time now sounding (`getOutputTimestamp`), not from frames or timers. Keys: Space start/stop, T tap, arrows ±1 bpm. It stops when you leave the page. Measured: beats at 120 bpm exactly 0.5 s apart; 67 % swing off-beats at 0.335 of the beat; swing ride skip notes at 60 %; 12/8 bell on eighths 0 2 4 5 7 9 11; phase advance per frame constant to 0.4 % of a beat (/tmp/fwrun/mig/metro.py).

## The masters and the hall (10 Nov 2026, user design; snapshot before it: tag `v-2026-11-10-before-big-change`)
The Games page is a hall: the castle with six pixel masters in a circle and the player (Emir) in the middle. One master per section (`MASTERS[cat]`): rhythm Bongi (monkey on bongos), neck John, theory Albert (the butler, at the castle's piano; the user has not named him yet), voicings Joe, ear Jessica, applied Jimmi. Original characters inspired by the user's references; the art is the user's own (generated sheets in `art-src/<cat>.png`, 2×3 poses on green).
- **Sprites**: `.venv/bin/python tools/sprites.py` keys the green, crops one common box per character and writes `art/masters/<id>.png` = six 128 px frames in a row (`FRAMES`: idle cheer wince talk angry amazed); Albert's own piano is masked out. `sprHTML/sprFrame/artURL` (dist embeds them as `ART_DATA['masters/<id>']` via build.py). Drawn `image-rendering:pixelated`, a two-step bob.
- **Hall** (`vHub`, view `home` when `hubOn()`: not `view.list`, width ≥ 760): sprites are positioned in castle image pixels (`pos` = feet, `h` = height) through the same cover-fit transform as the background (`hubFit`, `hubLayout`; depth scales the size). Hover shows `masterTeaser`, a “!” marks `masterUrgent` (frontier under 60 % or a week away). The bar on top has the Play session button and the old home links; “Game list” opens the card list (`view.list`). **The castle and the dark theme are now always on** (`bgWanted` true, `data-theme=dark`).
- **Master page** (`vMaster`, view `master`): the master (256 px) with a speech bubble typed out (`talkType`, mouth frames alternate), and that section's game cards (`gameCard`).
- **What they say** always comes from `masterFacts(cat)`: the whole picture first (minutes this week, plan ticks, every section's accuracy, levels passed, days away) and then each game of the section (`gameFacts`: frontier accuracy, trend over the last 3 rounds vs the 3 before (only with ≥12 answers each side), speed vs target, weak items from `focusItems`, repeated mix-ups from `g.cf`, timing lean, on-guitar accuracy, the top two `coachNotes`). `masterTalk` writes it by rules in the master's voice (`VOICE[cat]`: at most two findings, then what to play next, then one line of craft), `masterMood` picks the pose.
- **AI talk (optional)**: with the user's Anthropic key in `localStorage['fretwise-ai-key']` (never in STORE or exports; entered under “Masters' voices”), `masterAI` sends the same facts to the Messages API straight from the page (raw `fetch`, `anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access`; model `AI.model` = claude-opus-5-5, effort low; `fallbacks:"default"` with a plain retry on 400) with `aiSystem(cat)` (persona + “use only these facts” + order of content + `MOOD:` first line). Cached per master per day and data signature (`fretwise-talks`). Any failure falls back to the rules talk and shows the reason. **Not yet tested against the live API** (no key was available in the session).
- **Ask the master** (`chatPanel` on every lobby, `chatOf/chatSend/chatSystem`, state `CHAT[gid]` for the session): a chat with the section's master as an AI agent. The system prompt carries the persona, the game's description and levels, its lesson card and full notes, the unlock rule and `gameFacts` (the player's numbers in that game); the last 12 messages are sent each turn; needs the API key (without one the panel points to “Masters' voices”). Tested with a stubbed `aiCall` only.
- **Companion** (`buddySync` after every render, `buddySay`, `buddyReact`): in play, groove and results the section's master stands in the left margin (192 px, 128 px in narrower windows, hidden under ~1220 px) and reacts with a pose and a short line from `MASTERS[cat].say`: right answers, streaks of 5/10/15/20, misses (angry on a repeated mix-up), unlocks, the end of a round; in continuous sessions every 4 clean bars, a broken hold, an unlock.

## The mission (9 Nov 2026): integration, not more components
The user's goals: improvise, hear and recreate tunes, read, listen educationally, play jazz tunes — by playing the games “mindlessly” (their reference: Zweig's *Chess Story*: one book of real material repeated until it is owned). Scrutiny: the app trained components (naming, shapes, single degrees, 2–8 note phrases) and had no layer that integrates them. Built so far (32 games, 80 skills):
- **Play a session straight through** (`RUN`, `runStart/runDone/runBar/runButton`, actions `runstart/runnext/runstop`): one button on Games and Plan starts the next unticked game of the plan session at the user's level, switching to the input the level needs (`swapInput`); finishing a round (≥6 answers) or a continuous session (≥4 blocks) ticks it and offers the next. No choices.
- **Tunes by Ear** (`tunes`, mic, 6 levels, life/core): `TUNES` = 24 traditional/public-domain melodies as scale-degree phrases (`5,` octave down, `1'` up, `:n` beats, `b3`/`#4`); `tuneNotes`, `tuneSlots` (a repeated note is one grader slot), `tunePhraseQ({mode,min,max,pair,anyKey,memory})`: phrase by ear → longer → minor/modal/blues → two phrases → any key → from memory (name + cadence only). Item keys are per tune and phrase (the same items across keys), so the corpus repeats. **No copyrighted melodies** (standards stay out; one riff blues was written for the app). Keep the notes exact; the sweep checks every phrase parses and fits the neck.
- **Improvise** (`improv`, mic, 8 levels, life/core): `soloQ(SOLO.x)` asks for the user's own line over changes with the click; `soloGrade` checks rules, not an answer (as `walkGrade`): chord tones only / scale tones with chord tones on strong slots / the 3rd or 7th on beat 1 (`guide`) / chromatic notes that move a half step into the next note (`chrom`) / never the same note twice / ≥3 different notes a bar / a landing note. `soloChord` (ct, gt, scale from `SOLO_SC`, `name` spells by scale degree), `soloLine` builds one valid example (the 3rd on beat 1, up the scale) used as `q.target`. The chords are heard first, then click only (no backing while the mic listens). Sweep: the example passes; one repeated note and the example a half step up fail.
- **Still missing for the mission** (in order): a play-along backing (headphones) so comping and soloing run over a whole form — the capstone goals `tg`/`sg`; a vocabulary game (short II–V cells in twelve keys, memorised); a listening game (form, feel, meter, where the II–V is, in generated full choruses); reading the tunes corpus from the staff; the user's own tunes (melody entry).

## Reading L7 “Two notes, first position” (14 Nov 2026, user: level 7 was too big a step; 18 levels)
Inserted before the first read-a-line level: two written notes played as two quarter notes on two clicks in a row. **The click runs and the player starts on any click** (`readLineQ(win,strings,null,null,{len:2,free:true})`: `o.len` fixes the number of notes, `o.free` makes it a free-start question at 60: `freeStart`, `grid` 0,1, no count-in). It was first built with no click at all; the user asked for the running click the same day. Both notes must be right and within a quarter of a beat of their clicks. `REORDERS9` (flag `r34`) shifts the old levels 7–17 up by one; a player whose frontier was the old level 7 keeps level 7 as the frontier with the old one open as 8 (the user's case). `PATH`/`SKILLS` ranges and `LEVEL_NOTES.sight` moved with it. Verified on the user's data: 1,066 answers before and after, second load a no-op.

## The goals plan (15 Nov 2026, user; snapshot before it: tag `v-2026-11-15-before-plan`) — replaces the 4-week calendar of games and `PLAN` v2
The user set four goals: **(A) solo over the changes, (B) learn a tune by ear, (C) chord melody, (D) comp a tune in time**, and asked that every plan bubble name its game and levels.
- **`GOALS`** (core): `A B C D` and `F` (foundations), each `{n, short, col, end, stages:[{n, why, steps:[[gameId, from, to]]}]}`, five stages each. A stage's steps are worked side by side; the stage is passed when every step is (`lvPassed` on every level), and the goal moves up. **Every level of every game is in exactly one step.** The orders: A chord tones → arpeggios in time and a position → guide tones and scale lines → chromatic language → whole forms. B the key → melodies back → bass lines and chords → whole tunes → the harmony of standards. C triads on top strings → voice leading and first drop-2 → the note on top → colour and width → harmonise a line. D time and shells → cadences and first rhythms → the blues in time → standards → the whole tune.
- **`PLAN`**: 28 sessions × 3 bubbles, each bubble a goal key (18 bubbles per goal, 12 foundations; two alternating weekly patterns). `planCell(i,j)` = that goal's current stage and one of its unfinished steps in turn (open ones first). `stepState` → done / now (level `L` to play: the next unpassed open level, or the game's frontier when the next one is not open yet) / wait (the game has not reached the range). `goalState(k)`. `stepText`. The session runner (`runStart`, `runBar`, `runButton`) plays the bubble's step at `L`. `planSub` (learn-once substitutes) is gone. Old ticks were cleared once (`STORE.planV` = 2): they meant other games.
- **The skill tree is the plan** (same day, user: “I meant this plan in terms of the skill tree. I don't want a new section”). The goal cards I first built were removed. `PATH`, `SK_TRACKS` and `SKILLS` are now **derived from `GOALS`**: a track is a goal (five rows: A B C D and Foundations), a column is a stage, a box is one step and reads the game and its levels (“Arpeggios · Levels 1–4 · 0/4 · now 1 · 81%”). `pre` = the box with the same game's levels just below (the drawn line); `gate` = the boxes of the stage before in the same goal (used for ready/later, not drawn). Each goal has its own box in the last column (stage n of 5, levels passed). The hand-written 90-skill table and the five named stages (Ground … Advanced) are gone; 106 boxes. The Plan page is the tree first, then “Sessions” (the 28-session calendar, bubbles coloured by goal). **To change the tree, edit `GOALS` only.**
- **Three capstone levels** the ladders end on (appended, no migration): Improvise L9 “A blues chorus” (`SOLO.chorus`, twelve bars), Repertoire L9 “Comp the form, in time” (`repCompQ`: a blues chorus or the first eight bars of a standard, every chord where the chart puts it, chord and beat checked), Drop-2 L19 “Chord melody through the cadence” (`chordMelQ`: a three-note tune over a cadence, each chord as the drop-2 grip with its melody note on top; any drop-2 grip of the chord with that exact top note passes).
- Sweep: 28 sessions of three distinct goals with the right counts; every level in exactly one step; ranges valid and rising within a goal; **no stage waits on a later stage** (the levels below a step's range sit in the same or an earlier stage number, in any goal). Checked on the user's data: stages and “now” levels come out sensibly, the runner starts session 1 at Pulse & Feel level 5.
- Not built: a “tune of the cycle”; soloing or comping over a backing; anything that checks real standards' melodies (copyright: goal B's last stage tells the user to lift eight bars of a real recording outside the app).
- **When levels are added, moved or removed, update `GOALS` and `LEVEL_NOTES`** (`PATH` and `SKILLS` follow from `GOALS`; older notes in this file that say to update `PATH` and `SKILLS` by hand are superseded).

## Fast runs: three more detector faults (14 Nov 2026, fix-list reports #54–66, Scale Runner eighths and triplets)
The user: “because I hit notes quickly it doesn't pick up some of the notes I play”. Raw dumps of the takes (/tmp-style script: model output before the gate and before `bpMono`) showed the model had heard nearly all of them; the app threw them away.
- **Absolute gate on real playing.** The user's first-string notes sit at −48 to −51 dBFS; `gateDb` −46 dropped them (B4 on both sides of the top of a run). `bpGate` now switches the absolute gate off when the take clearly has playing (90th-percentile frame ≥ `gateDb` + 8); `gateNear` 20 → 24. Silent takes (#30–36) are still not graded.
- **Amplitude floor.** `bpMono` kept notes with amplitude ≥ 0.5; in fast runs real notes read 0.45–0.5 (D3 and G3 in position IX). An asked-for note (`want`) now needs only `BPO.ampWant` 0.4.
- **Open string ringing.** An open-string pitch that was not asked for, quieter than the note still sounding, is folded into that note's `alts` (report #61, a low E that was never played).
- One fault is the model's own: in #64 it read a G3 as a weak second A3. That is a real miss; the game's one-missed-note allowance covers it.
- **Reports now store the click and the verdict** (`fixContext`: `mic.metro`, `grid`, `free`, `verdict` with offsets). **`tests/fix_takes.py`** takes report ids as arguments, replays a clicked take at its own tempo (stored, or inferred from the spacing of the heard notes), and judges a free-start take's time around its own median offset (the replay's click is not in phase with the recording). `tests/read_fixlist.py` fetches recordings one at a time (all at once closed the driver once the list grew).
- Result on the ten new Scale Runner takes: 3 → 9 right; #63 fails only on the wrong note the user reported in it.
- **The lowest notes heard an octave up** (reports #38, #40, #52, #57, #59: E2, F♯2, A♭2 on string 6 read as E3, F♯3, A♭3 every time): the laptop mic does not carry those fundamentals, so the model never reports them. `micGradeOne` now accepts the octave above for any exact-pitch target at or below `BPO.lowOct` (46, B♭2). Trade-off: playing the octave instead passes for those notes. **If the user gets an audio interface, remove this and the Note Hunt `lowOct` rule and retune.** Still open: Note Hunt #14, #29, #53 (its own `bpHuntPicks` path: a spot not counted, a top D5 read as D4), and the Fretboard in-time takes #39–41 and #52, whose replay does not match the question.
- **Positions III and X** (the user: C under the first finger on string 5, and the “Dorian position” with the first finger at fret 10 of string 6) were only asked from the triad level on. `SC_SEVEN` = the five basic positions plus III and X; Scale Runner levels 2–6 use it.

## Scale Runner L3 split (14 Nov 2026, user request; 13 levels)
“Eighths and triplets” became L3 “Eighth notes” and L4 “Triplets”. `REORDERS10` (flag `r35`) shifts the old levels 4–12 up by one; the old level 3's answers stay on 3. `PATH`/`SKILLS` ranges and `LEVEL_NOTES.scales` moved with it. Verified on the user's data (frontier stays 3, 253 answers before and after, second load a no-op).

## Scale Runner question as tags (14 Nov 2026, user: “I have to look hard to know if it's eighth notes or triplets”)
The prompt of `scQ` and `scModeQ` is `scTags(what, position, play, div, sub)`: four big tags — key or mode, **Position**, what to play, and the rhythm in its own colour (`.qtags .rhy.r1` grey quarters, `.r2` yellow eighths, `.r3` green triplets, with “n per click”) — and one small line (`.qsub`). The standing instructions (running click, start on any click, alternate picking, stretches not shifts, the pass rule, the colour code) moved to the level screen: `LOBBY_NOTES.scales`. Keep new Scale Runner questions in this form.

## Scale Runner pass rule (14 Nov 2026, user rule)
Scale Runner questions carry `tol:{miss:1,late:.15,lateMin:2}`. In `micGradeOne` a run then counts as clean with at most one note not heard and at most `max(2, ceil(15 % of the run))` notes off the beat (the user's numbers: 2 in 10, 3 in 20; off the beat = more than a quarter of a beat, as before); **any wrong note played fails it** (a wrong pitch in a slot, or an extra note that is not in the run). The old share rule (three notes in four on time) does not apply to these questions. (First built as one late note; the user loosened the timing the same day.) The result carries `missN`, `lateN`, `wrongN`; `micFeedbackExtra` says what was let through. Along the Neck is unchanged. Sweep: clean, one missing, the allowed number late, and both together pass; two missing, one late too many, a wrong pitch, an extra note fail. Replay of the reported takes unchanged (9/10; #50 has wrong notes).

## Level details (14 Nov 2026, user request)
Every rung in a lobby has a yellow **Details** button (`.lvdetb`, action `lvdet`, open level in `view.det`, body from `lvNotesHTML(gid,L)`): what a teacher would say about that one level. `LEVEL_NOTES[gameId][level-1]` (core half, 340 notes, about 22,000 words) has two forms, as the user asked: a level that starts a new kind of skill has all four parts — `k` key takeaways, `w` what to pay attention to, `s` what skill it builds, `t` theory and tips — and a level that takes the previous one a step further has only `a` (what this level adds) with `w`/`t` where there is something new. No filler, no repeating the level before. The first level of every game is a full note. **When levels are added, moved or removed, move their notes with them**: the sweep checks one note per level, the form, no empty parts, Berklee numerals only. The content was written from the real level list and each factual claim (frets, shapes, formulas, tune facts) checked; if a level's generators change, re-read its note.

## The voicings review, built (14 Nov 2026; snapshot before it: tag `v-2026-11-14-before-voicings`; 35 games, 90 skills)
A Berklee-style critique of Joe's four games: the grips were taught as a catalogue (named, placed, never moved), Drop-2 never played a progression, started on the wrong string set, was half naming, m7♭5 came at level 12, Shells had drop-3 wedged into the comping levels, Tension Grips ended with two levels that were not about tensions, nothing was in time. The user said build all of it.
- **Strums in time** (chord and beat graded together): a question with `strumSeq`, `metro` and `grid` (beats) gets a count-in and the click (`micListen`), listens to the end of the form (`LS.endAt`), and `strumGrade` gives every matched strum an `off` and `onTime` (within `STRUM_TOL` 0.3 beat); `timeOk` needs three in four; strums in the count-in are dropped. `cinRun(q)` is the count-in display for both kinds of clicked question.
- **Generators** (block “VOICINGS IN USE”): `cadQ({fam:'d2'|'shell',sets,kinds,click,hide})` over `CAD` (`maj` II–V–I, `min` IIm7♭5–V7–Im7, `turn` IMaj7–VI7–IIm7–V7): each grip the nearest to the last (`nearGrip`, `gripMove`), any grip of the chord in the same family passes (`strumAltSeq`; drop-2 from the user's sheet via `d2All`). `bluesShellQ(form)` twelve bars with the click. `shellTenQ` over `SH_TEN` (a shell and one tension as an interval stack on four strings, `stackGrips`: C9 x-3-2-3-3-x, G13 3-x-3-4-5-x, C13 x-3-x-3-5-5). `gtLineQ('maj'|'min'|'turn'|'cycle')`: the 3rd and 7th of every chord as two-note strums on one string pair. `rlQ` / `rlGrips` over `RL` (rootless three-note grips: 3–7–9, 7–3–13, 7–3–5, 3–6–9 on strings 4-3-2 and 5-4-3). `tenCadQ({rootless,click})` over `TEN_CAD` / `RL_CAD`. `d2NewQ(mode,qs)` takes a quality list. `.gripwrap` is now a flex row (it had no rule: diagrams stacked).
- **Ladders** (`REORDERS8`, flag `r33`; the frontier is capped where the player really was, so a merely open level does not pass the ones before it):
  - Shells (11): 1–4 as before · 5 minor cadence and m7♭5/°7 shells · 6 the cycle and up the key (old 8, plus the harmonised shells from Tension Grips) · 7 a shell and one tension · 8 blues chorus in time · 9–11 drop-3 (old 5–7). Skill `c16` “Shells at work” (5–8).
  - Voice Leading (8): 6 through the cadence · 7 turnaround · 8 round the cycle (appended, guitar).
  - Drop-2 (18): 1 paper (old 1+2) · 2 the top set in C (new: 4-3-2-1 first) · 3 set 2–5 · 4 inversions on the two upper sets · 5–6 · 7 II–V–I on the top set, strummed · 8 other keys · 9 melody on top (old 14) · 10 · 11 m7♭5 and the minor cadence · 12 a key in drop-2 (from Tension Grips) · 13–17 (old 9–13) · 18 cadences in time on every set.
  - Tension Grips (9): 1–6 as before · 7 II–V–I in colour · 8 rootless grips · 9 rootless II–V–I (old 7 and 8 answers sit on 7).
- Sweep: every sheet/generated drop-2 grip used is its chord; rootless grips have their three notes and no root; shell+tension stacks match their intervals and three reference grips; timed strums pass on the beat and with a strum in the count-in, fail half a beat late (notes right, time wrong) and with one wrong chord; the shown grip is among the accepted ones; guide-tone pairs are a 3rd and a 7th.
- Verified: migration on the user's data (answers kept, other games untouched, second load a no-op); in the offline build the strummed and timed questions start with the chord detector, `endAt` and the count-in; `tests/chord_live.py` all pass. **Not tested with a real guitar**: timed strums, two-note strums, rootless three-note strums. Strum checking still cannot hear notes below C3 (shell roots).
- Not done from the review: “from close” naming stays (user rule) but is asked less; no play-along backing; drop-3 stays inside Shells (moving answers between games needs a merge).

## A fair count-in (14 Nov 2026, user: “make it fair only”; fix-list #39, #41, Fretboard L9)
The user keeps the count-in (they want the reading pressure) and declined a tempo ladder and centre-relative timing. Three fixes, for every clicked mic question:
- **Never cut off early.** `startListening` ended a take when `LS.real ≥ LS.max` notes had been heard; a sound in the count-in or a ghost octave made that count early and the take stopped before the last note. With a click (`LS.beat` and `endAt` or `free`) the note count may only end the take once the last beat has passed (`endAt − 1.5 beats`), and a pause (`gap`) does not end a count-in take; `endAt` does.
- **The count-in is not graded.** `micGradeOne` drops notes that start more than half a beat before the first beat (`RN`) for `q.metro && !q.freeStart`.
- **The count is on screen.** `cinHTML` / `#cin` in `micPanel`: four boxes light with the clicks (`S.cin`, timers guarded by `S.cinTok`), then “play”.
Sweep: a stray note in the count-in does not fail a take, an extra note inside the take still does. Measured in Chrome: boxes at 0.5 / 1.5 / 2.5 / 3.5 s, “play” at 4.5 s, still listening at 8.5 s. The Scale Runner replay is unchanged (9/10). The recorded takes #39 and #41 cannot be re-graded: the recordings themselves end before the fourth note.

## Reading L3 “Ledger lines, above and below” (13 Nov 2026, user request; 17 levels)
Inserted after the whole staff: `trebleQ` over steps −7…−2 (low E to middle C, up to three ledger lines below) and 10…14 (A to high E, three above). `REORDERS7` (flag `r32`) shifts the old levels 3–16 up by one; `PATH`/`SKILLS` ranges moved with it. A player whose frontier was the old level 3 keeps level 3 as the frontier (the old one, now 4, stays open). **`migrateReorder` now also remaps `g.lc`, `g.hm` and the history points `g.rl`** (it missed them; `lc31` rebuilt Hear the Interval's counts, which `r31` had left unshifted). Verified on the user's data: 409 answers before and after, second load a no-op.

## Steady runs: gate and sympathetic strings (13 Nov 2026, fix-list reports #42–51, Scale Runner)
Ten Scale Runner takes were graded wrong; nine were detector faults, two causes.
- **The loudness gate ate real notes.** `bpGate`'s floor was the 20th percentile of the take's 50 ms frames: in a steady run there is hardly any silence, so the floor was the playing itself and “10 dB above the floor” dropped notes (measured: threshold −32 to −40 dBFS against a median frame of −31 to −37; a 33-note run kept 3 notes). Now the floor is the quietest 5 %, and a note within `BPO.gateNear` (20 dB) of the take's loud frames (90th percentile) is never dropped by the relative rule. The absolute −46 dBFS gate is unchanged (the silent takes #30–36 are still not graded).
- **An open string ringing in sympathy was reported instead of the played note** (A2 under A3, G3 under G4, B3 with C5, E4 with B4, a stray E2): `bpMono` takes the lowest strong note of a group and rescues a weak octave below. Three changes: `want` (the question's exact pitches, from `micListen` for exact-pitch questions, through `LS.want` to `bpAnalyse`) lets a strong asked-for note win over one not asked for and stops the octave rescue leaving an asked-for note (and widens it to `BPO.symp` when the octave below is the asked note, report #37); every read note carries `alts` (the other strong notes that started with it) and `micGradeOne` may match an asked-for note to an alt (cost 0.02, so a direct match is preferred; `fin` in `stopListening` passes `alts` on); a quieter note starting within `BPO.symp` (140 ms) of the previous one is folded into its `alts`, not counted as a new note. Known trade-off: an octave slip can now pass when the asked octave sounds strongly with it; Note Hunt is unaffected (its own `bpHuntPicks`).
- Results (`tests/fix_takes.py`, which now takes game ids as arguments, opens the lobby directly and replays free-start questions): Scale Runner takes 5/10 → 9/10 through the replay (all ten were wrong live); the other 41 reported takes unchanged at 35/41; `tests/live_takes.py bp` 4 → 3 errors. #50 still fails: 33 notes are heard now, three a half step off (B♭ for B twice, C♯ for D, F♯ for F), which reads as fretting, not detection. `tests/hunt/skip2.js` is an offline prototype with its own detector copy and stale paths: it does not exercise this code and was not run.
- Still open from the same list: Fretboard “in time” reports #39 and #41 (the count-in cut the take before the player started: the same complaint that made Scale Runner free-start), #38 (low A♭ on string 6 heard only as its octave), #40.

## Scale Runner starts when you do (12 Nov 2026, user: “it gives very little time… it doesn't dictate when I start”)
Scale Runner questions carry `freeStart:true`. `micListen` then runs a plain click with no accent (`clickRun(bpm,n,true)`, about a minute plus the run) and listens with no count-in and no per-question limit: up to 60 s for the first note (`wait`), then the length of the run from that note (`LS.free` sets `LS.endAt` when the first note arrives). `micGradeOne` moves the grid to the click the first matched note fell nearest (`base`), so every note is judged against the clicks from there: a constant lean still shows as an offset, a run that drifts fails on time. Sweep: the same run begun 7 or 11 clicks later passes with offsets measured from that click; a drifting run fails; Along the Neck is not free start (the user asked for Scale Runner only). Also fixed: a clicked line's listening cap now follows its length (the default 25 s cut long lines at slow tempos). Not tested with a real guitar.

## Along the Neck (12 Nov 2026, user request; 35 games, 89 skills)
The user wants to move freely over the neck; Scale Runner only ever plays in position. `along` (neck, mic, 12 levels, life/core, course 201; skills `a1`–`a3`; in `PLAN` three times) is the travelling half, decided with the user: one game, major first then modes and melodic minor, **only notes shown, no fingerings**.
- `alScale(k,kind,mode)` (`AL_SC` maj/mm; a mode = the parent major with a new home; `deg`, `nameOf` relative to the home), `alStr(sc,s)` (frets 0–`AL_HI` 15), `alAll`, `alSVG(spots,homePc)` (a neck from the lowest fret used, open strings drawn before the nut; red ring = `st`), `alM` (the common mic question: exact pitches with the click).
- Levels: 1–3 `alOneQ` (one string from the lowest note the string has, one octave: up, up and back, thirds, eighths, twelve keys) · 4 `alTwoQ` (three notes + three notes on two neighbouring strings, the group moved up a scale step three times) · 5–6 `alShiftQ` (`alBasic(rp)` = the key's five basic Leavitt positions; `alShift` plays one as far as a string, then the next position from the next scale note, the first note after the shift ringed) · 7–8 `alFullQ` (`alRoute`: lowest note of string 6 to fret 15 of string 1, four notes a string; `own` shows the whole map instead of a route) · 9 and 12 `alRoadQ` (free travel: a start and a target 10–14 scale steps away, sixteen eighths, land on beat 1 of bar 3; graded by `roadGrade` via `q.road`: in the scale, start and landing note exact, step or third, no repeated note) · 10 modes along a string · 11 melodic minor.
- **The mic hears pitch, not the string**: one-string and own-route levels are checked for notes, order and time only; the shift and full-range lines are wider than one position, so there the travelling is forced. Said to the user.
- Sweep: every full route and shift line (all keys, both scales) is in the scale with no skipped step and on the neck; a shift line is longer than one position; `alBasic` gives C = II V VII IX XII and only basic types; the road example passes and a flat line, a line a half step up, an octave leap, a short line and a wrong landing fail. Untested with a real guitar.

## Interval listening loop (12 Nov 2026, user request)
The Hear the Interval lesson card starts with a player (`ivPlayer`, state `IVP`, `ivpStart/ivpStep/ivpStop/ivpPaint`, actions `ivpiv/ivpdir/ivpgo`): one chip per interval (m2 … P8), Ascending / Descending / Together, Play/Stop. It plays the chosen interval every 3 s from a new random start note (both notes within A2–C6), shows the note names, repaints in place (a render would close the card) and stops when the lobby is left. Test: /tmp/fwrun/mig/ivp.py.

## Course numbers (12 Nov 2026, user request)
`COURSE[gid]` = 101 / 201 / 301, a university-year order inside each master's section (101 = where the section starts, 201 builds on those, 301 the top). Not a lock: the user decides what to play. Shown as the first badge (`badgeChips`, classes `b-c101/201/301`), `gamesIn` sorts by it, and the master page groups the cards under 101 / 201 / 301 headings. Sweep: every game has one and every section has a 101. Neck: 101 fretboard hunt sight scales, 201 triads neckiv, 301 guidetones arp. Theory: 101 intervals, 201 spelling analysis, 301 chordscale. Voicings: 101 shells, 201 voicelead drop2, 301 tgrips. Ear: 101 eardeg eariv sing tunes, 201 earchord earprog outer cr, 301 earmode. Applied: 101 bends, 201 walking improv, 301 repertoire. Rhythm: 101 pulse rhyread, 201 rhytheory comping, 301 jazzrhy. **A new game needs a `COURSE` entry.**

## Badges, required inputs, de-duplication (9 Nov 2026, user decisions after a system review)
- **Badges** (`BADGE[gid]=[kind,weight]`, `BADGE_TXT`, `badgeChips`, `gameLearned`): kind **life** (never retires: pulse, sight, rhyread, jazzrhy, comping, eardeg, earprog, outer, earmode, sing, cr, arp, repertoire), **warm** (drill to the standard, then check), **once** (knowledge: intervals, analysis — when every level is at the master standard the game shows “Learned ✓” and its plan cells show a substitute, `planSub`: the for-life game of that session with the weakest recent accuracy); weight **core / support / side** (support: hunt, neckiv, eariv, earchord, guidetones, voicelead; side: bends, walking). Shown on the home cards and the lobby header (with the one-line meaning). The sweep requires both axes for every game.
- **`PLAN` v2**: for-life/core games 3–4 sessions in 28, keep-warm 2–3, learn-once and side 1 (the old table had Bends ×3 and Hear the Progression ×2). The user's ticks (`STORE.plan`) were kept; cells changed meaning where the game changed.
- **Inputs are required, not skipped**: `modeSkip` is a no-op; a frontier level the current input cannot ask shows a banner in the lobby with a **Switch** button (`swapInput`: tap games toggle `opts.tapMic`, play games toggle `g.mode`). A game is only finished when both kinds of level are passed. (Before, `modeSkip` moved the frontier past such levels and recorded them in `g.skip`.)
- **De-duplicated levels** (`REORDERS5`, flag `r30`, answers moved to the nearest kept level): Guide Tones L5–6 (arpeggios in position = Arpeggio Runner L1–3), Hear the Progression L10 play the bass line (= Bass & Top L7) and L11 write it down (the dictation already sits in L1/2/4/13/14), Hear the Chord L10 tension on top (= Bass & Top L5); Hear the Degree L9 lost `earBassQ` (= Bass & Top L1) and is now “Long dictation”. Skill `c10` removed (75 skills); `PATH`/`h15` ranges narrowed. Generators `earProgPlayQ`, `earTopQ`, `earBassQ` are unused but kept. Not merged (user: de-duplicate levels, no mergers): Guide Tones vs Arpeggio Runner, Voice Leading vs Triads/Drop-2.
- **Hear the Interval L2 “Major 2nd or major 3rd”** (11 Nov 2026, user request): inserted after the anchors; `REORDERS6` (flag `r31`) shifts the old levels 2–10 up by one (11 levels now).
- **Ordered answer rows** (11 Nov 2026, user request): a choices question with `q.row` shows its choices as small boxes on one line in their natural order, with short labels from `q.chLab` (answers and stats keep the long names). Used by Hear the Interval (every interval of the level, m2 … P8, no more six-of-twelve subsets), Hear the Degree (single notes, 1 … 7), tensions, Bass & Top (top note; the chord-tone row is fixed: root 3 5 6 7 ♭9 9 11 ♯11 13), the mode colour note. Found on the way: `chordToneLab` returned interval names (P5, M3) for plain chord tones in Bass & Top L5; fixed.
- **Still inconsistent, noted for later**: master standard = unlock (85 %) in earprog, sing, cr, bends, comping; speed gates only on clicked recall; ladder lengths 5–16; Rhythm Theory mixes learn-once knowledge with for-life dictation (badged warm as a compromise); Applied category text is stale.
- **Also fixed in the review**: Chord Spelling L9's extensions generator threw (no `altExplain` branch), hidden because `pickQ` swallows throwing generators; the sweep now calls every generator directly.

## The teacher's list (8 Nov 2026): hearing in context
From the user's lesson: accents, modes by ear, progression conventions, outer voices, tensions as intervals. Built as two games and new levels (30 games, 76 skills; `PATH`, `SKILLS` h21–h27 + t5/h15 widened, `PLAN`, `STD`, `GSTD`, `SHORTN`, `LESSONS`, `PLAY_GAMES` all updated — the sweep enforces each).
- **Bass & Top** (`outer`, cat ear, 8 levels): `outerChords(k,name)` builds a progression from `PROGS` with a chosen top note per chord (`pickTop`: chord tone 70 %, a tension from `TOP_TENS` otherwise; `chordToneLab` = what the note is to its chord), `voiceTop` puts it on top (G4–F♯5) with the inner voices below, `playOuter` plays bass 0.8 / inner 0.42 / top 0.95 (the polarity is audible). `outerQ(kind,names)`: `bass`/`top`/`both` are `ui:'spell'` with degree tokens (`degLab`, `degSort`), `top1`/`ct` are choices after a cadence, `playbass`/`playtop` are mic (`M()`, octave-free). Pools `POP_PROGS`, `SEV_PROGS`, `MIN_PROGS`.
- **Hear the Mode** (`earmode`, 8 levels): `playMode` = drone (root and fifth, low) + the scale up + the colour note + home; `earModeQ(modes,'char')` asks the colour degree (`MODE_CHAR`, `MODE_WHY`); `earVampQ` two-chord vamps (`MODE_VAMPS`); `earBorrowQ('which'|'note')` one borrowed chord in a diatonic phrase (`BORROW`: IV→IVm ♭6, V→♭VII ♭7, VIm→♭VI ♭6, IIm→IIm7♭5 ♭6, IIIm→♭III ♭3); `modePlayQ` mic; `earMMModeQ` melodic-minor modes.
- **Hear the Progression L12–14**: `earMotionQ()` two diatonic chords → root motion (`MOTION_LAB`: 4th/5th, 3rd, step, tritone), `earMotionQ('phrase')` whole phrases by 4ths/3rds/steps (`MOTION_SETS`); L13 pop clichés (new `PROGS`: VIm–IV–I–V, I–♭VII–IV–I, I–VIm–IV–IIm, IV–V–IIIm–VIm royal road, Pachelbel, VIm–IIm–V–I, IIIm–VIm–IIm–V, I–IIm–IIIm–IV, I–♭VII–♭VI–♭VII; `PROG_NOTES` for each); L14 `earSongQ()` eight chords with a melody on top from two clichés (the user: examples from common progressions, not real songs).
- **Tensions as intervals**: Hear the Interval L9 `earTensionQ` (♭9 9 11 ♯11 ♭13 13 above a root: melodic then harmonic), L10 `earTensionPlayQ` (mic); Hear the Chord L10 `earTopQ` (maj7/7/m7 with a tension voiced on top).
- **Pulse accents (mic only)**: `startListening` in onsets mode records the level of every onset (`LS.onsLv`, dBFS); groove onsets carry `acc` (from the spec's `acc(bar,beat,frac)`) and `lv`; `accentDb(hits)` = mean strong − mean weak; `grooveMeets` needs `≥ GRV.accDb` (5 dB) on accent levels, shown in the caption and an “Accent · last 16” tile. Levels 12–14 (`accentQ` discrete placeholder with `mic:true`, so the level is guitar-only): accent on 1, backbeat 2 and 4, the and of 4. Without the mic the session shows a notice and does not start (`GR.noMic`). `mig.pu1` turned strumming on for Pulse once (the keyboard has no dynamics; teacher's point). Test: /tmp/fwrun/mig/groove3.py (+10 dB backbeat unlocks, flat dynamics does not).

## Groove: the continuous format (user design, 6 Nov 2026)
The four kinetic rhythm games (Pulse & Feel, Reading Rhythm, Jazz Rhythm, Comping) no longer use rounds. `start(gid,L)` routes any level with a `c` spec to `grooveStart`: one session, the click never stops (`GR.beats` grid for `GRV.max` = 180 s, per-beat tempo so `bpm2` can alternate tempos), expected hits generated 8 bars ahead from `c.bar(k,M)` (`grooveBars`), taps (`grooveHit`, latency-corrected) or mic onsets (`LS.ons` polled live) matched to the nearest expected hit within `tapTol` (`grooveMatch`; unmatched = extra, which breaks the hold). Dashboard: moving averages over the last 16 and 64 resolved hits (tightness = mean |offset|, lean = mean offset, misses), a sliding timeline canvas (`grooveDraw`), and the hold bar: `GRV.hold` (64) consecutive hits within the game's `STD.ab` (feel levels also need the 16-hit lean within ±15 ms of `c.feel(bar)`) unlock the level (`g.lvl++` in `grooveRecord`). Every 8 resolved hits write one entry to `g.hist[L]`/`g.hm`/`g.lc`/`g.pl` (an entry is 1 when all 8 were hit within tolerance with no extra hit: accuracy means hit rate; the 25 ms standard is judged by the timing numbers and the hold bar, not by accuracy — the first version scored blocks by the standard and showed 0% at 240/240 hits, wiped by `mig.gh1`); a keyboard-made click on the TAP button (`e.detail===0`) is ignored, Space already counted it; **The hold and the unlock** (6 Nov 2026 evening, user: 240/240 hits, tightness 24 ms, best hold 9 — “why can't I open the next level?”): the hold counts hits in a row without a miss or an extra hit (`GR.hold`, breaks on those only); the level unlocks when `grooveMeets()` holds: hold ≥ `GRV.hold` **and the tightness over the run ≤ `STD.ab` and over its last 16 hits too** (`end`: tight at the end, not coasting). **The hold is counted in clean bars since 9 Nov 2026** (user: Reading L1 has one or two attacks a bar, so 96 hits took ~50–90 bars): `GRV.hold` = 24 bars; a bar is clean when every written hit landed within tolerance, nothing extra fell in it and every length was right (`grooveLoop` completes bar k once its end + tol has passed; `grooveBreak(fromBar)` resets the run and `GR.holdFrom` on a miss, extra or length fault); `grooveMeets` measures tightness over the hits inside the run (`o.bar ≥ GR.holdFrom`) and needs at least `GRV.minHits` (16) of them; fire from 2 bars, full at 24, bursts every 4. (History: 64 hits → 96 hits → 24 bars.) (the moving average the user asked for; a per-hit bound equal to the average standard made a run of 64 statistically impossible — the first version did that). Tightness = spread around the window's own mean (`spread`, `adjOff`); the lean (`l16`, `pl.dr`) is shown, not judged — measured on the user's Mac, their taps land ~20 ms before the grid with *no* correction (−58 ms with it) while the output latency is right (`getOutputTimestamp` 39.7 ms vs reported 38; clock-mapping jitter ≤3 ms; handler delay 0.4 ms): the universal negative mean asynchrony of tapping to a click. Feel levels (`c.feel`): the “on” bars define the centre (`GR.base` = lean when leaving a `feel===0` phase); leaving an ahead/behind phase records `GR.met[feel]` = |l16 − base − feel| ≤ `GRV.lean`, and the unlock also needs every target in `GR.feelSet` met; `adjOff` subtracts the bar's asked placement so tightness is measured around the asked position. The hold bar caption (`#gholdc`) shows run/64, tightness over the run and the feel ticks; the lobby summary says why a held run did not unlock. `mig.gh2` removed the pre-19:00 blocks of 6 Nov (all scored 0 by the first rule) from `pl`, sessions, counts and `lc`. `GRV.max` is 300 s so a run started late is not cut off. **Fire**: `fireSync()` takes the streak from `GR.hold` in a continuous session (`GRV.fireOn` 8 → full/blue at `GRV.hold`, bursts every `GRV.mile` 16) and from `S.streak` in a round; `grooveStats` calls it every frame, `grooveEnd`'s render puts it out. Test: /tmp/fwrun/mig/groove2.py (steady −20 ms over 100 hits → unlock; ±30 ms jitter and a 3 ms/hit drift hold 64 but do not unlock; feel phases must each be placed). Why: measured on the user's Mac, their taps land ~20 ms before the grid with *no* correction (and −58 ms with it), while the output latency is right (`getOutputTimestamp` 39.7 ms vs reported 38; clock-mapping jitter ≤3 ms; handler delay 0.4 ms) — the universal negative mean asynchrony of tapping to a click, which an absolute 25 ms standard turns into a permanent failure. Test: /tmp/fwrun/mig/groove2.py pattern (feed hits with a constant lean → unlock; ±30 ms jitter → hold 0; feel phases relative to the centre). `grooveEnd` stores a session (`groove:1`) and shows a summary in the lobby (`view.gsum`). Spec builders (before `GAMES`): `cSub(k,…)`, `cCells(cells,swing,M,…)`, `swingBar/offBar/hemiBar`, `cComp(pick,…)` over `BLUES_F`, `tuneBar` (the user's tunes). Spec fields: `bar, click(bar,b), extra(bar,b), feel(bar), bpm(user), bpm2(bar,bpm0), meter, swing, ts, noTup, note`. The lobby hides round/time/mix options for these games; Space starts or taps, Escape ends. The old generators stay in `g` (the sweep still exercises them) but are not played. Sweep checks every spec for 24 bars. Dropped from the continuous versions: Reading Rhythm's 2/4 and 3/4 etude halves, Jazz Rhythm's 5/4 (one meter per session).

## Notation on paper (10 Nov 2026, user request)
Every piece of written music is a white sheet with black ink: `svg.rhy` and `svg.staff` scope `--fg/--muted/--surface/--line` to ink colours and carry the paper background (the green/red answer colours still show). `rhySVG` draws a five-line staff with a percussion clef and the notes on the middle line (the small cell pictures, `bare`, keep one line); a whole rest hangs from the line above the middle, a half rest sits on it.

## The page that stands still (11 Nov 2026, decided with the user)
A scrolling staff with a red line trains visual reaction, not reading: the line keeps your place, pulls the eyes to the present and gives the time. So **Reading Rhythm and Jazz Rhythm show a page** (`grooveView` → 'page'; Pulse and Comping keep the scroll; `opts.staff` overrides, lobby select “The music”): three lines of two bars (`GPAGE`, `gpageSys`, `groovePage`), read top to bottom and back to the top; a line turns over to new music once the first bar of the next line has been played, so the music ahead is always in view. Nothing moves. A faint marker on the current bar (never the beat) is on for the early levels and off from the syncopation levels up (`grooveMarker`: rhyread ≤ L7, jazzrhy ≤ L3; `opts.marker` on/off). Hit marks appear under the notes after the fact (`.gm`), the count-in above the page (`#gcount`). Test: /tmp/fwrun/mig/page.py logs the lines shown per bar.

## The scrolling staff (10 Nov 2026, user request)
In the continuous rhythm games the music is one long paper staff that scrolls under a fixed red line (`grooveStrip`, `grooveNowHTML`, `grooveScroll`; `RSTRIP` = geometry and scale 1.8): eight bars from the bar before the current one, drawn once by `rhySVG(…,{strip:scale})` (no clef, bar lines at every bar) and redrawn only when the window moves; the clef and time signature sit in a fixed panel at the left; each frame the strip is translated so the note under the line is the one due now (measured: the head is on the line to within the frame delay). Chord names (Comping) ride above their bars. **The play screen is only this** (user: minimal, “the whole console should be a scrolling clef”): a small header (level name, one line, tempo, clean bars), the staff (scale 2), a thin hold bar with its caption, the numbers as a row of pills, Start/TAP/End. The long instructions moved to the level page (`grooveHelp`, shown for the selected level). The separate timeline canvas is gone: `grooveFx` draws on the staff itself the count-in and, under each written note, where the hit landed (green on time, amber outside the standard, red ring missed, red tick extra).

## A clean start (11 Nov 2026, user: “the beginnings are still bugging”)
Measured in real Chrome with a simulated player landing exactly on the heard clicks (/tmp/fwrun/mig/first.py, startsync.py): the first click of a session sat about 30 ms off the others against the audio output, and click spacing wandered 496–506 ms at 120 bpm. Fixes: `grooveWake` keeps a −68 dB noise running from the moment a level opens so the output device is awake; `grooveGo` shows “Get ready…” and waits (`settle`) until the audio clock is advancing and the reported latency has not changed for 250 ms (1.5 s at most) before the count-in; the count-in clicks and every beat go through one scheduler (`grooveSched`, 350 ms ahead) on one clock mapping (`GR.syn`: the largest recent `currentTime − performance.now()`). After: spacing exactly 500 ms, first hit within 2 ms. The harness scripts groove2/3/4.py call `grooveGo()` and assume an immediate start: they need a wait for `GR.started` before feeding hits.

## Count-in (10 Nov 2026, user: “it bugs in the first two bars”)
Every continuous session starts with one empty bar of clicks (`GR.cin` = one bar at the first tempo; `grooveGo` schedules the ticks, `lead` = 600 ms + the bar): nothing is expected and hits are ignored (`grooveMatch` returns for t < −tol), the timeline shows the count. Besides giving the tempo, the bar lets the audio device report its real output latency before the first graded hit: without it the first hits were corrected with the pre-audio figure (about 6 ms instead of 38), which read as wrong for the first bars. Test: /tmp/fwrun/mig/cin.py.

## Held lengths in the reading games (9 Nov 2026, user: “hold the space bar for half notes”)
In Reading Rhythm and Jazz Rhythm on the keyboard (`GR.holdLen`: not Pulse, not when strumming — a muted strum has no length), every expected hit carries its written length (`o.dur`, from the bar's `ev`). A press on a note of **a dotted quarter or longer** (`GR_LEN.from` = 1.5; quarters stay press-only since 9 Nov 2026 — the user found holding quarters ergonomically poor, and the key-hold is only a proxy: the voice or a ringing, then damped, open string would carry length properly — not built, offered) becomes `GR.heldOn`; `grooveRelease` (Space keyup, pointerup on the TAP button, or the next press) judges it (`GR_LEN`: short if held < 60 % of the length, long if released more than 0.35 beat after the note ends; `grooveLoop` marks a key still down past that as long). A wrong length sets `o.len`, breaks the hold run and counts in the “Held wrong” tile; the accuracy windows stay hit-rate. The timeline draws each length as a bar (grey pending, green/red judged) with the held time above it. The TAP button now works on pointerdown/pointerup (the click handler ignores `gtap`). Test: /tmp/fwrun/mig/groove4.py (80 % holds → clean; a 30 % release → short; a key never released → long; Pulse and strummed sessions unaffected).

## Latency (6 Nov 2026): reported, never learned
Taps in the continuous games are corrected only by the audio device's reported output latency (`outLat()` = `AudioContext.outputLatency + baseLatency`, read when the click starts: the browser reports a smaller number before audio runs). Nothing is learned from the player's taps any more (`tapLearnLatency` is no longer called; it was circular and would erase a real lean). `opts.raw` applies no correction; the lobby shows the reported value and warns above 80 ms (Bluetooth). The mic path corrects onsets by `micLat(false)` = reported output latency + 40 ms (an estimate of capture + onset detection), re-read at every onset (the one-time read at `startListening` caught the pre-audio 6 ms figure in continuous sessions), so groove applies `GR.lat` to taps only. The 40 ms could be measured with a speaker→mic loopback on the Mic check page (not built). Measured 6 Nov 2026 on the user's Mac (built-in device, 44.1 kHz): baseLatency 5.8 ms, outputLatency 32 ms, `currentTime` steps of 2.9 ms, `getOutputTimestamp` agrees with the reported figure to 2 ms. The player's constant lean is therefore not a latency error; see the groove section (steadiness around the centre). The user had switched `opts.raw` on; leave it to them. Scheduled ticks are tracked in `TICKS`; `Piano.tickStop()` silences the ones still to come (`grooveEnd`, `tapAbort`): the click no longer runs on after a session ends.

## Kinetic and paper never mix (user rule, 6 Nov 2026)
A rhythm game is either kinetic (every question `ui:'tap'`, tapped or strummed) or on paper (choices/spell), never both. Pulse & Feel, Reading Rhythm, Jazz Rhythm and Comping are tap-only; **Rhythm Theory & Dictation** (`rhytheory`, 9 levels, screen only, in `FLU_GAMES`) holds note values, meter, “which count”, swing vocabulary and the dictation levels (`rhyDictQ`). The sweep fails any `tap:true` game that produces a non-tap question (proved by injecting one). `REORDERS3` (flag `r28`) moved the old Reading Rhythm 6/11/12 and Jazz Rhythm 10 answers onto the nearest kept level.

## Rhythm rebuild (Nov 2026): four games on Leavitt's order
Time & Groove and the old Comping were replaced by **Pulse & Feel** (`pulse`, 11), **Reading Rhythm** (`rhyread`, 14, *A Modern Method* Vol. 1 → 2 order of figures), **Jazz Rhythm** (`jazzrhy`, 11, *Melodic Rhythms* territory: Charleston family, pushes, dotted chains, quarter-note triplets, phrase placement, hemiola, bebop cells, 3/4 and 5/4, swing dictation, etudes, the user's own transcriptions) and **Comping** (`comping`, 8, mic). Reading got L17–18 *Melodic rhythms* (`readLineQ(win,strings,cells,swing)`: pitch + rhythm from the same cells). Migration: `MERGES.pulse/rhyread` from `rhythmtap` (latency `opts.lat` carried), `REORDERS2.comping` (flag `r27`), `mig.ur2` opens every Pulse and Reading Rhythm level (the user had asked for Time & Groove open).
- **Cell grammar** `RCELL` (b = beats, `ev` notation events, `tup` tuplet group; whole-bar jazz figures too) → `rhyBar(cells,bars,swing,M)` / `rhyBarCells` (keeps the cell sequence for dictation); cells never cross a bar line; `M` = beats per bar. `rhySVG(ev,bars,{meter,ts,noTup,swing})` draws any meter, groups tuplets by id, hides the 3 for 12/8; `rhyLines` stacks two bars a line for etudes. `beatName(t,M)` — never `.map(beatName)` (the index would become the meter).
- **Question kinds**: `tapReadQ(cells,bars,swing,{meter,ts,noTup,bpm,prompt})` (8 bars = etude), `tapBackQ`, `rhyDictQ(cells,swing,M)` → **`ui:'rhywrite'`, the rhythm writer** (7 Nov 2026, user: picking cells is multiple choice, “ask me to create the sheet music”): the player writes the bar value by value (`RW_VAL` w h q e s; dot, rest, tie one-shot; triplet stays on until the group adds up to whole beats; keys w h q e s . r t _ Backspace Enter — the branch sits before the global `h` hint shortcut), `S.rw` state, `rwAdd/rwUndo/rwFull`, the bar drawn live with `rhySVG`, Check when full. `q.answer` is the event list; `rhyWriteGrade` compares the **sound** (`rwSound`: note starts, and rest runs; a tie and a longer value are the same, a rest and a held note are not; tolerance 1/12 beat) and says why; `rhyText` is the compact text (`q. e e3 re`) for stats and mix-ups. Playback sounds every note for its written length so rests are audible. Sweep: the answer passes, a whole-note bar and a swapped pair fail. (`RLAB`/`tokHTML`/`cellSVG` remain for `tokMusic` spell questions: cells drawn, never typed — the Musical Symbols block draws as boxes on the user's Mac.) `rhyReadQ(cells)` counting, `feelQ(ms)` (grader checks the **mean** offset against a target: `q.feel`, ±25 ms; latency learning skips these), `subdivQ('switch')`, `twoFourQ('drop4'|'drop8'|'slow')`, comping: `compQ(names,bars,{clicks,bpm})`, `compPairQ` (two-bar bossa), `compCallQ` (hear, strum back), `compChorusQ` (a form over a piano bass line `q.track`, headphones), `compTradeQ` (four in, four out; extra attacks in the rest are misses). `COMP` has bossa/bossa2/samba/ballad/ballad2/push3.
- **Flow mode**: round length 0 on tap games → `S.round=Infinity`, `S.flow={hist,msg}`; each tap question starts by itself (`nextQ`), the next comes 2.8 s after a miss too, and `flowAdapt` moves `g.opts.bpm` (+4 after three clean answers within 35 ms, −4 after two misses, 50–240). HUD shows “n · Flow”. `G()` no longer resets `round` 0.
- **Your tunes**: `STORE.tunes[]` `{n,rhy,swing,ok}`, `parseRhy(text)` (w h q e s, `.`, `r`/`rq` rests, `_` ties, `|` bars, leading `3/4`/`5/4`; bars must add up), `tunesBox()` in the Jazz Rhythm lobby, `tuneQ()` asks them at L12 (an etude until one exists).
- Standards: `STD.pulse/rhyread/jazzrhy/comping`; path stages and skills `t1 t2 t3 t5 r1 r2 r3 y1 y2 y3 j1 j2 j3 k1 k2 k3 s17 tg`; sweep: tap self-grading adds `q.feel`, music glyphs need the `u` flag in regexes.

## Layout

```
src/fretwise.html   the app: <title>, <style>, markup, one <script>. Edit this.
piano/*.mp3         21 Salamander grand samples (MIT, tonejs-instruments), minor thirds C2–C7, 4.5 s, 96 kbps
build.py            src + piano → dist/fretwise.html (single offline file)
tests/extract.js    pulls the <script> out of src into tests/fw.js
tests/sweep.js      generator sweep + self-grading checks for every game/level (node)
tests/screens.py    Playwright smoke run with fake mic, screenshots to tests/out/
vendor/             bp.js (Basic Pitch + tfjs bundle), model/, LICENSE — built by tools/build-vendor.js, committed, deployed with the site
tools/              npm project for the vendor bundle (npm install; node tools/build-vendor.js); node_modules git-ignored
recordings/         the user's guitar test takes (git-ignored): Mic check → Record test takes → ~/Downloads/fretwise-take-<id>.wav
tests/replay.js     offline: node tests/replay.js [live|basicpitch|bpmono] scores a detector on recordings/ against TAKES
tests/tune_bp.js    grid search of Basic Pitch thresholds + guitar filter on the recordings
tests/live_takes.py real Chrome window (GPU), feeds each recording into the app's live listening; compares old vs Basic Pitch
```

Python tools run from `.venv/` (git-ignored; `python3 -m venv .venv && .venv/bin/pip install playwright numpy`; Playwright uses the installed Chrome via `channel='chrome'`).

Serve for development: `python3 -m http.server 8765` from `src/`'s parent with `piano/` reachable (tests/screens.py does this for you), or just open `dist/fretwise.html`.

## Verify before publishing

```
node tests/extract.js && node --check tests/fw.js && node tests/sweep.js   # must print bad 0
python3 tests/screens.py                                                   # look at tests/out/*.png
.venv/bin/python tests/pages_check.py                                      # every page, lobby and stats page rendered on the user's real data: must print all ok
python3 build.py                                                           # refresh dist/ (embeds vendor/ too)
.venv/bin/python tests/live_takes.py bp                                    # mic changes: live detection on the user's recordings
git commit -am '…' && git push                                             # deploys the live site
```

Node may not be installed: run the same scripts with macOS's `jsc` (`/System/Library/Frameworks/JavaScriptCore.framework/Versions/Current/Helpers/jsc`) by shimming `require('fs'/'path')`, `console.log` → `print`.

The sweep instantiates ~38,000 questions and checks: choice lists contain the answer with no duplicates; multi/fret/tap/mic questions pass their own grader with a perfect answer and fail a wrong one; `snd` descriptors only emit sane midis; every question has key/label/prompt/explain; play-mode conversion yields ascending exact-pitch targets.

## Architecture (all in one script, top to bottom)

1. **Music core** — `P()` parse note name → `{l,a}` (letter index, accidental), `pc`, `nm`, `tr(root,num,semi)` spell an interval up, `trDown`, `IV` interval table, `CH` chord formulas, `chord()`, `scale()`, `SC`, `diatonic`, `respell`, `mc(ans,distractors,n)` builds a shuffled choice list, `C()`/`A()` question wrappers (choices / audio), `okAcc` (limit double accidentals), `rootPick(list)` (twelve-key coverage: prefers roots this game has seen least; sets `LAST_ROOT` which pickQ stores as `q.rootKey`).
2. **Drawing** — `fretSVG({dots,click,win})` (string 1 on top, dots `{s,f,cls,t}`), `staffSVG` (accidentals drawn as paths, not glyphs), `boxSVG` chord diagram, `rhySVG` rhythm notation, `tapTimeline`, `circleSVG`.
3. **Piano** — `Piano` sampler: `load/note(midi,t,dur,vel)/chord/tick/ready/now`; source is `window.PIANO_DATA[name]` when present (dist) else `piano/<name>.mp3`.
4. **Sound engine** — `playSnd(q,g)` plays a question's `snd` descriptor after answering (`note | seq | scale | chord | grip | prog | chordseq`); every generator attaches one. Ear games use `q.audio()` instead (returns duration in seconds; timer starts after it).
5. **Mic** — guitar games (everything but Sing It and Comping) use **Basic Pitch** (`BP`, `bpLoad`, `bpAnalyse`, `bpMono`, settings `BPO` tuned on the recordings): audio captured on the audio thread (`BP_CAP` AudioWorklet, loaded from a data: URL because blob: fails on file://; capturing on the main thread drops chunks while the model runs and the gaps read as new picks), the last 6 s re-analysed every 0.4 s, the whole take once more on stop. Falls back to the built-in detector without WebGL. Built-in detector: YIN pitch (`yinPitch`), `segment()` (attack + pitch-change segmentation, octave-shadow filter, `mf` float midi for cents), `startListening(onDone,maxNotes,{beat,beatStart,limit,onsets})` (onsets mode records attacks only, for strummed comping), `micGrade` (strict/octave-free, held-note merging, swing `grid`, alternative `targets`), `micPanel/slotsTarget/slotsHeard`, tuner view.
6. **Tap engine** — `tapBegin/tapHit/tapGrade` against an audio-clock-scheduled click; latency learned per game from clean rounds (`g.opts.lat`).
7. **Generators** — one closure per question type, returning a question object (schema below). Grouped by game; the drop-2 sheet `D2C`/`D2PROG`/`COMBO` is the user's own voicing sheet, keep its grips as they are. `genDrop2`, `drop3Grips`, `shellGrip`, `triadGrips`, `spreadGrips` generate grips and verify them against chord tones.
8. **Play mode** — `toPlay(q)` converts a `multi`/`fret` question into a strict `mic` question (exact pitches, ascending, alternatives preserved) when `g.mode==='play'`; `PLAY_GAMES` lists the games that offer it.
9. **GAMES / CATS** — each game `{id,cat,name,area,desc,levels:[{n,d,g:[generators]}], mic?, tap?}`; level count varies (5–16). **Level numbers index saved histories**: append new levels; any insert or reorder needs an entry in `REORDERS` (new flag if it is a second reorder of the same game) and a `PATH` update. Chord Spelling was rebuilt on 2 Nov 2026 (recall levels: majors by heart around the cycle of fourths, then alterations; `recallQ`, `altExplain`) and its saved data reset once at the user's request (`v2` in `G()`). A reorder needs a migration in `G()` (see `v7` for fretboard, `v67` for Note Hunt levels 6↔7, 2 Nov 2026). A level with `open:true` is playable before it is reached (Note Hunt L6 “Fret by fret”); review draws only from unlocked levels, stretch only from your own level.
10. **Store / sync** — `STORE.games[id]` = `blank()` fields (`lvl,last,limit,review,preview,round,sound,mode,opts,rk,sessions,items,cf,hist,best,n,c,ms,time,pn,pc`, plus `pl,sp` from the stats log); `K(key)` hashes item keys; `saveGame` → localStorage + artifact db; `initCloud` waits for the runtime, retries, surfaces errors (`CLOUD_MSG`), merge rule "more answers wins".
11. **Engine** — `pickQ` (review 25% / preview 15% mix, weakest-item weighting, `rare` generators), `start/nextQ/answer/finish/quit`, `check/givenText` per `ui`, level unlock at `NEED=16` recent answers ≥ `PASS=.85` clean.
12. **Stats analysis** — `perfLog` (in `answer`) records per answer: `it.r` last 8 results, `it.rt/rn` time to first played note, `it.gp/gn` note-to-note find time, `it.qn/qc` mic answers since the Basic Pitch detector, `g.pl` log (≤300) `{t,lv,k,ok,rt,gp,n,dr,ab}` (dr/ab = signed/abs offset to the click, ms), `g.sp` find time per slot label (strings in Note Hunt, chord tones in arpeggios). `coachNotes` writes the teacher's notes (prioritised, max 5), `focusItems` the practice list. Analysis skips pre-detector guitar mic results (`trusted`, `OLDMIC`), caps rounds left open (`roundCap`).
12a. **Statistics page (rebuilt 12 Nov 2026)** — `vStats` shows what each kind of game is about over the last 60 days. Daily history `g.days[YYYY-MM-DD]` = `{n,c,ms,dur,tn,dr,ab,gn,gp,rn,rt,hold}` (`dayAdd` from `answer`, `finish`, the groove blocks and `grooveEnd`; `dayPrune` keeps 60 days; `dayBackfill`, flag `d60`, rebuilt it once from the stored rounds and the performance log). Kinds: **rhythm** (clean blocks, tightness and lean per day, longest run of clean bars), **played** (accuracy, seconds per note or time to the first note, timing where there is a click), **ear** (accuracy, and `confMatrix`: what was played against what was answered, for intervals, degrees, chord qualities, modes), **recall** (accuracy, answer time against the level's unlock speed). `dayChart` draws one value per day (line or bars, goal line); tiles compare the last 7 days with the 7 before. Own panels: neck heat map (Fretboard, Reading), note table (Note Hunt), by key, `groupBars` by tune (Tunes), by fingering type and pattern (Scale Runner), by chord type (grip and spelling games), where you hesitate (`g.sp`). Then at most three coach notes, the practise-next list, repeated mistakes, and the level table (answers, recent accuracy and the level's measure from `lvMetrics`/`metricBits`, passed / at standard). Removed as uninformative: best streak, score, the recent-rounds table.
    **History by points, not days (12 Nov 2026, user: no day axis, fine-grained, everything, no empty space)**: the charts draw `g.rl`, one point per round (continuous rhythm games: one per 4 blocks = 32 hits, the session's best hold on its last point), fields as a day record plus `t`, `L`; `rlAdd` keeps `RL_MAX` = 1500 points; `rlBackfill` (flag `rl1`) rebuilt it once from the stored rounds and the performance log (only what the old caps had kept: 80 rounds, 300 log entries). `ptChart(P,val,opts)` spreads the points edge to edge, oldest left: thin line each point, thick line running average, faint uprights where the date changes, the goal named in the caption. `rlOlder` (flag `rl2`) added the continuous games' sessions older than the first point as one point each (clean blocks only; their timing was already gone from the 300-block log). `g.days` is kept (`DAYS_KEEP` 400) for the 7-day tiles and the one bar chart of minutes per practice day (only days practised). `statDays`/`dayChart` are unused.
    **Dual games are split by input (12 Nov 2026, user request)**: every point carries `m` ('g' guitar / 's' screen or keyboard; `rlTag`, flag `rl3`, tagged the stored points from their round's or session's mode, pre-`ONE_INPUT_T` “playing it” rounds only when most answers were really played, and gave each continuous session's time to its last point). For `dualMode` games `vStats` shows two tabs (`view.inp`, action `statinp`, default = the current input) and everything is that input only: points filtered by `m`, tiles from `daysFrom(P)`, the stored questions split by `pn/pc` (`gi`), the level table as `lvRow` for that input (or “screen only / guitar only”). Coach notes and mix-ups still cover both (said on the page). Test: /tmp/fwrun/mig/dual.py renders both inputs of every dual game.
12b. **Lesson cards** — `LESSONS[gameId] = {how, rules, take}` rendered by `lessonCard` at the top of each level page (open until the game has answers). Every game needs one; keep the rules factually exact (they are checked against the neck). **Full notes** (9 Nov 2026, user request): `LESSON_NOTES[gameId] = [{h, p:[paragraph | [bullets]]}, …]` (core half, before the VIEWS marker) rendered under the take-away as a collapsed `<details class="lcnotes">` — the summary card stays on top. The sweep requires notes for every game (≥3 sections), Berklee numerals only, no code leaks. Keep them factually exact; they are the reference text of the app.
13. **Views** — `vHome, vPathPage (vPath + vTree), vLobby, vPlay, vResults, vStats, vOverview, vReference, vTuner, vRec`; one delegated click handler keyed on `data-act`, plus `change`, `pointerdown` (tap) and `keydown` handlers.

## Question object

```
key        stable id for stats (same item across modes); label  short text for stats tables
ui         'choices' | 'letters' | 'pc' | 'fret' | 'multi' | 'mic' | 'tap' | 'spell'
answer     choices: string · letters: 'F♯' · spell: ['D','F♯','A'] (need = count; entered note by note, S.spell) · pc: 0-11 · fret: validated by valid(s,f) · multi: [{s,f}] (+ alts: [[{s,f}]]) · mic: target midis · tap: onsets in beats
prompt, explain, hint, answerText, visual (html), reveal (html or {dots}), staff/staffOk, win [lo,hi], mark, fixedDots
factor     time multiplier (Infinity = untimed); rare: true = only when drawn first
snd        answer sound descriptor; audio() for ear questions (returns seconds)
mic:       target, targets (alts), names, degs, octaveFree, maxNotes, metro (bpm), grid (beat positions), hideTarget, after()
tap:       bpm, len (beats), countIn, clicks (beats in bar that click), onsets, call (onsets played first), mic (grade from onsets), dropOut
```

## Conventions the user has asked for

- Scale degrees as numbers; Roman numerals only in Berklee form (IIm7, V7, IMaj7 — always upper case, quality in the symbol).
- Drop-2 naming "from close" only (never "by bass note" in questions).
- Find-the-grip questions are mark-on-neck (or play it), never tab multiple choice.
- **Sound: no piano-only rule any more (16 Nov 2026, user: “It wasn't supposed to be a rule like this. Use whatever instrument serves the educational point”).** Any instrument or sound may be used where it teaches better (bass, drums, guitar samples). Older notes in this file that cite “the piano-only sound rule” as a blocker (Hear the Chord with guitar audio, drums only on the Metronome page) are superseded. Check the licence of any new samples before vendoring them.
- Keep all stats; never reset or restructure `STORE` without a migration (see the fretboard `v7` level-shift migration in `G()`).

## Not built yet (from the review)

- Spaced review across days (per-item due dates); the data for it is already in `g.items` (`n,c,w,h,ms,lv`).
- Daily session builder (a 20-minute mix across areas based on weakest items; `weekSummary()` already computes candidates).
- Mobile pass: scrolling/zooming neck, larger answer targets, single-row hud.
- Polyphonic chord detection (strums are graded by onsets only; chords are arpeggiated in play mode).

## The review of the theory games: build 1 (23 Nov 2026, brief `.claude/theory-build-1.md`, reviews `.claude/audit/{intervals,spelling,analysis,chordscale}.md`; working tree only, not committed)
Right answers that were marked wrong, one chord-scale table, every spelling askable, functional numerals. **No level added, removed or renumbered; no existing item key changed; no saved data touched.** New item keys beside the old ones: `ckiv-<key>`, `ckhold-<root>` (Harmony & Analysis 6), `cs-kr-<key>-<degree>` (Chord–Scale 1), `cs-mif-<function>` (Chord–Scale 8), `cs-sy-9♯5 / 7♯11 / 7♭9♭13` (Chord–Scale 5), `spc-<symbol>` (Chord Spelling 9, multiple choice), `sp-D♯dim7`, `sp-G♯dim7` and the triads and °7 chords with one double accidental.
### 1. Answers and prompts
- **Harmony & Analysis 16**: a device has a name as a choice (`DEVICES[].c`) where its plain name is also true of another pattern: “modal interchange with no dominant chord” against “backdoor dominant”. The numeral-string question (`anProgNumQ`) offers only strings of the answer's length (the other patterns of that length, then the answer with one numeral changed, `PN_NEAR`); its prompt says “Which numerals are these?” (it never was written). **15**: “Constant structure: one chord type moved, with no dominant resolution between the chords”. **6**: `keyIIVQ` (“Dm7 – G7: the II – V of which major key?”) and `keysHoldQ` (“Which three major keys hold Dm7?”, a token question answered in any order: `q.anyOrder`, `q.tokHint`) are four of the level's six generators; the two older questions come first in the list.
- **Chord–Scale 1**: `inKey` gives chord and key, not the degree; `inKeyRev` asks the key from a chord and its mode. **2**: the care notes are read from the table (IIm7: the 6). **5** (`symUse`): symbols that name their scale (13♭9, 7♯5, 9♯5, 7alt, 7♯11, 7♭9♭13, °7); a scale that holds every note of the symbol is never a wrong choice (`cstHolds`, the dominant's 5th not counted). The items `7♭9` and `7♭13` are no longer asked (their stored records stay). **7**: three choices, each the answer of some item; for V7/II the darker Mixolydian ♭9 ♭13 passes too (`q.also`). **8** (`miScaleQ`): a chord in a key with no label; the five borrowed and substitute chords (60 % of the draws, old keys) among IMaj7, IVMaj7, IIm7, IIIm7, VIm7, V7 and ♭IIIMaj7, so the type alone does not decide.
- **Chord Spelling 4, 8**: choices have the number of notes of the chord shown (triads: the four triad types only; four-note chords: the 6 chords and add9 join). **9**: the multiple-choice generator writes `spc-` keys.
### 2. Texts
Every item under “Errors found” of the four reviews. Among them: the minor cadence in C♯ and G♯ minor (`AN_MIN`, `anKeyFor`); a IIm7 and the dominant a fourth above it spelled a fourth apart by letter (`anSeq`: F♯m7 – B7 in D♭) with a sentence where the chart respells a root (`anChart`); one explanation and hint per question in Harmony & Analysis 3; the numeral hint no longer counts letters to a respelled root; the description of level 7 and of the game; C♭ major pentatonic; whole-half spelled 1 2 ♭3 4 ♭5 ♭6 6 7 (`SYM`); Lydian augmented's tensions 9 and ♯11; altered written 1 ♭9 ♯9 3 ♯11 ♭13 ♭7 everywhere; Chord–Scale chapter 14 points to Modes; D/C with both readings (Chord–Scale 10, Chord Spelling 10); the look-alike pair named on its own root; one speed figure in Chord Spelling 5; the texts of Intervals & Keys 1 and 7.
- **“Maj7”, not “maj7”, in what the student reads** in the four theory games: `symD(root,q)` and `majD(s)` are the display forms; `sym()` and every key built from it are unchanged. `anChordOf().sym` is now the display form, so the ear games that show it read CMaj7 too.
### 3. One chord-scale table: `CSN` and `CST` (core, before `MAJ_MODES`)
- `CSN[name] = {s, d, aka?}`: the eighteen scale names. `CST` = a chord in its function → `sc`, `ok` (also accepted), `ten`, `care`; `ctx` `maj | min | sym | blues`. Helpers `cst(f,ctx)`, `cstTones`, `cstOk`, `cstHolds`, `csSpell(root,name)`. **To change what a chord takes, edit `CST` only.**
- Chord–Scale Reflex's generators, lesson card, level notes and chapters follow it; `CS_CHART` (Reference page, chapter 4) is computed from it; the other names of a scale are said once, in chapter 4 (“One name for each scale”, computed from `aka`). `SYM`'s ids (`half-whole diminished`…) are part of item keys and stay; `csD()` gives the name shown.
- **Improvise** (`SOLO_SC`): the VI7 of the turnaround takes `v-II` (Mixolydian ♭13 ∪ Mixolydian ♭9 ♭13: 9 or ♭9), the Im7 of the minor cadence `m-I` (Aeolian ∪ Dorian). Nothing else in its grading changed; `SOLO_SCN` uses the table's names. **Arpeggio Runner**: `ENC_SCN.aeo` = Aeolian. Modes, Hear the Mode: alias names removed from their texts.
### 4. Double accidentals
- The spell keyboard has two smaller rows, double flat and double sharp; the flat or sharp key pressed twice arms the double (`S.acc` ±2; also in the `letters` answer). `P()` reads 𝄫 and 𝄪. `okDbl(ns)` = at most one double accidental.
- Lifted for the recall of diminished and augmented triads and °7 chords (`RC_DBL`, `recallPick`) and for the mode spellings of Chord–Scale 3 and 4. C°7, F°7, B♭°7, D♭°, E♭°, A♭°, C♯+, F♯+, B+ are asked. **E♭°7, D♭°7, A♭°7 and G♭°7 need two double flats and stay out; D♯°7 and G♯°7 are asked in their place (`RC_MORE`), so every pitch has its °7 chord.** Not lifted for intervals, minor sevenths on G♭ and the like (the brief: no questions built around double accidentals).
### 5. Functional numerals
- `fnForms(ans)`, `fnShown`, `fnNote`, `numFn(q,minor)`, `spCheck`, `spOk`, `spShow` (core, after `progTokens`). `q.answer` stays the degree form (keys, labels, statistics); `q.fn[i]` is the functional form accepted in that slot, `q.fnRes[i]` = it resolves to its target and is the form shown. VI7, VII7, II7, III7 in a major key always have one; I7 only before a IV chord (shown only when that chord is not IV7: a blues I7 stays I7, V7/IV passes); ♭II7 → subV7 only before I. Minor keys: none.
- The row gets the functional token of every such dominant it holds.
