Used by Harmony & Analysis (`anWriteQ`), Hear the Progression (`earDictProgQ`, `earSongQ`, `earSecQ`, the two cadences, `earEightQ`). **Repertoire has no written-numeral question**: its multiple-choice `repNumQ` shows the functional form when the chord resolves, and the degree form passes through `q.also` (it is not a button).
### Decisions the brief did not settle
- **The sweep's two rules on the table have one exception each, because the table itself needs them**: a tension lies a whole step above a chord tone, except ♭9 and ♭13 on a dominant; a care note lies a half step above a chord tone, except the 6 of Dorian on IIm7 (its tritone with the ♭3). Both exceptions are written in the sweep.
- Bebop scales, “natural minor”, “major scale”, pentatonic and blues scales are not in the table and are not held against it; the check is on mode-type names and on the alias names.
- `keysHoldQ` asks m7 chords only (three keys). The reverse question of Chord–Scale 1 leaves out Ionian (its key is the chord's own root).
- The functional form is accepted wherever the chord has one and shown only where it resolves (Take the A Train's II7 is shown II7; V7/V passes).
- A triad's wrong choices in Chord Spelling 4 are the other three triad types (sus chords come four levels later).
### Not done, or not verified
- The lesson cards (`LESSONS`) are in the views half and are not reached by the sweep: their texts were checked by hand.
- The explanations of the multiple-choice numeral questions still answer in the degree form (VI7), with a sentence naming the function.
- Hear the Progression's own chord symbols (`epSyms`) still write maj7 in lower case: outside this build.
- Nothing was tried by the user. The new keyboard rows and the three new question types were run headless and muted (typed C E♭ G♭ B𝄫 passes; three keys in any order; V7/II for VI7).
- Sweep block “THE REVIEW OF THE THEORY GAMES: BUILD 1” at the end of `tests/sweep.js`; two older checks were adapted (a spelled answer may hold one double accidental; the borrowed-chord check reads `CST`).

## The review of the theory games: build 2 (23 Nov 2026, brief `.claude/theory-build-2.md`; working tree only, not committed)
Analysis of real harmony, and scales chosen by function. **Five levels appended** (Harmony & Analysis 17 → 19, Chord–Scale Reflex 10 → 13). No level inserted, removed or renumbered; no existing item key changed; no saved data touched; where an old level got generators they stand after the old ones. Code: the block “THE REVIEW OF THE THEORY GAMES: BUILD 2” (`/*T2_BEGIN*/ … /*T2_END*/`, just before `QUARTAL`); sweep block of the same name at the end of `tests/sweep.js`. **Nothing was tried by the user.** Sweep, `pages_check`, every new or changed level started headless and muted (`tests/ear_start.py analysis:5,7,8,9,10,11,12,14,16,17,18,19 chordscale:3,4,5,6,9,10,11,12,13`), sampled questions and the new chapters read as text.
### Harmony & Analysis
- **The wider material `ANX`** (beside `DEVICES`, which is unchanged): 25 lines typed as numerals, `{id, g, n, at, dev, why}`; every chord is computed from its numeral (`nuP`, `nuSeq`, which keeps a II – V a fourth apart by letter and gives ♭III°7 its flat root). Groups `sd` (V7/II … V7/VI, five lines), `rel` (each with its related II: m7♭5 before a minor target, m7 before a major one), `sub` (subV7 of I, II and V, alone and with its II), `mi` (IVMaj7 – IVm7 – I, ♭VIMaj7 – ♭VII7 – I, IIm7♭5 – V7 – IMaj7), `dim` (♯I°7 and ♯II°7 in one line, ♯II°7, ♯IV°7, ♭III°7), `dec` (V7 to VIm7, to ♭VIMaj7). **To add a line, add an entry; the sweep proves each entry is what its group says.**
- **Written lines** `anxWriteQ(groups)`: keys `anx-w-<id>`. Level 8 `sd`, 9 `rel`, 10 `sub`, 11 `mi` and `dim`. The row (`nuRow`) is the seven chords of the key, the answer, and the numerals of at least two other lines (at least three outside numerals the answer does not use).
- **subV7/II and subV7/V** are functional forms now, beside ♭III7 and ♭VI7: `numFn2` (a wrapper round build 1's `numFn`, which is unchanged), accepted and shown only when the target follows; `fnNote2`, `nuShown`. `TOK_ORDER` gained the new numerals (the order of the older ones is unchanged).
- **9** `anxDualQ` (`anx-dual-<target>-<type>`, seven items): the related II of a secondary dominant, is it also a chord of the key (IIIm7, VIm7, VIIm7♭5, or not). **12** `anxDimDomQ` (`anx-dimdom-<root>`): the dominant a rising diminished chord stands for; the three other dominants that hold the same four notes are never among the choices. **14** `anxModQ` (`anx-mod-<id>`, `ANX_MOD`, eight lines): the pivot is found by rule (`anxMod`: the chord before the first chord the old key does not have, if the new key has it); three of the eight are direct modulations.
- **16 and 17** `anxCapQ(write)` / `anxJoin`: two lines of different groups joined in one key, seven or eight chords (a shared chord at the join is written once; a line that ends on V7 is followed only by one that begins on I; never the same dominant alone and with its II). 16 (`anx-cd-<a>-<b>`): five choices, each naming the two places by their chord symbols and a device for each; a wrong choice changes a device name (`dev`, `not`). 17 (`anx-cw-<a>-<b>`): every numeral written.
- **Minor keys**: level 5 `anxMinNumQ` (`anx-mn-<key>-<d>-<type>`: ♭IIIMaj7, ♭VIMaj7, ♭VII7, IVm7, Vm7, V7, IIm7♭5 in seven minor keys, `AN_MINK`). Level 7, after its three old generators: `anxMinWriteQ` (`anx-wm-<id>`, `ANX_MIN`: the cadence IIm7♭5 – V7(♭9) – Im and four other minor lines), `anxMinFuncQ` (`anx-mf-<numeral>`; `minFunc`: the raised 7th makes a dominant, the ♭6 without it a subdominant minor chord, neither a tonic), `anxMinSrcQ` (`anx-ms-<numeral>`; `minSrc`: which of the three minor scales hold the chord; V7 is answered harmonic minor and melodic minor passes through `q.also`).
- **18 “Analyse eight bars”** `an8Q`: the ten spans of `E8` as chord symbols in a named key (`anBars`, CSS `.anbars`), the numeral of every bar written; key `an8-<form id>-<first bar>`; the form is named only in the explanation.
- **19 “The key of the moment”** `kmQ(false|true)`, material **`KM`** (computed from `FORMS` when the script loads): a m7 or m7♭5 chord, the dominant 7th chord a fourth above it, and the chord after (a II – V played twice is one cadence). The key is a fifth below the dominant; minor when the cadence lands on a minor chord on that root, or, where it does not land, when the II chord is half-diminished. **24 passages qualify** (the brief asked for at least eight): jazz blues in F and B♭ (bars 4, 8), Bird blues (2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8), Autumn Leaves (1, 27, 28), Fly Me to the Moon (6), Satin Doll (3, 5, 6, 17, 21), Blue Bossa (9), Take the A Train (16), All The Things You Are (10, 17, 21). Shown in the key the form is stored in, four to eight bars, the II – V marked. Keys `ank-<form id>-<bar>` (the tonic, a row of twelve) and `ank-<form id>-<bar>-r` (its place: `keyRel`, thirteen relations computed).
- **`q.flu`**: a question may carry its own speed target in seconds (`fluTarget`). Written lines 2 s + 2 s a chord (10 s for four, 18 s for eight bars); the two-device choice 15 s. Everything else keeps the general rule.
- Notes: chapters “More of each device, in a line” (the table of `ANX`, dual function, the dominant behind a diminished chord, which minor scale has a chord: all computed), “Analysing a tune” (two worked eight bars from `an8Rows`, the table of `KM`), “Blues harmony, the line cliché and the pedal point” (pointers to Repertoire, Hear the Progression, Bass & Top by level name).
### Chord–Scale Reflex
- **Levels 3 to 6**: `spellMode`, `spellAlt`, `spellSym`, `bebop` are entered note by note (`csSpellQ`, the root given), same item keys. `q.spAlt` (in `spOk`): another correct spelling of the same notes, accepted slot by slot: the altered scale on seven letters or by its degrees, half-whole with ♭3 or ♯9, whole tone with ♯5 or ♭13. The spelling shown is the one without a double accidental where there is one. Target 1.5 s + 1 s a note entered.
- **9**: the pentatonic on the 4th of Ionian and of Mixolydian is no longer the answer (`PENT_CARE`), as the chapter says; the items `cs-pent-Ionian-5` and `cs-pent-Mixolydian-5` are not asked any more (their records stay). **10**: `upperQ` no longer prints the numeral of the triad; `upperScaleQ` (`cs-usc-<numeral>`, `uscHold`): the dominant scale that holds the triad with the chord's 3rd and ♭7 (for the ♭V triad altered, half-whole accepted).
- **11 “Available tensions”** `tenQ(false|true)`: the thirteen rows of `CST` that have tensions (the chords of the key, V7/II … V7/VI, subV7), given as a chord and a key. Chosen from the row of seven sets (`cs-ten-<function>`), then entered as notes (`cs-tenn-<function>`, target 4 s + 1.5 s a note). For V7/II, ♭9 and ♭13 pass as well.
- **12 “The scales of a progression”** `csProgLineQ` (`cs-pr-<line id>-<chord>`): a line of `ANX` with a chord from outside the key, one chord marked (six times in ten the outside one); `nuCst` finds the table's row from the numeral. **13 “The scales of eight bars”** `cs8Q` (`cs8-<form id>-<first bar>-<bar>`, `CS8`, `e8Cst`): the spans of `E8`, one bar marked. Both answer from a fixed row of thirteen names (`CS_ROW`); what `CST` accepts passes.
- Notes: chapter “From the chord to its function to its scale” (two worked eight bars from `cs8Rows`, tensions by function, the scale behind each upper structure: computed).
### Goals
`GOALS.B` appendix `analysis 9–19`; `GOALS.A` appendix `chordscale 3–13`. No new level is required by a goal.
### Decisions the brief did not settle
- **Levels 16 and 17 keep their old generators** (the sweep of build 1 reads them, and the old items stay asked); the two-device lines are four of seven generators in 16 and three of four in 17. The joined lines have seven or eight chords (every line of the list but one has four).
- **The deceptive endings are asked only inside the two-device lines**: the brief gives them no level of their own.
- Level 19 is two generators with two keys (`…` and `…-r`); the first asks the tonic only, and the explanation says major or minor.
- **A chord the table has no row for is never the marked chord** (levels 12 and 13): a related IIm7 from outside the key, and in minor keys everything but Im7, IIm7♭5 and V7. `CST` was not given new rows. In Autumn Leaves' first eight (G minor) only three bars can be asked.
- subV7/II and subV7/V read the table's `subV7` row (Lydian ♭7); a diminished chord its `°7` row (whole-half).
- The eight bars of levels 18 and 13 are shown in a drawn key, as Hear the Progression plays them; the passages of level 19 in the key the form is stored in.
- `tests/sweep.js`: one check of build 1 adapted (a mode's answer is now a list of notes).
### Not done, or not verified
- Nothing in the list was left out. The lesson cards (`LESSONS`) are not reached by the sweep: their two new lines each were checked by hand.
- The level-16 choices are long lines of text (two places, two names); looked at once as a rendered page, not tuned.
- The speed targets carried by `q.flu` are estimates, not measured on the student.

## The review of the theory games: build 3 (23 Nov 2026, brief `.claude/theory-build-3.md`; working tree only, not committed)
Chord Spelling and Intervals & Keys: the other half of their notes. **Four levels appended** (Chord Spelling 10 → 12, Intervals & Keys 7 → 9). No level inserted, removed or renumbered; no existing item key changed; no saved data touched; where an old level got generators they stand after the old ones. Code: the block “THE REVIEW OF THE THEORY GAMES: BUILD 3” (`/*T3_BEGIN*/ … /*T3_END*/`, right after the build-2 block); sweep block of the same name at the end of `tests/sweep.js` (it recomputes every answer from letters and half steps, without `chord()` or `scale()`). **Nothing was tried by the user.** Sweep `bad 0` twice, `pages_check` `all ok`, started headless and muted (`tests/ear_start.py spelling:4,8,10,11,12 intervals:1,7,8,9 chordscale:12,13 analysis:19`), ten samples of every new generator read as text, the new chapters read as text.
### Chord Spelling (12 levels)
- **`CH` gained eight entries, none changed**: `7sus4`, `7♯5`, `7♭5`, `7♯11`, `7♭13`, `13(no 11)` (1 3 5 ♭7 9 13), `maj7♯11`, `6/9`. Their names are in **`QNAME_X`, not `QNAME`** (`qnX`): the naming questions of levels 4 and 8 draw wrong choices from `QNAME` and must not change. `formulaQ` now draws its wrong choices from `Object.keys(QNAME)` (the same 23 as before).
- **4 and 8**: `nameRotQ(qs, roots)` after the old generator: the notes shown from the lowest up, rotated (an inversion), keys `nmr-<symbol>`. Two choices on the true root and two on the note in the bass; a chord with the same notes as the answer (X6 for a m7 chord, the other names of an augmented triad or a °7 chord) is never a choice, and the explanation names it.
- **10**: `symReadQ` and `lookAlikeQ` are rebuilt in the build-3 block (`SYM_PAT`, `LOOK_PAT`): every pattern on any root, notes computed, **item keys and labels as they were** (`sym-C△7`, `la-C6`: the pattern as written on C). `slashSpellQ` (`sls-M3 | m3 | M5 | m5 | Mb7 | M2`: a major or minor triad with its 3rd or 5th in the bass; a major triad over its own ♭7, D/C, and over the note a whole step above its root, B♭/C): the bass first, then the other notes **in rising order above it**. `slashNameQ` (`slw-up2 | dn2 | up5`: D/C, B♭/C, G/C): a bass note and a triad it does not belong to, named from five choices; both readings in the explanation.
- **11 “Dominant colours and the big chords”** `colourQ(COL_DOM | COL_BIG, roots)`: recall, the root given, keys `sp-<root><quality>` (`sp-C7sus4`, `sp-Cmaj7♯11`, `sp-C6/9`, `sp-Cm11`; **the 13 chord is `sp-C13(no 11)`**, because `sp-C13` is the seven-note 13 of level 9). One double accidental where the spelling needs it (`okDbl`); m11 on the roots minor chords are written on (`MINOR_ROOTS`). The explanation (`colWhy`) starts from the seventh chord and ends with what a guitarist leaves out (`COL_OUT`). Target 1.5 s + 1 s a note entered.
- **12 “The 3rd and the 7th”** `gtPaperQ` (`sg-<symbol>`, two notes, nothing given, 3.5 s; six qualities; minor qualities on `MINOR_ROOTS`, °7 on every pitch through `RC_MORE`) and `gtCadQ` (`sgc-<key>`, the six notes of IIm7 – V7 – IMaj7 in the twelve keys of `CYCLE4`, 7.5 s; `gtCadOf(k)`).
- Notes: chapter 7 and 11 say what the levels ask now; two chapters **appended at the end** (so stored highlights keep their chapter numbers): “The colours of the dominant, and three big chords” (computed table on C, E♭ and A; ♯5 against ♭13, ♭5 against ♯11; what is left out) and “The 3rd and the 7th” (computed tables; pointer to Guide-Tone Voice Leading).
### Intervals & Keys (9 levels)
- **1**: `spellMaj` spells all fifteen major scales (`ALL_MAJ`; keys `spm-<key>` as before, five new ones).
- **7**: `ivInvNotesQ` after the two name questions (`ivn-<interval>`, 18 items: the nine pairs of `INV_PAIRS` both ways, A2/d7, A4/d5, A5/d4, A6/d3 among them; `IVX` = `IV` plus A6 and d3, `IV` itself is unchanged).
- **8 “Below a note, and beyond the octave”**: `ivDownQ` (`ivd-<interval>-<note>`, twelve intervals, target 4 s) and `ivTenQ` (`ivt-<tension>-<root>`, the prompt names the tension as an octave plus its simple interval, target 3.5 s). One note entered; a double accidental where the spelling needs it.
- **9 “The minor scales, every key”**: `minScaleQ` (`ms-<kind>-<key>`, 44 scales: three forms in the fifteen keys of `MIN_KEYS`; **A♯ melodic minor needs two double sharps and is not spelled whole**; target 10 s) and `minDegQ` (`msd-<kind>-<key>-<6|7>`, 90 items, 3.5 s). The explanation names what changed from natural minor (`minChanged`).
- Notes: the minor table has all fifteen keys (it said thirteen); a computed table of the tensions as notes; the augmented and diminished inversion pairs; the 6th and 7th of the three scales in every key; each chapter says which level asks it.
### The two additions from the review of build 2
- **`CST`: the chords of the natural minor key** (`ctx:'min'`): ♭IIIMaj7 Ionian (9, 13; care 4), IVm7 Dorian (9, 11, 13; none), Vm7 Phrygian (11; care ♭2, ♭6), ♭VIMaj7 Lydian (9, ♯11, 13; none), ♭VII7 Mixolydian (9, 13; care 4). The three older minor rows got their tensions and care notes too (Im7: 9, 11, care ♭6; IIm7♭5: 11, ♭13, care ♭2; V7: ♭9, ♭13, care 4). The sweep derives the five from the rule (a non-chord scale note a half step above a chord tone is a care note, else a whole step above one is a tension). Chord–Scale 13 can mark every bar of a minor span (`CS8` has 52 items; all 32 chords of the minor spans have a row); **Chord–Scale 12 got a fourth generator `csProgMinQ`** (`cs-pr-min-<line id>-<chord>`, the five lines of `ANX_MIN`, any chord marked; `nuCstMin`). “Available tensions” (level 11) still asks the thirteen major-key rows (`TEN_F`). Chord–Scale's last chapter has a computed table “In a minor key”.
- **Harmony & Analysis 19, a chain of II – V cadences**: `KM` entries carry `chain`, `nx`, `nk`, `nh` (the cadence lands on a m7 or m7♭5 chord that is the II of the next II – V; five of the 24 passages, Gm7 – C7 – Fm7 – B♭7 of Autumn Leaves among them). There `kmWhy` says the II – V points to the tonic and that the chord it lands on at once becomes the II of the next key (or of the tune's own key); it no longer says “the key of the moment is F minor”. Answers and keys unchanged.
### Goals
`GOALS.A`: Intervals & Keys 1–3 in stage 1 (was 1), 4–5 in stage 2, with `GOAL_WHY` `intervals:1` rewritten and `intervals:4` new. `GOALS.D`: `spelling 11–12` in stage 4 (`spelling:11`). Appendices: A and C `spelling 8–12`, R `intervals 3–9`. 354 levels are required by some goal.
### Decisions the brief did not settle
- The 13 chord of level 11 has its own table entry and item key (above); it is shown as “C13”.
- Slash chords are answered in one fixed order (bass, then rising); their item keys are per pattern, like the rest of level 10. The “which chord is this” question uses three patterns, all with a bass outside the triad (the only case with two readings).
- The II–V–I of level 12 is in major keys only (the brief's keys are `sgc-<key>`); the minor cadence, whose 7th falls a whole step into Im7, is not asked.
- Level 8 and the single degrees of level 9 allow one double accidental (a major 3rd below G♭ is E𝄫; the 7th of A♯ melodic minor is G𝄪). Build 1 had kept doubles out of the older interval levels; those are unchanged.
- Level 1 spells C major too (fifteen scales).
- Speed targets by `q.flu`: 4 s below a note, 3.5 s a tension or a minor degree, 10 s a minor scale. The rest follow the general rule. Estimates, not measured.
- Chord–Scale 12 takes one draw in four from the minor lines; the older minor rows of `CST` got `ten` and `care` so the notes' table has no empty cells.
- `tests/sweep.js`: three older checks adapted (the reverse question may be an inversion; the minor table has fifteen keys; the minor spans of Chord–Scale 13 read the new rows).
### Not done, or not verified
- Nothing in the list was left out. The lesson cards (`LESSONS`) are not reached by the sweep: their three new lines each were checked by hand.
- Intervals & Keys 6–9 are in no goal of A (the brief names the appendices that already held the game's upper levels: only R's).
- `-r` questions of level 19 on a chain still say in the prompt that the II – V “points to F minor” (the answer, the relation, counts it as a minor key); only the explanation was reworded.
- A slash chord over a bass outside the triad is asked on major triads only.
